# Continuity State

Canon established in the bible, the series and volume outlines, and now in Chapter prose through Chapter 10.

## Core canon

- Setting: Nacre, a modern coastal city-republic. The formal public network is the Civic Weave; the common name is the Soul Network.
- Protagonist: Marek Senn, 22, third-year at the Nacre Institute of Applied Practice, night repairer at Civic Spine 7. He wants a license, a wage, and to stop being the family's problem.
- Marek's flaw is taking responsibility before being asked and hiding its cost. His need is to accept chosen interdependence without turning it into ownership.
- Primary romance: Talia Venn, resonance nurse and consent advocate. They are not partners at the opening.
- Family: Lena Senn is Marek's mother (nights at a long-stay clinic on Kestrel Row); Nell Senn is his younger sister, 16; Evan Senn is his missing father.
- Recurring allies: Rafi Pell (roommate, courier), Sera Quill (Spine 7 lead repairer), Asha Reed (Movement IV onward), Dessa Kwan (from Chapter 10).
- Central antagonist: Dr. Iona Sorn, the Conductor. Oren Vey, Iven Sore and Leo Marr represent commercial, institutional and privacy pressures before the Crown Clause's final use.

## Power rules

- A share carries observations and technique structure, not private thoughts, innate talent, or full memories.
- Consent is scoped and can be withdrawn. Use, Remember and Derive are separate permissions. Silence, proximity and a pre-signed workplace form are not agreement.
- Connections produce aftertaste and attention debt. Overload can cause physical symptoms, emotional bleed, and lasting unmooring.
- A hinge is not merely a missing boundary label. It deletes the boundary-release beat that should apply the limit and complete the practice, then installs a key-holder's cue as the learner's expected replacement; it narrows practical independence but is not universal mind control.
- The opening brace is distributed by Threadline from an unnamed upstream technique bundle. Threadline is the contracted assembler and seller, not the author of the Crown Clause.
- Marek begins at Stage 0, Latent Counterpoint. His eventual stages end with Open Weave and the final Unbinding.

## Volume 01 outline canon

Set by `outline/volume-01.md` and `outline/batches/volume-01-batch-0001.md`.

- Volume 01 is titled *The Missing Beat* and runs Chapters 1–50 in five movements: The Drill (1–10), The Clinic and the Quarantine (11–20), Saltmarket (21–30), The Lower Wards and the Second Dependence (31–40), The Witnessed Drill (41–50).
- **Timeline anchor:** Chapter 1 opens on Tuesday of week 1. The original Solo Seal run is the Thursday of week 4 in Chapter 5. The replacement witnessed assessment is offered for **Thursday of week 7, 09:00, examination hall, board convened**. Chapter 10 is Thursday night of week 6, so the date is seven days away. Movement II runs weeks 7–9 and **withdraws the date in Chapter 12 before it can arrive**; the final assessment is set five days after Chapter 40 for the Chapters 44–48 climax.
- **Verified in-world timeline of Chapters 1–10.** Use this before assigning any new date. Weeks run Monday to Sunday; Tuesday to Thursday is two days forward, Thursday to Tuesday is five days forward.
  - **Tue wk 1, 01:40–05:00** — Ch 1, Spine 7 night shift. Mon wk 1 evening: courier job, shoulder injured. Tue wk 1 morning: practical review, roster, the discovery.
  - **Thu wk 1, 09:20–13:00** — Ch 2–4, the drill, the fall, the four-second line, the incident sheet, then housing and the Spine that night (load book opened; Marrow Falls page copied at about 23:00). **All of Chapters 2, 3 and 4 happen on this one Thursday.**
  - **Weeks 1–4** — Ch 5, the three ordinary weeks, then the Solo Seal run on Thu wk 4 at 09:00. Darla Vint hands over the folder the same afternoon. Second courier job: Wed wk 2.
  - **Tue wk 5, 18:10–22:00** — Ch 6, the clinic, then Tam's room on Sennet Row — **the doorway conversation and "come to the front of the room like a person" happen on this Tuesday** — then back at the clinic. Talia's scope is offered.
  - **Thu wk 5, 21:30 – 01:05 Fri wk 5** — Ch 7, the clinic bay, then the Institute kit store at 23:40. **The bundle label, the crate count, the instructor booklet and the empty licensor field are read on the Thursday night of week 5, not on a Tuesday.** The work-setting notice arrives; the dock is Tue wk 6, five days out.
  - **Tue wk 6, 18:15–21:30** — Ch 8, the west berth. The Institute's office calls at 21:30. **The five days Ch 8 counts back from this dock are Fri wk 5 to Tue wk 6, and they contain no other scene.** The clinic visit and the Sennet Row doorway both happened on the Tuesday of the week before (Ch 6) and are outside that window — do not put either of them inside it.
  - **Wed wk 6** — Ch 9, department office, corridor, the Continuity Office form, the kit store, the copy room, the west berth. **Eight days to the week-7 assessment**; the clinic-bay rule was written six days earlier.
  - **Thu wk 6, 18:10–23:40** — Ch 10, the Exchange, the ramp, the ward. **Seven days to the assessment.**
- The 4-19 stock is present at four sites: the Institute, two satellite sites, and Talia's clinic on the Saltmarket route. By Chapter 14, Movement II places a limited local quarantine on all four. **The Continuity Office can review and recommend lifting it, but each site retains local authority. In Chapter 40 the Office completes its review and records a recommendation that is *conditional*: it recommends the holds be lifted if and only if a working non-hinged release is demonstrated at the assessment it is setting five days out. There is no demonstration in Chapter 40 and no automatic release. The outline's own wording is *recommend lifting it if a working non-hinged release is demonstrated at the assessment*.**
- The scored release cannot use, follow or accept Rusk's substituted cue inside the scored window. Rusk may remain in the room and may speak before that window, but anyone who follows his cue disengages before the board calls the release.
- Marek **does not receive the Solo Seal**. The seal line stays as written: *not completed. Fourth beat absent. Performance dependent on external cue.* The **provisional Working Share** is granted in Chapter 49, never earlier.
- Volume-end power state is **Stage 1, First Knot**, with painful shared aftertaste and one clear failure: the knot collapses under load during the witnessed drill and a person is hurt. He cannot invent a practice; the climax's counterpoint sequence is a knot between two honest practices, not a technique of his own.
- Tam Arrick's forearm injury, his lost kitchen wages, and his slow recovery are permanent costs and are never reset.
- The Volume 01 climax requires three genuinely independent lines, since the Movement II attempt at a three-source comparison fails as **false consensus** (all three observers trained by the same person).
- The Chapter 8 release is an accidental non-hinged sample, not a qualifying scored release: Marek voices Rusk's cadence before refusing it, so it does not satisfy the no-cue rule.
- The **Crown Clause is never named in prose** in Volume 01.

## Canon now fixed in Chapter prose (Chapters 1–10)

These are established in finished prose and are binding on later chapters.

### The kit and its paper trail

- **Threadline Practicer Kit, Rescue Brace, rev 4-19.** Assembly lot **TL-4-19-0311**. Assembled by the Threadline Service Depot, Lattice. Service ref **SB-4-19-03**.
- The bundle label's **upstream technique bundle** and **upstream licensor** lines are both ruled and both **empty**, drawn with the same rule on the same day. This must stay unanswered through Chapter 50.
- Service notice **SB-4-19-03** reads: *Return contact, revised for district distribution. Not a fault. Do not claim. Prior practice continues unchanged.* It is the first thread that can be followed upward.
- The 4-19 **instructor notes booklet** is eleven pages. Page 3 sets out the four-beat drill; page 4 carries the emergency header *where a local node does not answer, the last approved civic sequence is used as the provisional response*. It states the fourth beat is a support beat, that the learner cannot perform it alone and is not expected to, that the instructor confirms scope aloud on the beat, that the learner releases on the instructor's confirmation, and that the learner must not proceed on a self-initiated release. **No page in the booklet carries a name, a date, or a set of initials, and Chapter 7 makes no claim about where the substitution came from.** Marek names four live possibilities in Chapter 7 — a bad print run, a card printed against the wrong revision, a supplier assembling a bundle out of somebody else's book, or a person who sat down and chose it — and states plainly that he cannot tell which, because a chosen omission and an accidental one look identical from inside a store room. **The design-approval entry and revision history that make the substitution deliberate do not appear anywhere in Chapters 1–10 and are reserved for the Chapter 25 midpoint proof.** Chapters 1–10 establish only the absence, the script's own words, and the 3-08/4-19 wording difference (*the practitioner confirms aloud* against *the instructor confirms aloud*).
- The Institute's manual is **3-08**. Beat four on 3-08 reads: *The practitioner confirms aloud, at this point, what the brace has been permitted to touch, and returns the next decision to the subject of the brace.* Rusk personally requisitioned the 3-08 reprint on the eleventh of March, four months before 4-19 landed, from an eight-credit teaching budget. The manual is never updated during the volume.
- The drill sequence is four beats: **anchor, load, close, scope**.
- The nine crates were moved from the open kit store into a chained caged store, the **crates intact and the wax seals on them**, on a laminated card wired to the cage reading **TRAINING STOCK — 4-19 — LOCKED PENDING SERVICE. Access: department only. Equipment handling only.** Locked, not quarantined.
- A Threadline field service engineer, **Petar Vance**, services 4-19 at the Institute, both satellite sites, and the Saltmarket clinic. He is not a villain; he is a service engineer protecting warranty exposure.
- **O. Vey, Operations — Threadline** is the authorizing name on the department's purchase docket. Named on paper only; he does not appear in person in Movement I.

### The drill, the record and the seal run

- The week-one drill record was corrected to: *Sequence not completed as scripted. Student intervention at the point of release. Equipment sound. No further action.* Darla Vint's data-protection reasoning is that naming one student would have put a question about third-year capability in front of a board.
- **Anneke Roos**, Deputy Registrar of the practical department, 54, nineteen years in the building, offered Marek a technician apprenticeship on the City maintenance track (forty-one hours, the Spine's own rota), stated that he cannot hold the apprenticeship and run the investigation in the same term, and said plainly that she would describe the kit as a hardware matter if he stayed quiet. She also refused to have the stock reissued, and did not call that a favour. In Chapter 9 she **made him refuse out loud**, made him give a reason that would survive being written down (*I would have taken it if being wrong had cost me nothing*), recorded the refusal with the reason, turned the form so he could see it, and told him not to thank her in the corridor. She is not a villain and is not an ally yet.
- The Institute holds **three hundred and forty students**; **Group C is twelve**, eleven of them in the room at the week-one drill. Rusk taught a hundred and eleven third-years the 4-19 card in September.
- The Solo Seal certifies solo competence and grants no permission to work with partners. The exam office rule: *where a candidate's sequence does not complete, the deficiency is recorded against the candidate, and remains recorded until the candidate demonstrates otherwise to the satisfaction of the department.*
- The seal scoring sheet has a **physical wooden tray marked *incomplete*** on the examiners' table. The scored run requires a candidate to declare the run ended themselves.
- **Talia Venn keeps a copy of every scope she writes.** The Institute's pre-signed facilities form covers premises, equipment and handling of training stock only, and its final clause reads: *This form confers no permission in respect of any individual practice, share, record, or observation.*
- A Continuity Office **Network Practice Incident Notification** offers three categories: (i) equipment; (ii) technique variance attributable to the trainee; (iii) technique variance not attributable to the trainee. **Category (ii) is already ticked in a different ink before Marek signs it.** Hollis Pratt has not appeared.

### The Marrow Falls log

- The page is pinned to the Spine 7 wall of handwritten maintenance notes. It is **Marrow Falls — District Relay Log — Day 2**, fourteen years old — Marek was eight. Row 04:20 reads: *Node 9-Barrow no answer. Carried last approved civic sequence as provisional response. Bridge held. Field crew did not report for second hour; no fault found at node.*
- It is signed *I. Sorn*, with a typed block: *Continuity design office — resident architect, Iona Sorn. Emergency design liaison to Ashfields response.*
- Marek **does not know the name.** **Sera Quill recognizes the page, tells him to give it back and not to ask, and never says the name aloud in this volume.** Her recognition is not yet an identification of authorship. Marek copied the line and the typed block **out of the notebook on the night of Thursday of week 1, at the Spine 7 wall, at about eleven at night**, and did not give her the page back. There is **one** copy in existence and he made it that night; Chapter 9 recopies from that notebook, not from a second reading of the original.
- The same phrase appears in the 4-19 booklet's emergency header, and Marek saw it on Rusk's laminated card in Chapter 2 without registering it.

### Evan's card

- Grey transit sequence card, corners soft, a shallow file scratch across the printed beats. Four printed beats. A fifth line ruled beneath them and left blank, with nothing on the card that explains the blank. It lives in Marek's tool roll and is used as a shim under a plate frame. It is a tool, not a clue, and is never explained in Volume 01.

### People fixed in prose

- **Marek**: 22; counts exits on entering rooms; work gloves under his school jacket; pencil behind one ear; **split thumbnail on the hand he braces frames with**; bad right shoulder from a courier job levering a jammed gate on a service ramp in Nine Bridges, taken on the **Monday evening before the week-one drill**, and no better for a second courier job on the Wednesday of the second week — forty minutes of levering, the wrong arm again, which cost him the Sunday's rent; **it is still bad at the end of Chapter 10 and is never described as re-injured**; lives in a rented room above a laundromat on Pell Street with Rafi; his mother's flat is three streets away on Nine Bridges; had eleven credits in the world in week 1. Keeps a cheap spiral **load book** with a crane on the cover, titled by week, in first person, with a *Do not know* section at the bottom of each entry. This habit begins in Chapter 4 and persists for the volume.
- **Talia Venn**: 24; resonance nurse on the Saltmarket route; keeps a clipboard nobody asked for; has had Tam Arrick's chart since he was sixteen; trained in the same course family as Marek's department; has fitted twenty-six 4-19 braces since September and has an *interference noted* column. She has asked Marek for a Thursday slot twice, out loud, and written to him once; he has refused twice and never replied. **She taught herself the brace the summer she turned nineteen in a county clinic, has been fitting them every working day since, stopped counting at nine thousand, and traces her fourth beat to a woman called Iris Vandermeer** — a name distinct from Odile Fenn's and not a character in the volume.
- **Rafi Pell**: 21; courier; carries tools he does not own; his balance step has no fourth beat and he compensates with his left shoulder against a wall, which he calls technique; he has carried a shoulder mark for two years. He is the first voluntary partner and the first repeat victim.
- **Sera Quill**: 45; Spine 7 lead repairer; writes warnings on the backs of forms; calls a return path a cut in metal with a location, not a promise; has refused to let the Spine's public relay be used as laboratory equipment; recognizes the Marrow Falls page.
- **Davan Rusk**: 47; twenty-two years in the practical department; key-holder by position and belief; ordered the obsolete manual himself; tells Marek to take his evidence to somebody who can be harmed by it. Not a villain. Never becomes one.
- **Tam Arrick**: 20; third-year, Group C; night and prep work at a kitchen on Sennet Row; **flexor tendons cut in the right forearm, two of them, third intact, middle finger unusable. Dressed palm-up in a padded dorsal splint from below the elbow to the knuckles with the fingers free, not in a cast, and taken to theatre at eight the Friday morning; six weeks before he can put weight through it and three months before he can hold anything heavy.** He filled in the drill incident sheet himself, wrote *Senn* in the name box on line four, and wrote *He said it. I let go because he said it. Both are true.* He granted use for the completed rescue only, refused retention and future use, and said never again. He is writing his own recovery plan and has asked for an observer who was never taught the card. He has named **Farrow**. In the ward he tells Marek that he asked the ward sister which name was on the form, so that Marek would be told.
- **Mal Farrow**: 61, former courier, bad hip, works the fish market at Saltmarket. Seen in Chapter 10 as an unnamed porter taking a full crate on his chest, **narrating his own release to himself, out loud, to nobody** — and Chapter 10 now gives the in-frame reason: nobody else is within earshot, the crate has gone down out in the traffic, and there are eleven people between him and the scales, which is why he narrates and why the narration cannot be a share — he names what has touched him (*that's the floor, it's cold, it hurts, I can hear the ice*), then what is off him (*it's on the crate, it's not on your hip anymore*), then asks himself a question and answers it (*get it off yourself or leave it, it's your call, I've got you* — *leave it*) — then getting up unaided with his own two hands and a table leg, lifting the load himself, and going back to work — **with twelve people in the room, including Marek at six feet, and not one of them checking him, and no form anywhere that records what happened to his ribs.** **The attribution is load-bearing: the man performs the release himself and asks nobody for anything. Do not rewrite it with Marek supplying the lines, and do not stack glosses on it — the prose carries one physical reason and one gloss, and the load-book entry is the only place that is allowed to explain it.** Marek's load book marks this line **NOT COMPARED**, because he has no way of finding out whether the man is hurt that is not a question about fourth beats. Tam names him. **Marek has not spoken to him.** He becomes a named clinic patient under a care-link in Movement II.
- **Dessa Kwan**: 27; line cook; runs the kitchen at the Saltmarket Exchange; teaches nobody for money; will not let a practice be performed for an audience; hands off a hot pass by naming out loud what the pan touched and handing the decision back, then leaves the pass. Refuses to have her kitchen practices turned into a standard.
- **Gus Haldane**: courier crew lead, Saltmarket west berth; slipped on wet concrete in Chapter 8; holds the copy of Marek's record in his coat; told Marek about the Exchange.
- **Odile Fenn**: third-year, Group C; writes *scope: force only, no joint, no sensation* on the card; fills in incident sheets; caught Tam's fall on the plate and could not act.
- **Bern Colley**: refuse driver, ~50, clinic patient with a crushed ring finger; the patient in front of whom Talia makes the correction in Chapter 6.
- **Darla Vint**: practical department administrative officer; can alter an access note to name the absence of a share and can countersign a request for that. In Chapter 9 she is the person who rewrites the Threadline engineer's access note as *equipment handling only, no individual practice, no share, no observation*, and who gives Marek the choice of putting his own name on it rather than letting him hide behind *staff present*.
- **Anneke Roos**: Deputy Registrar, practical department, 54, nineteen years.

### Places fixed in prose

- **Civic Spine 7**: repair bench, parts cage, night list on a clipboard on a nail, day book, tray in the cage door, sink with warm water, wall of handwritten notes, relay cabinet. Two exits, east steel and a sticking aluminium door behind the parts cage that must be lifted before it is pulled.
- **Nacre Institute of Applied Practice**: practical building, room two with the stuck roller blind and a taped floor, gymnasium exam hall with two basketball hoops and a painted-over crack in one backboard, kit store behind the porters' cage (brass key on a loop, never asked back), copy room, exam office, department office.
- **Talia's mobile clinic**: a white box on wheels on the Saltmarket route, lay-by outside the wholesale kitchens, diesel auxiliary unit, eight-foot treatment bay, fold-down step, a rule about the floor.
- **Saltmarket Exchange**: a former ferry waiting room, trestle tables, parts bin, a kitchen at the back in an old kiosk, a noticeboard a month out of date.
- **Saltmarket west berth**: wet concrete falling away to a drain channel, hydraulic tail lift, yellow barrier, pallets of iced crates.
- **Marek's room**: the flat above a laundromat on Pell Street, Nine Bridges.

## Mystery and ending constraints

- The original Civic Compact required a visible Return Path and did not permit silence as agreement. Iona inserted the hidden Crown Clause into a working copy of its emergency protocol during the Marrow Falls outage; it treats silence as provisional consent and seeds hinge techniques.
- Evan Senn deliberately severed his own link to interrupt a Crown response; he is not resurrected and leaves no hidden inheritance.
- Iona's final conflict is the Nine-Minute Hush, which begins only in Volume 15. In Volume 13 the coalition exposes the Crown Root Interface as a council-witnessed physical maintenance credential, not a personal power. Marek refuses the Crown Key, distributes the Return Path, and destroys that interface, giving up city-wide inspection and emergency validation while retaining only consent-based access to local networks.
- The ending is the Civic Commons: a voluntary, inspectable public institution with local councils, withdrawal rights, maintenance, and no single owner.
- The Assembly appoints Talia a provisional independent consent ombud in Volume 11 and formally confirms the role after the final crisis. She is not Marek's subordinate or reward; their relationship remains a chosen, bounded partnership.
- No new final enemy, hidden bloodline, secret higher system, or universal consent is permitted.

## System panel usage to date

- Chapter 3: "Two practices agree. One beat is not answered. / Do you offer the next step?" — nothing follows.
- Chapter 7: "Two practices agree. One boundary is not performed." — nothing follows.

**Formatting rule now in force:** a System panel is introduced by a `* * *` separator. A `---` separator is a scene break. Do not introduce panels with `---`.

No other panel exists in Chapters 1–10. The interface has never supplied a solution, and Marek has never once been able to reproduce a line on demand.

## Verified in-world timeline of Chapters 11–20 (Movement II, weeks 6–9)

Use this before assigning any new date. Weeks run Monday to Sunday. Chapter 10 ends on Thursday night of week 6; the batch spans Friday of week 6 to Saturday of week 9.

- **Fri wk 6, 07:10–12:20** — Ch 11 part one. The theatre list, the operation (ARRICK T., 3-19, RND 4, tendon repair R forearm, FDS (mid) / FCR), out of theatre at 11:20, the registrar, the ward, the load-book entry.
- **Mon wk 7, 08:20–21:00** — Ch 11 part two. The two notices on the board; the supplementary invigilation application (Thu 18:00–21:00, 14 credits an evening, **Vint's Monday-of-week-7 estimate was four evenings and about fifty-six credits, and it did not survive the week**); Ninth Street, Ines Sarr's refusal; the bench at Spine 7; the promised visit to Gus Haldane at the west berth and the lot number **TL-4-19-0307**, dated **19 August**, twenty-two plates across four boxes. **The first paid invigilation shift actually worked is the Thursday of week 9.** He did not work the Thursday of week 8, because that was the night he sat at the back of Sarr's class, and the two collide at 18:00–21:00.
- **Tue wk 7, 08:00–17:00** — Ch 12 part one. The withdrawal notice **PD/7/114** on the board; Darla Vint; Deputy Roos's office and the department's four-sentence plan; the term-review item to be tabled on the Friday; the stores line *community and family use* (about ninety households out in September, about forty back, fifty unaccounted).
- **Wed wk 7, all day** — Ch 12 part two. Rusk in the corridor outside room two (he telephoned the faculty office on the Tuesday at half past four, was held eleven minutes, and was told to raise it with his head of department); his unsolicited disclosure that he has seen a set return contact twice, once out of a fish crate in 2019; Lena Senn's flat on Nine Bridges; the load-book entry.
- **Wed wk 8, 07:10–10:30** — Ch 13. The fish office yard, then the market clinic, then the rescue. **The collapse is at four minutes to nine** (the figure 09:15 in older notes of this line was never in the chapter and is gone; the 06:45 at the start of the old range was never in it either and is gone with it). The scope is **closed at 10:05** and the breathing was restored at **09:26**. Those four are the times anything in this case turns on, and the chapter also has the clinic opening at eight and a cold store at five in the morning, which are scenery.

- **Fri wk 8, 11:30** — the clinic's hold. Talia telephones Harbridge at 12:05.

- **Sat wk 8, morning** — the Continuity Office letter arrives at the clinic by hand; Talia forwards it to three addresses at 10:30 with a three-line covering note. Marek sees Roos and the new card at about 11:00 and Halloran in the afternoon.
- **Sun wk 8, morning and afternoon** — Ch 14 part two. Ninth Street, then the clinic. Load-book entry written on the Sunday night.

- **Tue wk 9, 23:00–Wed wk 9, 07:15** — Ch 16. The bad night at Sennet Row at 23:11; Tam walks to Pell Street at 23:45; the Exchange back yard at 06:50 on the Wednesday; the eleven seconds at **07:08**; the failure at 07:04 when the hand stops at the strap.

## Canon now fixed in Chapter prose (Chapters 11–20)

### The withdrawal and the route

- **PD/7/114**, the withdrawal notice, three true reasons: the examination hall is contracted to the faculty for the whole of week 7; the board observer has withdrawn and fourteen days' notice is required for a member resident outside Nacre; a provisional assessment outside the term curriculum calendar needs a faculty resolution, taken in the week after the term review. Reference numbers roll weekly; 114 is a Tuesday.
- **The department's plan, in Roos's four sentences:** he is not readmitted to any assessment route before the term review; the transcript line stands and is reprinted in March; the scholarship review opens at the end of term in the ordinary way; the file is closed to the department as a hardware matter and a vendor enquiry will take six to nine months and produce a new lot number and nothing countable. Her phrase for it: *you fail quietly and go — there is no event.*
- **The term practice-variance panel** is not a board, has four members, and **sits three times in a term on a Thursday**. It is never called the *end-of-term* panel in prose, because it is not one. Roos tabled the 4-19 variance on the **Friday of week 7**, three days after the hall went, having needed to table it by Friday or the term closed without it. **Rusk is on the panel**, did not put himself there, and cannot get off it. Marek is named in a four-page file and may attend and may bring somebody. **The fourth member is a man of thirty-eight from the trust and he is new to the panel since the second sitting**; the other three — Onyeka, the district woman of fifty, and Rusk — were there for the first morning.
- Roos booked the week-7 hall herself on **2 September**; the faculty calendar shaded it in **October** in a different hand. Rusk asked his head of department by telephone and was refused.
- The **variance is dated the fifteenth of week 6** (the dock) and was tabled on the Friday of week 7, four days later. Roos did this on purpose and says so in Chapter 19.

### The four local holds

- Each is a **limited local hold**: nothing leaves, nothing is opened, the chain or the shelf stays, reversible in a minute by a person with a key and a reason in writing, **no finding of variance**, and a named responsible person. Each site acts on its own authority. Each may refuse the Office's recommendation later.
- **The card at the Institute was drafted from Marek's own words, in the corridor, in the middle of the term, by Anneke Roos on the Wednesday of week 8, with one word changed: his "the department is not going to name one" was replaced with her own name.** This is canon and must not be undone.
- **Continuity Office letter**, one page, arriving at the clinic by hand on the Saturday morning of week 8: does not object; cannot recommend or indemnify; any further hold attracts the same advice and the same absence of indemnity; preliminary review scheduled. Talia rang Harbridge at 12:05 on the Friday and forwarded the letter to three addresses at 10:30 on the Saturday with a three-line covering note: *You will have received this from us. We are not telling you what to do with it. T. Venn.*
- **The four sites:** the Institute (Anneke Roos); **the Cordage, Saltmarket** (Bo Halloran, 51, dead right hand in an eleven-year sling, twenty-nine years on the water, 61 students across a week of whom 4 are Institute-trained, 15 plates into a bucket on a high shelf, no document, and no invoice); **the Ninth Street rooms, Nine Bridges** (Ines Sarr); and **Talia's clinic** (26 wrist units and 8 hip variants off the shelf and off six people, one of whom asked for it back and was refused).

### Mal Farrow, the plate, and the scope

- **The copper return contact sets.** When a load goes through a 4-19 plate and no release is performed, the return contact folds and sets hard in the shape of the load path, and it stays like that, and it does it again next time. Rusk has seen it twice. It is the physical signature of a missing boundary-release beat and it is now the strongest fact Marek has.
- **The mechanism of the 9 October-style collapse:** a man in a 4-19 hip variant cannot get a full breath, because the brace holds the practice open with no exit. The tag and the review are a **visible-badged emergency** on a piece of card: one tag, one time, one review, a bit of tape, and a plate that cannot be tracked. It is the *only* document in the case that belongs to a patient, and it is the only fact nobody can argue with.
- **Farrow's history, given once, to Marek, on request, in week 9:** ribs in March 2011, two broken and one cracked, **on the ice at the fish market**; the doctor at the time said he'd be lucky to see fifty and he saw fifty, **with eleven years on that**, which is how a man of sixty-one gets to sixty-one; the two-and-a-half-minute episodes go back to when he was fifty and the doctor he saw was in **2015**; twenty cigarettes a day when he is on, none when he is off; the inhaler in a cupboard, used twice a year. **Nobody had ever asked him.** There is still no form anywhere in the city that records what happened to his ribs. **Do not import "out of a gate at work" — that is the unnamed man of seventy-two in Lena Senn's ward, in for a hip, and the two must never be conflated.**
- **The care-link, read aloud in a vehicle in August and read aloud again in week 8:** reason (sudden breathlessness or loss of consciousness in a patient with a chest or hip presentation on a mobile route with no ambulance within nine minutes); permits physical airway support, bag ventilation, and one hand's contact at the load or strap, nothing else; duration until the patient is breathing, or an ambulance takes over, or ninety minutes, **on the clock and not on judgement**; permits **no share, no observation of the patient's practice, no recording of anything the patient said about how he works, and under no circumstances a technique**; the patient may stop it by word, including the wrong word; review is the next time he is in the chair, out loud, whatever he says. Farrow objects to the ninety minutes and says so.
- **Marek's scope, written by Talia in a vehicle on the Wednesday of week 8, read back to him, read back to Farrow in Farrow's own words, signed by both with Marek's name on the other side of the line:** purpose (the bag), duration (until Farrow can get a full breath on his own, or she says stop, whichever is first), what stays (nothing, no echo, no trace, no use afterwards, not carried into a room with a nameplate on the door), who may stop it (Talia, Farrow if he can speak, Marek if he puts the bag down), and the person it is for. **The share closed at 10:05 on the page, signed by both, with a review date.** What remains is a headache and the smell of a cold store at five in the morning.
- **Farrow's consent, exactly as he gives it:** he will be watched; if he does a release he will do it the way he does it and Marek can write down what he saw; when Arrick is ready, Arrick comes, and Marek can be in the room or in the country; and he is not to be written about as having done a release without knowing he was doing one, because that is not what he does.
- **The scene at the back door of the Exchange, 07:04–07:15 on the Wednesday of week 9.** Tam did a written, single-use consent on a delivery docket (the top half retained in his pocket, not copied, not photographed) for a named purpose, with no hands, and with *if I do not work, we do not do it again for four days*. He broke his own column two and wrote it at the top of the page before he started.
- **The partial success, at 07:08:** the right hand came out of the sling, palm flat, spread, on the top edge of a nine-kilo box, for **eleven seconds**, and the load stayed in, and he put the box down with his left hand and stepped back, and nobody said anything. It is not a sentence and it is not a name and it is not a technique. **Dessa Kwan said, out loud, in the room, "That wasn't a release. That was a man being careful."** Tam said write it down as both. **The fourth time, at 07:04, the hand got as far as the strap and stopped** — the strap is the department's, comes off in about four weeks, and is a good strap that comes off with two fingers. **The one condition under which it works is the condition under which it cannot be checked or witnessed.** D. Kwan is not the observer and will not be.
- **Tam's plan, in his own hand, on the back of a kitchen supply order form, four columns, dates in the margins.** Column one: what the hand can do. Column two: what the hand is not going to be told to do. Column three: what he is allowed to be bad at. Column four: who watches and what they are allowed to say. The first condition under column four is *somebody who was never taught the card* and the second is *and I want to know they said no*. **The middle finger has not moved in six weeks, and item four in column three is the word *finger*, pencil, gone over twice in biro.**
- **Tam's five conditions on Marek reading the plan, given Thursday of week 9:** all of it or none of it, including the finger; tell me the day before, not the week, and not on the way to the building; if somebody wants to know what he thinks, he comes and says it in his own voice; and **you do not get to arrange anybody else.**
- **The bad night, Tuesday of week 9 at 23:11.** Tam can do both voices perfectly, on his own, in an empty room, in a queue, and gets *ready* for it now, and the second one (Marek's) goes deeper than the first. He wrote: *This is not a fourth beat. This is the opposite of a fourth beat.* He then walked eleven minutes to Pell Street to give Marek a copy, on purpose, so that Marek would be the only person in the city who knew. **Marek has written it in the load book as not-for-circulation and knows he will not be able to keep that.**
- **Tam's terms for an observer, final as of week 9:** a witness who is allowed to say *no, that wasn't a release*, and **three people who do not agree.** Refused so far: Rusk (November), the department physio (not allowed to make him fail), O. F. (on the same course, has never been allowed to stop a release in her life), W. Oyelaran of St Anselm's boxing, D. Kwan (*I don't do it in a kitchen*), and Mal Farrow, who said not yet — *when he asks me, and not before* — and whose door is therefore open and not shut.

### The false consensus and the scope that holds

- **The Chapter 17 scope**, written by Ines Sarr on the back of a class timetable before anybody went in, on Marek's request: purpose, four named people, *nobody is being asked to reproduce anything they were taught, nobody is being asked to be right, nobody is being asked to be kind*, any one of the four may end it by word without giving a reason and it is not recorded as a failure, nothing stays, timings and hands and voice only, **the four names do not leave the sheet, and the sheet is destroyed in front of the four of them on the twenty-eighth**. **The twenty-eighth is a calendar date and it is the same Wednesday as Vance's depot deadline — five days after the Friday of week 9, so it is the Wednesday of week 10. It is not "the twenty-eighth of week 9"; the week-9 reading was wrong and has been corrected.** Halloran is paid four pounds out of Sarr's petty cash, on the grounds that a man who has given his working life to a boy's theory wants a receipt.
- **The four:** Ines Sarr (a spoken instructor's cue, *Scope. Off.*, in nine seconds); Bo Halloran (a coaching sentence with **no fourth beat at all** — it ends when he stops talking, and there is nobody in a sack of sand); Odile Fenn (palm flat on the copper, reading where the load is going before deciding — the best thing anybody does in the room); Rafi Pell (the balance step, ending at a wall, done in the air because there is no wall in the room). A volunteer named **Conor**, nineteen, from Halloran's Tuesday class, is in the strap.
- **The panel, and it must not be concluded by anyone:** *Three sources agree. Nothing is missing.* Nothing follows it. The chapter states that the network is not wrong and that the only person performing in the room was Marek.
- **What actually happened:** all three stopped at the same place and looked at Marek, and Rafi saw it and named it — *that's a room, that's what a room's for* — and withdrew, correctly, without giving a reason, because the paper said he didn't have to. **Rafi's diagnosis of his own wall is now canon: the wall is a place where somebody has to be, and it took his body about a year to learn that once somebody pointed at it.**
- **The consent failure of Chapter 17 is Marek's own and it is stated on the page:** the paper is four names and four bodies and a woman with a clipboard who can stop it, and he was a fifth thing in the room holding the paper, and he did not tell her. He writes it under his own name.
- **O. F. removed herself from the count** — she has been counting since week 1 and says she is not a release, an observation or a source — and Marek refuses to un-report her, writes her name down with the breach under his own, and loses her. **She is not a source and never was, and the three he had were counted before she said anything.**

### Talia's scope, the ledger, and the demonstration requirement

- **The Chapter 7 scope closes on the Friday of week 9**, in the ordinary way, with a line through it and a date. Talia refuses to extend it and gives the reason: *a scope is not a relationship, and it is not a thing that gets extended because a person needs it to go on being true.* **The clinic chart is Mal Farrow's and he has said no; the chart is a record of what was done to a man and the man decides.** The care-link review is the next time he is in the chair, out loud, whatever he says.
- **Talia's own confession, in her own hand, in her own chart:** six entries in an *interference noted* column since September, one of them a hip belt she altered herself which did not let go when a man went down, and she wrote *variable release under load* instead of the truth. It was careful and it was a lie.
- **The honest ledger** (Thursday of week 9, **00:20 to 02:30, four headings, fifteen numbered lines**): four things known of which two are a document; one thing wanted; six things that are not evidence and have been treated as if they were; four things he does not know and will not write down as if he did. (The 01:30 in an earlier version of this line was in no chapter; Chapter 18 has him on the end of the bench **at twenty to one in the morning** and finishing **at half two**, which is the night the 4-19 letter goes out before the first bus.) **The four explanations for the omission are all still alive and he says he will not collapse them into the one he wants.**
- **The letter to the Continuity Office**, posted on the Friday morning of week 9 before the first bus, four false starts, the shortest thing he has ever written: he is not withdrawing the notification, is not asking for a fault, and is asking for a preliminary review of the assembly record and a view on whether the Office can identify the body that licensed the upstream bundle at section 4 of the label. *If the answer is that you cannot, then please say so in writing, because I am going to have to take it to a panel in a fortnight and I would like to be able to say that I asked.*
- **Hollis Pratt, Continuity Office field assessor, 51, nineteen years in the job.** Procedural, fair, and frightened of the precedent, not of the licensor. **He counted the blank fields himself, on a Sunday, with a pencil: 4,106 registered technique bundles in the district, 940 with a blank upstream licensor field, 211 of those revised at least once since registration.** (**Chapter 19, and the 211 is in the prose in words — *two hundred and eleven* — not in digits. A search of the manuscript for the figure finds nothing and that is not evidence that it is not there. It is there. Do not remove it.**) He cannot trace one blank without tracing all of them, and he has four assessors. He read the Threadline holding reply aloud from a telex copy — the pack arrived with section 4 empty and there is nothing to conceal — and he recorded the blank as *a fact about a document rather than a conclusion about a person*. He will not open a file on a hurt person who has not asked him to, and he notes that Darla Vint **did not speak for fifty minutes** and that this is going in two files in two different ways.
- **The demonstration requirement, in his words, on Continuity Office letterhead with a reference number:** *a term panel is able to act upon a demonstrated non-hinged boundary release, performed in a public room by a named practitioner, with witnesses, and with no substituted cue used, followed or accepted within the scored window; and no other matter within this office's knowledge is capable of being placed before that panel to the same effect.* **It is a requirement, not a permission, and there is no date.**
- **Petar Vance (Threadline field service) has asked twice whether the wax paper was on the crate and wrote down that Marek said he did not remember, twice.** Marek now remembers: it was on, he replaced the wax and pressed it with his thumb. He has promised to go to the depot, in a room, with a witness, before the **Wednesday of week 10**, and Vance will write *volunteered*. **An envelope addressed to Marek at the Threadline depot counter, left on the Tuesday of week 9 by an unknown person, arrives at the Spine on the Saturday of week 9 and is still unopened.**
- **Rusk's unsolicited disclosure:** he has seen a set return contact twice, once out of a fish crate in **2019**, and there is a department stores file about it that he has never read.

### The turn

- **Chapter 20's recognition, arrived on a Saturday at a bench at 17:40 with a marker cap in his teeth:** *I have been doing a job that is not mine. I have been the person in every room who decides whether the room is told anything, and who is looked at at the fourth place, and whom everybody believes is the one who knows.* **The reason is not helpfulness and not courage. It is that he cannot bear to be in a room where nobody has said what is happening — so he goes and makes sure a question gets answered, and a question answered by him is a question taken off the table. "One frightened man, walking round a city, being competent at things, so that no room is ever left with an open question in it. And there is not one person in this city who has ever printed it on a label, because nobody has ever had to buy it."**
- **His four decisions, written the same evening:** stop arranging; do not chase; ask one person one thing at a time and take the answer; **stop believing that being right is the same as being believed.**
- **Rafi Pell's correction, and it is the chapter's real content:** Marek never announced that a room was happening, so Rafi could not know there was a moment to say no. He is going to count to two out loud, visibly, before he answers anything, for the rest of his life, and to be embarrassed about it, and he is staying. *"You'll not get me back with it. I said I'd stay and I am staying. I just got tired of being a yes."* His wall, he says, is *a place where somebody has to be*.
- **Sera Quill, in person, one time, and she explains nothing:** she recognises the Marrow Falls page and will never discuss it, and *if that is a pattern, and I am not saying it is, then it is not a school matter, and a school matter is the only kind I have ever been able to do anything about.* She refuses to let bringing her a page become a transaction, and sends him to the Saturday backlog instead.
- **The Choir of Practice is named, once, by Talia, on the telephone, as information and not help:** a co-operative that publishes lineages in plain language, the only complete record in the city of who wrote a technique, when, and what they changed; a copy of every quarterly lies on the table at the Saltmarket Exchange for anybody off the street; they are not an authority, they will not do anything for him, and one of them will tell him to his face that he is wrong in front of nine other people. **This is a Movement III pointer and nothing more. The Choir has not appeared on stage and no one in the case has met a member.**

### New people in prose, Chapters 11–20

- **Ines Sarr**, 45, nineteen years running the Ninth Street rooms above a community hall in Nine Bridges. Trained by Rusk in her second year. Shuts the door on Marek in week 7 and has thought about it every day since — **thirteen days later, on the Sunday morning of week 8, she says so herself, and that interval is canon.** Held a Friday-night meeting; **eleven came, nine of them wanted it stopped and two wanted a date for a replacement**, and she told both groups the answer had to wait on the department. Has said *what has the plate actually done* in that room since before Marek was at the Institute. **Worked out on the Friday lunchtime of week 8, alone, over a cup of tea, with a cardstock cut and waiting for the bell, that she has been a key-holder in her own corridor for four months.** Has told Marek she will take him out of her classroom by the arm if he takes one note — the one time she did it, the man was forty-eight and a police sergeant. **There is no member of her room who has said he will not pay for a stoppage; that detail was never written and must not be used.**
- **Bo Halloran**, 51, the Cordage at Saltmarket, an old rope works with evening classes. Twenty-nine years on the water, right hand dead, eleven years in a sling. 61 students across a week, 4 Institute-trained. **Has watched for nine years which of the fifty-seven put a hand on a crate before they pick it up; about eleven do, and every one of them is over fifty and has been hurt before.** Will not give Marek the bucket, will not write a document, and will not be doing him a favour.
- **Hollis Pratt**, 51, Continuity Office field assessor, District 3, nineteen years. See above. Procedural, fair, frightened, and not a solution. His telephone habit: *I always write it down.*
- **Deb**, about fifty, head cook on Sennet Row, a ledger and no face. Kept Tam on the books while she could and was told *liability* out loud in a kitchen about him. Wants him back on the board in September. **Marek is not the one who is to tell Tam this.**
- **Peta**, a market porter with a hand truck who takes the fish in at the Exchange back door at six in the morning and does not read the top half of the delivery dockets.
- **Wick Oyelaran**, St Anselm's amateur boxing, Lattice. Refused, and the reason he gave is the most useful sentence Tam has heard since the operation.
- **Conor**, nineteen, Halloran's Tuesday class, in the strap in Chapter 17.
- **Harbridge**, Continuity Office District 3, the man who answers a telephone and does not make policy.
- **An unnamed man of seventy-two** in Lena Senn's ward, in for a hip off a gate at work, up and dressed at four in the morning. **Not Mal Farrow. Do not conflate.**
- **There is no vacancy at Spine 7 and Ms. Quill has asked for nothing.** A lead repairer's post is never mentioned in Chapter prose in this batch; the line about Roos's vacancy and Sera asking twice was in this file only and has been removed, because it invents a fact and offers Movement III a lever the text has not set up. If a Spine vacancy is wanted later, it must be established on the page first.

### What is deliberately still unspent

- **The licensor.** Blank, unanswered, now with a count attached (940 in the district) and a refusal in writing. Nobody has proved anything about who wrote the fourth beat and nobody has looked for an approval page.
- **The four explanations** in Chapter 7 are all still live and Chapter 18 says so in his own hand.
- **The instructor booklet has been read and copied; what it has never been given is a provenance.** Marek read all eleven pages in the store room on the Thursday night of week 5 and copied pages three, four and nine by hand in the copy room in Chapter 9. **The earlier line in this file saying page nine is unopened was wrong and has been removed — it contradicted finished prose in two chapters.** The live reservation is unchanged and narrower: **there is still no revision history, no design-approval entry, no search for one, and nothing on any of the eleven pages that ties the script to a person, a date or an approval.** He has the artefact and no provenance, and Chapter 25 is where the connection is made.
- **Marrow Falls.** Sera has not said the name, has not explained the page, and has not said who wrote the row. The Crown Clause is not named. Nobody in this batch identifies the signature's authorship.
- **Evan's card** was used once as a shim, on a Saturday, at a bench, and Marek looked at the blank fifth line for the first time in seven years and worked out that a father can leave a blank line. **He got nothing else out of it. The card is still in his tool roll. It is a tool and not a clue, and this volume must not explain it.**
- **The envelope** left for Marek at the Threadline depot on the Tuesday of week 9 and still unopened on the Saturday.
- **Rusk's 2019 stores file** about a set return contact, which he has never read.
- **The stores line** for spare stock issued to households: ninety out in September, forty back, fifty unaccounted, untracked since 2019. Roos has said it is not a scheme she knows about and that she now has to think about it. **Nobody knows whose fifty houses they are.**

## Corrections carried by the Batch 0002 fix pass

Recorded so that the next batch does not reintroduce them. Each was a contradiction in finished prose, not a planned change.

- **Who signed for what.** Chapter 7 is canon: in September, standing in a queue, it was **Marek himself** who put his own name and his own student card against the delivery note for nine crates, on a Tuesday, without reading it. **Rusk signed for the kit itself, off a depot docket, in the summer, because signing for things is his job** (Ch 9, Ch 11). Chapters 11 and 19 had merged the two events and given Rusk Marek's queue; both are now corrected, and Chapter 19's refusal speech names Marek's own signature, which is the point of it.
- **Farrow's first sighting is a Thursday.** Thursday of week 6, at six feet, in a room, in a loud market. Chapter 13 once said Tuesday in one line and Thursday in two others. **Marek has been down to the fish office yard three times** — Thu wk 6, Wed wk 8, Thu wk 9 — and Farrow counts them in Chapter 18.
- **The rule in Marek's own handwriting is four weeks old** as of Chapters 16 and 17. It was written on the Thursday night of week 5. It was previously given as three weeks and five weeks in three places.
- **PD/7/114 is a Tuesday number and 100 is a Tuesday number.** The deduction in Chapter 12 works on 114 − 100 = 14, so both must fall on the same weekday. It previously had 100 on a Friday, which made the whole diary-not-a-hand inference impossible.
- **Tam's box at the Exchange is nine kilos.** The eighteen-kilo figure in Chapter 16 contradicted the scene, the platform and the ledger in the same chapter.
- **The four-page file offer was declined on the Friday of week 9**, at ten past four, in Roos's office, on the form in Chapter 19. The form had said Thursday.
- **Chapter 14 ends on the load-book entry.** The closing narrator paragraph that announced when the recognition would arrive has been removed: it was narrator prolepsis rather than scene, it was the wrong number of days out, and it pre-spent Chapter 20.
- **The Chapter 25 embargo holds.** Verified by reading: no revision history, no design-approval entry, no search for either, no claim that a person chose the substitution, anywhere in Chapters 1–20. Chapter 19's only deliberate act is Roos tabling the variance, which is a different thing and is spent in full.
- **Tam's "6 weeks" on the middle finger is his own figure and is not anchored to the operation.** He wrote the line on the Thursday of week 8, about nineteen days after surgery, and it is item four of column three — the item that costs him, and the one he underlines. **Do not try to reconcile it with the operation date and do not quietly renumber it.** If a later chapter needs a duration, use the registrar's own words from Chapter 11: he will promise the other two fingers and not the middle one, and he will know more in a fortnight than anybody knows now.
- **Choir of Practice, envelope, Rusk's 2019 stores file, the fifty houses, Oren Vey, Nell** — all unchanged and all still unspent.

## Corrections carried by the Batch 0003 review fix pass

Recorded after a second, external review of the batch. Each was a contradiction in finished prose or in the state files, not a planned change. No chapter was added, cut, restarted or reordered, and no canon was reversed.

- **Chapter 23 had two houses and two Nells in one unbroken scene.** The Fenn hallway, the Senn front room and the Fenn hallway again ran together with a single scene break, and Yvette Fenn's line *both my daughters* made Marek's sister the Fenns' daughter. **The chapter now has two houses and a scene break between them:** the Fenn hallway at about 19:20 on the Wednesday of week 10, then the Senn front room at 19:25. **Nell Senn is Marek's sister and is in the Senn house. The Fenn daughter is Ama.** The borrowed kit was bought by the Fenns and handed to the Senns; see the Fenn entry under new people.
- **Nell is sixteen.** The September form's *girl of fifteen* is Nell **in September**, which is why the line exists at all; every present-tense reference to her age in Chapters 23 and 29 is sixteen.
- **The Choir is eleven on market day and nine at the table.** Chapter 21's public beats happen before nine people. Chapter 23 no longer says *eleven people at this table*. Both numbers are now stated together in the Choir section below.
- **The eleven-page instructor script is on the page in Chapter 25**, in the boundaries column of the full sheet, with its four lines printed and its source named. **The script → revision history → design approval link is now visible in the chapter rather than asserted off-page**, which is what the batch card required and what `open-threads.md` thread 11 already claimed.
- **Chapter 25 now puts the shoebox rule on the table before breaking it.** Marda tells Marek, in front of nine people, that the sheet is out of the box, that she refused him on the Wednesday, that she has not changed her mind and has been outvoted eight to three, and that the member was told and has not withdrawn it. The box has been in the co-operative for two years.
- **Chapter 23 reconciles the *none supplied* entry with the box.** Marda says the entry is true of every word including *none supplied* — the originating practice has never sent them a line — so that a supplier not giving the co-operative a design record is not the same thing as the co-operative holding a document a member walked in with.
- **The two voices are Tam's, given to Marek on purpose in week 9, and Marek's Chapter 24 page no longer claims them.** He had stolen Tam's Saturday queue scene out of the ledger. What Marek's page now records is that the fluency is *his* and that he has earned nothing about the gift. **The two voices in Tam's own release all persist, and Marek must not become good at something he has not practised.**
- **The SC-4 refusal is FIVE noes and a stop.** The previous canon of four was a miscount that dropped Mal Farrow, who speaks and refuses in the scene. Chapter 29, this file, `current.md`, `open-threads.md`, `character-state.md` and `chapter-summaries.md` all now say five plus Tam's stop. **Do not change it back to four.**
- **The nineteen seconds are at 06:35, not 06:52.** 06:35 is the boy's own stamp on his consent sheet and the attempt follows it. 06:52 was a load-book page header, read as the event time. The yard opens at about ten past six and Marek is against the door by half past six.
- **Chapter 24 no longer narrates Chapter 29.** It had the four refusals in the past tense six days before they happen. The page now says he knows they would refuse and has not asked any of them.
- **Nell's silence is counted from the Wednesday of week 10 and is nine days on the Friday of week 11 and twelve days on the Monday of week 12.** The old figures were six, nine, sixteen and nine in four places.
- **The service bulletin is read once.** Chapter 26 gives Marek the carbon out of the envelope. **Chapter 27 no longer has Vance read the card out again as if new: what he gives Marek is the depot's outbound van book, with four signatures and a fifth line carrying a date and no signature.** The five addresses are one copy each; the depot did not forward copy five and has never sent a van to the address on it.
- **Chapter 28 is one day, the Friday of week 11.** It contains no week-12 scene; Vey proposes the Monday in it and Marek's ledger anticipates it.
- **The photocopies in Chapter 25 now happen.** Roos sends Marek to the copy room, keeps a dated initialled copy for the file, gives him his, and the dated slip she hands him is identified as the slip. The state file no longer says she took the only copy.
- **Choir correction read aloud in a market no longer contains a placeholder.** *Recorded [date]* is filled in.
- **Chapter 22's yard page is stamped 11:20**, which is what an arrival at 09:40 and an hour and forty minutes by the scales produces.
- **Chapter 26's switchboard says eight days**, spoken on the Thursday of week 11 about the 28th, which is a Wednesday of week 10. The nine-day figure belongs to the Friday of week 11 and is still used there.
- **Chapter 27's figures are five weeks and eleven years**, not a fortnight and a month, for the operation and for Farrow's ribs.
- **The drill sequence has been in the Choir's quarterly for eight years**, not eleven. It was first written in 2016.
- **Vey is forty-one. Kosta has been at the yard thirty-one years and drove a shunter for twenty-six of them.** The word *variant* about Rafi, and Marek's own relationship to it, are unchanged.
