## Verified in-world timeline of Chapters 21–30 (Movement III, weeks 10–12)

Use this before assigning any new date. Weeks run Monday to Sunday. **The only calendar date fixed by earlier canon is that the twenty-eighth is a Wednesday, and that Wednesday is the Wednesday of week 10.** Everything else in Movement III is given as a week and a weekday, and should stay that way: a single month cannot be reconciled against the two earlier timelines, and Batch 0003's fix pass removed every invented month-date rather than add a fourth contradiction.

- **Mon wk 10** — Ch 21. The four rules copied onto a work order. The counter at 09:10, the Halloran telephone refusal, Roos in the corridor at 10:15, the Exchange and the Choir's table from about 15:00, the load book after 21:00.
- **Tue wk 10, 05:40–22:30** — Ch 22. The Exchange kitchen from 05:40, the fish office yard from about 09:40, the ramp off the Cordage at about 14:30, the waste yard off the canal from about 18:30, the stairwell on Pell Street at 22:30.
- **Wed wk 10, 10:10–00:20** — Ch 23. The Exchange and the Choir from 10:10, the Fenns' hallway at about 19:20, his mother's kitchen at 23:00, the load book at 00:20 (which is therefore the **Thursday** of week 10 and is dated as such).
- **Thu wk 10, morning** — Ch 24 opens. Marek tells Rafi before work. **Thu wk 10, 19:00–21:10** — the Ninth Street class.
- **Fri wk 10, 06:20** — Tam Arrick asks Mal Farrow, in the fish office yard, unarranged, and Farrow says yes on three conditions and writes them on a docket.
- **Sat and Sun wk 10** — the bad weekend: the queue outside the wholesale kitchens on the Saturday, and the column-two break on the Sunday at 21:10, logged as grid item 12.
- **Mon wk 11, 09:40** — the strap comes off, thirty-two days after the Friday of week 6. **Mon wk 11, 16:35** — the failed attempt, grid item 13. **Mon wk 11, 22:40** — the two voices get better, grid item 14. The strap goes back to the department in an envelope.
- **Tue wk 11, 06:10 onward; 06:35** — the nineteen seconds at the Exchange back door bar. **06:35 is the boy's own stamp on his consent sheet and is the time of the attempt.** Farrow's written refusal, in his own hand, is dated the Wednesday of week 11.
- **Wed wk 11, 07:40 onward** — Ch 25. Rusk and the 2019 stores file; the Choir's full sheet and the design approval; Roos's office; Pratt on the telephone; Sarr in the corridor; the canal wall; the load book 21:45–00:10, then a short coda with Rafi.
- **Thu wk 11, 11:00–15:30** — Ch 26. The service engineer in the lay-by before 11:00, Talia in the treatment bay, Sera at the Spine at 13:15, the depot telephone at 14:10, the envelope opened at about 15:30. The load book at 01:00 on the Friday. **The closing scene, Farrow's written account, is the Friday morning of week 11 at 09:10 at the Spine and comes after the load-book page, not before it.**
- **Fri wk 11, 08:10–10:30** — Ch 27. The Threadline service depot in Lattice. **Wed wk 10 was the twenty-eighth and the depot run; this is the Friday after it, and Marek is nine days late on a promise made in a corridor on the Friday of week 9.** Oren Vey appears in the corridor at about 15:40.
- **Fri wk 11, 11:00–19:00** — Ch 28. The panel sits on the Friday of week 11 and adjourns at about 12:10; Oren Vey is outside the department office at 14:40 and takes Marek to lunch on the Friday. **Chapter 28 contains no week-12 scene: Vey only *proposes* the Monday of week 12 there, and Marek's load book anticipates it.** Do not read the chapter as spanning two days.
- **Mon wk 12, 10:10–13:20** — Ch 29. The Exchange back room, about forty people, **five noes and a stop**.
- **Tue wk 12, 08:10–01:00** — Ch 30. The noticeboard sheet; the telephone calls; Petar Vance at 11:00; Aurel Kosta at 16:30; the request to the Choir; the load book at 23:30. **The Exchange monthly meeting is the first Wednesday, which is the Wednesday of week 12, and Movement IV opens on it.**

## Canon now fixed in Chapter prose (Chapters 21–30)

### The Choir of Practice

- A co-operative of **212 members** — nurses, repairers, two teachers, a midwife, four drivers, a man who mends organ pipes — that meets on market days on a trestle table at the Saltmarket Exchange, next to the noticeboard. **Eleven people on market day, and nine of them are at the table at a time.** Every public beat of the Choir happens *in front of nine people*; the two numbers are not interchangeable and are not a contradiction. A quarterly, four times a year, a hundred and forty pages, printed in the district, **nine hundred addresses**, a free copy of the last one on the table and the table itself is free to read.
- **A lineage has four columns and no fifth: origin, revisions, boundaries, refusals.** The origin says who wrote it, where, what they were doing at the time, in a sentence a person can understand, not a department's word. Revisions say what changed, when, by whom, and **what they said the reason was**. Boundaries say what the technique may not touch and who may stop it. **Refusals say what the co-operative asked for and was told no.**
- **Everything on a sheet is a claim.** The Choir prints lineages it is quite sure are wrong, with the year it became sure in the margin, because a co-operative that only prints what it believes is a church. In four years it has printed four it is sure are wrong.
- **A request is either five pounds out of a print fund (the tin has four pounds and change in it) or free on a public-safety ground.** A safety request goes into the request book in full — name, technique, ground, date — is read out at the next market day to anybody who comes to the table, and if it is upheld the requester is **named in the quarterly with the ground**, and there is no mechanism for taking either back out. **Everything about a person, published by them, is published by name: corrections, disputes, requests, refusals.**
- **New people. Marda Szabo**, 51, orthopaedic nurse turned records clerk, a member for eleven years, does the lineages and the market table. **Ekow Nartey**, 33, keeps the request book, wears a market coat, tells you the cost of a thing before you ask for it, and reads corrections out loud to nine people in a market. **Beattie, Yusuf** — a member with a daughter, who voted against printing a distributor's design reference and is right, and is named in the minutes.
- **The rule about the box.** There is **one** design record in the co-operative's holdings, in a shoebox, in a brown envelope, and the line on the envelope is *you keep this, it is the only one anybody has ever sent us*. **It does not come out on a request, from anybody, for any reason.** The reason is not a rule: the last time a document came out of that box on somebody's request, the person who brought it in was named in a newspaper, she is a member, she still comes to market day, and she does not speak to nine of them. **It has been in the box for two years, and the member brought it in having been in a room in the middle of August.** **Chapter 25 is where the co-operative breaks this deliberately, by vote, eight to three, and the three noes are printed** — Sarr, Beattie and Ekow, with Marda in the majority and one of the three. **Chapter 25 puts the promise on the table in front of nine people before it breaks it**: Marda tells Marek, in the room, that the sheet on the trestle is out of the box, that she told him on the Wednesday and refused him, that she has not changed her mind and has been outvoted, and that the member was told at her own table and has not withdrawn it. **Do not break this rule again after Chapter 25.**

### The design approval — TRC-419-88 (the Chapter 25 proof, spent)

- **Raised the eleventh of August, in the week before the term began. Approved the fifteenth of August. The nine crates went out in the first week of term, on a Tuesday, in a queue outside a car park.** Inside the term nothing may be called "the summer"; the term before this one is the phrase.
- The **practicum drill sequence's own lineage**, printed in full on the Choir's sheet: origin 2016, Nacre Institute methods group, **the group did not record who wrote it**; revisions 3-08, 3-11, 4-02, 4-19; sources for the first three *in-house, methods group, held*; source for 4-19 the design approval record itself.
- **The instructor script is on the page, in the boundaries column, and it is the connective tissue.** Eleven pages, **no name on the title page, no date, no initials anywhere in it**; one copy was given to the Choir by a member who has never performed anything from it. Four of its lines are printed: *Four beat (scope). The learner does not perform this beat. The learner takes the scope. The instructor confirms the scope aloud. The learner releases on the instructor's confirmation.* **The script and the approval record are printed in the same column, on the same sheet, in the same room, and the sheet says why: a co-operative that filed them separately would be a co-operative that let somebody put them back in a box.** Three thousand of the eleven-page books went out in the crates, and they reached a hundred and eleven students.
- **The Institute's 2016 methods-group note, printed in full on the same sheet and in the building:** *the fourth beat is the practitioner's, and a class that cannot make the fourth beat has been taught wrongly, not taught safely.* **This is the document that makes the case, and it is in the same college as a store room, a cage and a hundred and eleven students.**
- **The approval record carries no name.** A function, a reference number, a date and a stated reason. **Nobody in this city has read it except one unnamed member of the Choir, who copied it into plain language by hand and has not asked to be named and would not be printed if they did.**
- **The rationale, in the record's own words, and it is a good reason:** a learner cannot be relied upon to apply the scope under load; where the class contains mixed prior training the scope has been applied early, with the subject still in the load, twice in the last six months, and in one of those the subject was a fourth-year assistant; a single supervised release route has been retained, the instructor confirms, and **the learner is not to be taught to release on his own initiative**; design review accepts the loss of the practitioner beat as within tolerance for a supervised course; **the change is not intended for unsupervised practice and the card is not to be issued to community or household distribution in a revision that omits the practitioner beat, which is what this revision is.**
- **What Marek physically holds is four sentences in his own handwriting**, copied off the overleaf at the table, initialled and dated by Ekow Nartey. In Roos's office on the Wednesday of week 11 he photocopies it on the machine at the end of the corridor, **and Roos photocopies it too and initials and dates hers**, so the departmental file holds one and he holds one. Roos's line: *I am keeping one, because a receipt that is not in a press is a rumour, and the only copy in the city is not going to be in a filing system in a building that has refused to produce a working file for four years. And I would rather it was in your pocket and in a market.* The dated slip she gives him **is** the paper with the date typed at the top; the file entry is a line in a book with a number against it, and the number is not a decision.
- **The embargo is now spent and must not be re-proved.** The four explanations of Chapter 7 died all at once in Chapter 25 and were replaced by one: somebody chose it, and wrote down why, and the why is defensible. **The blank licensor field remains unanswered and must stay so to Chapter 50.**

### The supply chain

- **The service bulletin SB-4-19-03.** Issued **in the first week of term, three days after the last crate on that lot went out of the Lattice gate**, by a regional service office two districts up, second line, Ashfields Road, which is the only office in the company that writes anything about a practice. Received at Lattice two days later and forwarded **one copy to one address**, copies one to five: the Nacre Institute practical department, the Cordage evening classes, the Ninth Street rooms, the mobile clinic on the Saltmarket route, and **a registered office with a postal town on it that is not Nacre.** The depot's own outbound van book has four lines, four dates and **four driver signatures for copies one to four. Copy five went on the van and came back: the driver asked whether he was meant to sign it, was told to put it on the shelf, and it has sat on the second shelf under a pile of manifests ever since, with a date against it in the van book and no signature.** **The fifth copy never left the building, and the envelope in Chapter 26 is the envelope somebody used to take it out in.** **Marek does not get a second reading of the sheet in Chapter 27; what he gets is the van book, and Vance's own new sentence is that he does not think the man at the counter came in for Marek.** The subject line is *return contact — revised for district distribution. Not a fault. Do not claim. Prior practice continues unchanged.* **Vance's horror is that it was written in advance, before there was anything to claim, so that any future claim can be answered in four minutes by a true and dated sheet.**
- **The envelope.** A buff service envelope, a printed label reading **M. S. SENN — INSTITUTE — CIVIC SPINE 7**, a second label pulled off with a fingernail leaving a corner, left at the Threadline depot counter on the Tuesday of week 9 by a man who stood about four minutes, did not sit down, gave no name, and wrote four lines in pencil on the back of a **routing slip**: *Ask for the distribution list, not the bulletin. The bulletin is what everybody gets. — don't write back to this envelope.* The depot's visitor column for that Tuesday reads *counter, unaccompanied*. **Marek opened it on the Thursday of week 11, not before.**
- **The component intake summary for lot TL-4-19-0311**, second shelf, signed out by Vance only: received in the first week of term on a Tuesday; supplier **Meridian consolidation point, lot onward**; 24 practice plates across 9 crates, one instructor notes booklet per three crates, one card set per three crates, 26 boundary labels, 8 folding frames; upstream technique bundle and upstream licensor **both ruled and both empty**; **upstream revision: 4-19.** The eleven pages came in the box, three thousand of them, with no name, no date and no initials on any of them, and Vance has had that exact book in his hands about four hundred times.
- **Two other lots off the same consolidation point, same revision:** **TL-4-19-0307**, 19 August, twenty-two plates across four boxes, to a courier crew at the west berth; and **TL-4-19-0322**, eighteen units, to a district clinic in the Ashfields direction Vance has never been to. **The lot leads to a building, not to a person, and that is the chapter's turn.**
- **Vance's two written lines** in the depot book, against Marek's initials, both *I don't remember*, dated Wednesday and Friday of week 6. **The volunteered third line is dated Friday of week 11**, written by Marek in his own hand on the grid and countersigned by Vance, typed and signed by **Kerry Amoah**, 31, six years in the depot, chosen by Vance and not by Marek because the witness has to be somebody who writes what you say and not what you think you mean.
- **The stores open day is the third week of September, about ninety households, four weeks after the approval.** A single side of A4 with a tear-off stub, a different typeface in the reference number, and boxes for *purchase group* and *supervised by*. **Dashes are legal.** Nobody read two dashes back to anybody.

### Oren Vey, the job, and SC-4

- **Oren Vey, 41**, operations director, Threadline, two years and four months in the job. **He has never seen a page of the design record and has only ever had a four-sentence file summary.** He is not the author, does not know the author exists as a document, and has asked four times for section 4 to be filled in on intake and been refused by every supplier, one of which has twice given him a reference number that corresponds to nothing he can look up. **He has never put that in writing, because in writing it is a resignation.**
- He is courteous, specific, funny, tired, and the most likeable person in the batch. He **stopped the audit programme on the four sites on the Thursday of week 11, unasked**, and said so in advance so that Marek could not later say nobody thought about it — and the consequence, which Marek wrote down, is that there is now no routine reason for anybody from that company to be in those four buildings, and so nobody can check that he did.
- **The offer: district service, thirty-four, rising at the end of the first year, four weeks' holiday, a card in his name, a reference any workshop will honour inside two years, and the sentence *may practise under supervision*.** **It is not a Working Share and must never be written as one.** He also offered a **one-month trial** that can be handed back without difficulty. **Marek has neither refused nor accepted it and it is in his coat.**
- **SC-4, the supported completion route.** A wrist cuff, a contact, one tone, and a trained certified assistant standing inside the room. Same tone, same pitch, same length, same place in the beat, every time. His argument, made sincerely and better than the record: *a key-holder is only a problem when the key-holder is arbitrary; a man forty feet away twice a week in a bad mood is arbitrary; a procedure that is taught, the same every time, staffed, paid and complainable to is a rule you can rely on.*
- **It was refused in public on the Monday of week 12 at the Exchange back room**, in front of about forty people, **by five people and stopped by a sixth** — the five being Dessa Kwan, Reuben, Mal Farrow, Ines Sarr and Rafi Pell: **Dessa Kwan, 27** (the cuff tells the cook the load has settled; she needs to know about the man at table four; there is no machine in this city that can hear that; and it is not free, and a route that needs a wage is a route that stops when the wage stops); **Reuben**, 22, six weeks on the Saturday rate (eleven men on a Saturday, six of them over fifty, five of them if those six are off, and he is the youngest); **Ines Sarr** (she has been the word in her own room for four months and found it over a cup of tea on a Friday, and she would use it, on the first hard afternoon, against a nineteen-year-old she has known four months); **Rafi Pell** (you have not changed anything, you have put the man in a chair with a lanyard and given him a procedure); **and Mal Farrow** (he does not say it does not work — he says it works and he would use it on a Tuesday, and that he is the only one of them who has been the floor of a fish market with a plate on and nobody in the room but a nurse and a man with a key to the container, and that he did it himself, out loud, to nobody, for two and a half minutes). **Tam Arrick is the sixth: he gave a written consent on the Wednesday, read it out in the room, and then said stop, and was not asked why, because the paper said nobody may ask.** **THE COUNT IS FIVE NOES AND A STOP. An earlier note in this file said four; four was a miscount that dropped Farrow, who speaks and refuses in the scene. Do not change it back.** **Oren Vey then said the sentence against himself, unprompted: he had demonstrated the exact opposite of the only thing a panel can act on, and he had done it on a Monday morning for a commercial reason.** He is not defeated. He said there has to be something in its place, there is not one, and he has watched five people try.
- **The demonstration requirement is unchanged and still has nothing behind it**, and the batch ends on the recognition that the only demonstration offered to a public room in this city this term is the thing five people refused. **Movement IV must not re-stage the refusal, and must not count it again in a different number.**

### The dates, fixed

- **The strap came off on the Monday of week 11, thirty-two days after theatre, which is a bit over four weeks** — Mrs Ollerton's scheduled date, not Tam's estimate. She spells her name on the wall chart since 1988. She does not say *normal* about the middle finger and does not write on it. **The registrar's weight date is the last Friday in the term, at the end of week 12**, and it is the first day a hand may go under a load.
- **Tam's own "6 weeks" on the middle finger is his and is not anchored to the operation.** The finger had still not moved at the end of this batch.
- **The nineteen seconds, at 06:35 on the Tuesday of week 11**, are the largest thing anybody has done in the case. **06:35 is fixed by Tam's own consent sheet and is not a load-book figure; the 06:52 that appears in older notes was the load-book page header and was wrong.** They are witnessed, they are not a technique, the witness says so in his own words and refuses a name twice-over, and they do not count towards anything.

### New people in prose, Chapters 21–30

- **Marda Szabo**, 51. **Ekow Nartey**, 33. **Kerry Amoah**, 31, depot office. **Vesna Kolar**, 49, drives the compaction truck on the Saltmarket waste loop; her fourth beat is called *walking round the compactor*, it is not a beat, and she has been a person in a form once and it cost her a round a week for a year. **Yvette Fenn**, 48, bought four wrist units in September and lives two doors from Lena Senn on Nine Bridges. **Ama Fenn**, 22, Yvette's older daughter, **Odile Fenn's** older sister, uses a wheelchair and has a wrist that goes. **Odile Fenn** is a third-year in Marek's own group and is **not** in the Chapter 23 scene; do not put her in it. **Reuben**, 22, six weeks on the Saturday rate, crates off a truck up a run, and the only independent observation nobody in the case has ever been allowed to buy. **Aurel Kosta**, 58, **thirty-one years at the yard and twenty-six of them driving a shunter**, at a goods yard off the Meridian Corridor.
- **Yvette Fenn's four units resolve as: one on Nell's wrist, one on Ama's, two in a tea chest under the stairs.** The kit on Nell's wrist was **bought by the Fenns and handed to the Senns** — the Fenns are next-door neighbours on Nine Bridges *and* the family of a classmate, since Odile Fenn is a third-year in Marek's group, which is the outline's "a classmate's family" and the outline's "neighbour" at the same time. **Neither the Fenns nor the Senns are named on the September form.** The form's purpose box holds one line, *girl, fifteen, wrist*, which is Nell in September, and a second line in a different pen, *and one for my own, she is older, she has it worse*, which is Ama. **Two lines, no name between them: the two dashes are one box that could only hold one, not a lie about a second daughter.** Chapter 23 has two houses in it and they are separated by a scene break — the Fenn hallway at about 19:20, the Senn front room at 19:25 — and **Nell Senn is Marek's sister and is in the Senn house, not the Fenn one.**

### Planning deviations, recorded so they are not read as errors

- **Oren Vey appears in Chapters 27 and 28**, a chapter earlier than the batch card and in two places in Chapter 28 (a corridor and a café). The volume outline says he "appears in person once"; Chapter 27–28 is that appearance, and the outline should be read as *one movement*, not *one scene*.
- **The Chapter 30 mini-climax is split**: the public refusal of SC-4 is Chapter 29, and the service bulletin number becoming a thread anybody can follow is Chapter 30.
- **The registrar's weight date is the last Friday in the term, at the end of week 12**, which is the registrar's six weeks and matches the batch instruction.
- **New items that must not be contradicted in Chapters 31–40:** the intake summary's counts, lots 0307 and 0322, the four-corner custody list, the four Nacre addresses on the bulletin, the fifth address outside the district, the named correction to the noticeboard sheet, and the fact that the Choir's one design record is a **four-sentence** hand-copy rather than a photocopy.

## Verified in-world timeline of Chapters 31–40 (Movement IV, weeks 12–13)

Use this before assigning any new date. Weeks run Monday to Sunday. **The only calendar date fixed anywhere in this volume is that the twenty-eighth is a Wednesday of week 10.** Everything else in Movement IV is given as a week and a weekday, and no month-date is used in the prose. The derivation: week 10 runs Mon 26 – Sun 1, **week 11 = Mon 2 – Sun 8, week 12 = Mon 9 – Sun 15, week 13 = Mon 16 – Sun 22, week 14 = Mon 23 – Sun 29.** None of those month names appear in the chapters.

- **Wed wk 12, 09:30–12:00** — Ch 31. The Exchange monthly meeting from ten; the four minutes; the argument by the tea urn; the card in the tin; the four-column book pinned up at 11:50; the first entry at 11:50. **The Exchange monthly meeting is the first Wednesday of the month, which is the Wednesday of week 12, and it is also a market day** (the Choir is on the floor, eleven people, nine at the table).
- **Thu wk 12, 09:40–21:30** — Ch 32. The **end-of-term practice-variance panel** at ten, adjourned at 10:20 with no matter; Roos in the corridor; the Lower Wards from about 14:20 to 17:00; the bus down at 18:10; the three-line load-book entry at 21:30.
- **Fri wk 12, 08:00–17:00** — Ch 33. **Tam's registrar's weight date**, the last Friday in the term, off the Ninth Street from 08:10; the Spine at 11:50; **the rescue off Fell Road from about 10:25 to 12:30**; the ambulance at 12:20. **Marek arrives on Fell Road at 12:02, ninety-seven minutes after the man came off the chain.** The copy of Talia's page is given on the Saturday in Chapter 34, and the Weir's new white paint on the bottom rail of the lock-keeper's hut door is noticed at the end of Chapter 34.
- **Sat wk 12, 09:40–19:00** — Ch 34. The noticeboard and the book (**nineteen lines by 10:10 on the Saturday**); Ferry Road terraces; the Weir pull-off at half past two; the clinic lay-by at four.
- **Sun wk 12, 09:00–23:20** — Ch 35. The records request written in ninety seconds at the kitchen table; the laundrette; **Corvin Ashby's telephone call at four o'clock**; the pump house at half past four; the full four-heading load-book page at 23:20. **Ashby's entry in the Exchange book is line 28, written on the Sunday afternoon.**
- **Mon wk 13, 09:30–16:00** — Ch 36. The first-year common room from 09:30; the Exchange at 12:20 (**thirty-one lines, line 28 is Ashby's**); the Weir from about 14:30.
- **Tue wk 13, 07:00–02:00** — Ch 37. **Pell Street at 07:00 first, then the bench at the Spine at about 07:45** (the two scenes were transposed in the first pass and have been put back in clock order); the Weir at 10:10; the telephone to Asha Reed at 13:00; the fault form at 02:00. **The notebook line about breaking his own rule is written at the bench at about 07:50 and is forward-looking — he writes that he is *going to* ask two more people before one o'clock, not that he has.**
- **Wed wk 13, 06:05–21:00** — Ch 38. **The telephone call at 06:05, nineteen minutes, ended 06:24**; the Exchange at 11:20 on market day; the Weir at 14:30; the laundrette at 19:20; the Weir again at about 21:00.
- **Wed wk 13, 21:00 – Thu wk 13, 02:20** — Ch 39. The Spine night shift; **Oren Vey's telephone call at 23:10**; the full four-heading load-book page written at 02:20 on the Thursday.
- **Thu wk 13, 09:20 – 15:00** — Ch 40. **Hollis Pratt's formal review of the four local holds, 09:20 to about 11:45**, in the Institute's second-floor conference room; Rusk in the corridor at about 11:45; Roos's letter written at 14:20, **reference PD/13/158**, and read out at the end.

**The arithmetic the movement turned on, now fixed:** Chapter 40 falls on the **Thursday of week 13** and sets the witnessed assessment for **Tuesday of week 14**, which is exactly five days later and is the first day of Movement V. The term ended on the **Friday of week 12**, which was **Tam's registrar's weight date, and that date is six days behind Marek in Chapter 40** — it is behind him, not ahead of him. (The five-day figure belongs only to the gap between the Thursday of week 13 and the Tuesday of week 14 and must not be reused for this.) **The first round of the flood season is the Monday of week 14**, which is the day before the assessment.

## Canon now fixed in Chapter prose (Chapters 31–40)

### The Exchange monthly meeting and the four-column book

- **The Exchange monthly meeting** is the first Wednesday of the month. It meets in the back room from ten to eleven. **Anybody may put an item on a card into a tin before eight; the name on the card is the only requirement and need not be a member.** The chair is **Beatriz "Bea" Nunn, 61, ice merchant, nineteen years of chairing it.** There is **no vote for anybody**. Anybody who stands up and says the thing is important gets four minutes, and then the chair says *noted* in the tone of a woman noting a change in the weather.
- **The four-column book**, made on the Exchange's own headed paper on the Wednesday morning of week 12 and made into a lined book with a rubber band: **card — what is on it — what they were told — who took the call.** It is pinned to the noticeboard to the right of Marek's six lines. **The first name in the column is Bea Nunn's own, because she took the first call herself.** The **second line is Petar Vance's, in his own hand, dated the Friday**: he telephoned, gave his own name, said he is the service engineer on that bulletin in this district, said what the wall costs, said he knows the cost and is not asking for the sheet to come down, and said *a number is a reference until somebody with a sequence is looking for it*. **Nobody has written Marek Senn's name anywhere in it, and Marek has said in his own book that his name is nowhere in it — that is the load-bearing fact about the document and it must not be softened.** **Who writes a line: the caller writes their own words, in the first person, and signs the end of it, and the taker's name is recorded separately as the taker. Two conventions now on the page — Bea Nunn's first line is her own hand in the third person because she took the call and chose to write it as the taker, signed *— B. N.*; Corvin Ashby's line twenty-eight is his own first-person account, signed *— C. Ashby*, followed by *Call taken by B. Nunn, who wrote the rest of it down.* Never put a caller's signature in the fourth column as if it were the taker's.** The book has **two lines by the Friday morning of week 12** — Bea Nunn's from the Wednesday and Vance's from the Friday — nineteen by ten past ten on the Saturday, twenty-eight by Sunday afternoon, and **thirty-one by the Wednesday of week 13, and a thirty-second when Delia Frost hands in her sheet on the Wednesday morning.** **There is no figure of forty-one and there never was; do not write one.** All eleven of the first nineteen are wrists; four are hip units. **Vance's line is line 2 and it is dated the Friday. Vance telephoned the Exchange on the Friday morning of week 12 at about a quarter to eleven, not the shop, and Bea Nunn took the call and wrote it down. Marek read the book at the Exchange noticeboard at about eleven, saw the line, and caught a bus; he was on the bench at Civic Spine 7 when Bea telephoned the shop at 11:50 about the Weir.**
- **Asha Reed put a card in the tin on the Monday night and asked the room to keep the book**, on the ground that the sheet stays in the building and nobody can take it out of the building. It passed in about ninety seconds. On the Wednesday of week 13 she asked for it in the room, in front of eleven people, on her own account, and **Marda Szabo refused, and turned the refusal into a yes in a month** — the co-operative's rule is that everything about a person, published by them, is published by name, and Marda is fifty-one and has been waiting two years to be able to say a sentence like that to a person of nineteen. **Ekow Nartey** entered it as a request and read out the cost: five pounds out of a tin with four pounds in it, or a public-safety ground, in which case the requester's name goes in the book and in the quarterly if it is upheld.

### The Lower Wards, the mesh, and the flood season

- **The Weir**, off Fell Road, in the Lower Wards. A lock-keeper's hut a hundred and forty years old, a hand-painted sign reading **SLUICE & PUMP — CUTBANK WARDS / NO ENTRY WITHOUT A REASON / HAND TO A PERSON WHO KNOWS**, painted by nobody instructed, and **a new line of white paint along the bottom rail of the door** put on by the mesh after the Friday of week 12. The Cutbank steps, the upper pound, the flatbed with a tailboard, a van in the pull-off.
- **Corvin Ashby, 44**, twenty-two years in the wards, **keeps the mesh's records in a school exercise book with a rubber band round it and has kept it nine years, and it contains no names.** He **refused Marek at the door in nineteen seconds, before being asked, and gave the reason for free**, and the reason is that the last time anybody wrote down who does what on the Weir the report said *informal* and the pump money went in the spring and there has been no pump money since and the hand on that gate every year has been the ward's own. He has a mobile number in the book for somebody who was not answering on the Friday of week 12.
- **The round: the first round of the flood season, the Monday of week 14, ebb at ten past six off the tide table, **eleven days** out when it is first named on the Thursday of week 12, **ten days** out on the Friday and **nine days** out by the Saturday, about nineteen men, Cutbank to the upper pound, no cover, no licence, no insurer, no legal person.** (The figure was wrong twice in review: once as *two days*, then as *ten days on the Thursday*. Week 12 is Mon 9 – Sun 15 and the round is the 23rd, so the Thursday of week 12 is eleven days out.) If a man goes down, the form at the district wants a name and there is no name on the round and it writes *unidentified*. **Ashby has taken two men off the steps and is the man short on the pound.** The round is a back wall print with a line in white across it, and a new line of white paint.
- **Nkechi Osei, 33**, eleven years on the round, twenty-nine years in the work. **She counts to three on the rail on the way down off the last two, and she has never been taught anything, has never had a card, and does not want one.** On the Monday of week 13, on Marek's twelfth watch from the bank, **she began finishing on four** — and nobody in the lane caused it, nobody made a sound, and **Corvin Ashby saw it and Marek did not.** She is not frightened of him. She said so first, and then said she is frightened of the Tuesday. **Nobody in this city has a way of taking a count out of a person.** The only precedent in the case is a boy's two voices and she does not want it and is right not to.
- **Errol Nkemdi, 58**, Ferry Road terraces, retired ferryman, eleven years on the round in the past and now not on it, **wrist unit off the September open day, a form with a dash where the name goes, worn every day to hold a gate pole.** His sister-in-law **Bernadette Nkemdi, 41**, had said in front of thirty people in the Exchange that he should not have it on, and was right, and it took a stranger to make it happen. About eleven people on those three streets went to the open day and the van came round twice.
- **The district's flood-season return, DPS/FS/4**: four headings, on the form, in writing, with a date — who wrote it, what it touches, how a person stops it, how many people have been put through it — required of anybody in the district's area running a shared practice that involves a load. It is not an inquiry and it is not about anybody. **It carries a function, a reference number, a date and a stated reason, and no name, and Marek notices the shape and writes down that noticing it is not a finding.** The district panel member in chapters 28 and 32 is a woman of about fifty from that office.
- **The mesh is not a conspiracy and nobody in it is a cover for anything.** It is four to nine people who cannot write down who does what without losing the pump money.

### The rescue, and the narrow share

- **The Friday of week 12, off Fell Road at the upper pound. Bea Nunn telephones Civic Spine 7 at 11:50** (not 11:20: she is reporting an ambulance call rung at 11:40, forty minutes out, and the call has to come after it). Errol Nkemdi's contact had set in the shape of his own weight; **a set contact does not let go, it holds, and the contact is what holds the cuff shell shut, so the cuff cannot be opened until the weight comes off the hand, and the weight comes off the hand when he lets go of the chain, and the chain holds about a hundred and forty kilos of cast iron counterweight with about nine feet of water under it.** A nineteen-year-old called **Matteo** held a bar. Talia Venn was on Fell Road from about half past ten, off route, in her own time, taking two statements about a district open-day receipt list of ninety names.
- **Talia Venn's page, written in ninety seconds on the back of a card, the smallest page she has written in eight years. It has SEVEN headings, not five:** *purpose (his hand comes off this chain, nothing else, not today); duration (until it is off, or I say stop, or he says stop, whichever is first, on the clock and not on judgement); what stays (nothing — not his practice, not yours, not what I know about him, not what he says to me while it is happening); who may stop it (him, me, you, by word, no reason asked and none recorded); who it is for (him); when it ends (the day a person whose job that is looks at that arm and not one day before, or the Friday of week 14, whichever comes first, and it is not extendable by anybody who wants it to go on); how it ends (torn in half in front of him, neither half kept).* **Errol Nkemdi read it twice and said *leave it*.** It is signed three times: **hers in ink, his in capitals at her asking, and Marek Senn's in pencil**, which she made him come down the lane out of the place she had told him to stand to do, because a line that gives a man the power to stop something has his name on it. She keeps a copy of every page she writes (two hundred and six in a folder in a drawer) and **has never given one to anybody until now.** *(The sixth and seventh headings were added in review: the batch prompt required a review or destruction date on every share and the first pass had none.)*
- **The share Marek took: use only, of what she was doing, for as long as the chain was under load, on that plate. Nothing stays. Any of the three may stop it by word.** No care-link, no technique, no observation of the man's practice. **Marek's entire use in it was reading the outside of the cuff and saying four words to a man**: that it will not hold him, that a jammed contact holds the shape of a load and not the hand, and that it came off a table in a car park and nobody checked it. **Then he was sent to the top of the lane and stood there for eleven minutes and was not needed.**
- **The seventy minutes nobody remarked on, now on the page.** He was off the chain at 10:25, a nurse was on the lane from about 10:30, **nobody rang an ambulance until about 11:40**, and Bea Nunn reports the call at 11:50. **Talia Venn gives the reason out loud in front of nine people and writes it down and says her name goes under it:** he was awake and in no pain and when she asked what he wanted done he said *sit with me*; **she decided at about half past ten, when she got there, not to ring anybody, and did not ring until twenty to twelve, and says that most of an hour and a quarter was hers to take and she took it** (10:30 to 11:40 is seventy minutes; her phrase on the page is *most of an hour and a quarter*, which is hers and is correct as speech); a crew rung then would have been on the lane by about a quarter to eleven, an hour and a half before the one that actually came, and would have told him what she had told him, and then it would have been two of us asking a man to let go of a chain with a hundred and forty kilos at the end of it, and he did not need two. Asked whether she would make the same decision again: **at half past ten, yes; she would not sit on it for an hour and a quarter again.**
- **What it cost her:** she performed a technique on a patient who was not hers, on a day she was not on route, on a plate with no chart and no care-link in front of him, and the second page of her record has to say so; the plate is in the second drawer of a trolley in her vehicle and is a piece of a wrist unit off the September open day with no number on it; and she will be asked by a district office in about three weeks. **What it cost him:** her competence arrives and does not go away, he is sent up a lane, and the best afternoon of his term so far is one he was sent away from. **What she refused:** to let him thank her, to know whether he will file the records request, and to let him into the second page.
- **Talia's own thread, given to him as information and not as help:** the list of six people she had to take stock off came from a **district open-day receipt list of about ninety names**, of which she took two statements herself; the district has a copy; **she has an appointment about it on the Tuesday of week 14 in a first-floor room in Bower Street with a man whose title is *continuity and returns*.** Nobody in the city knows who compiled the list and it is not established that the district's practice standards office did.

### The records request, and the fifty

- **The September register.** The only instrument the case has ever had is the Institute's own open-day issue register and the counterfoil stubs, about ninety households, forty of the plates back and about fifty unaccounted for. **Marek wrote the records request out in ninety seconds on the Sunday morning of week 12 and did not post it. It is still in the flat in a drawer under a battery and a roll of solder, in a carrier bag with his name on it.** Grounds: patient safety, equipment traceability, no witness. **The cost is named in the load book: the register is right and the register is a list of ninety houses with a plate in them, and the second he asks for it the list is in a building that has already locked a cage and told a journalist nothing. He is not going to be the man who makes people findable.** This is written down as the fifth time he has found that out and not acted.
- **Enid Sarkis, 68**, the laundrette counter under Marek's own bedroom, nineteen years on the till, has had a wrist unit for three years and had it before he moved in. **She made him write her name, her age, and her wrist on a page in front of her and initial it**, and made him change *looked at* to **looked at by a person whose job that is**, and told him not to dare ring her at half past nine in the morning and ask her to talk to somebody. **Her own sentences are her own and they are the batch's most exact: *I want somebody to look at this wrist. Not today. Not because a man in a van comes round. Somebody who looks at it and tells me what it is, and if it is no good then it comes off and somebody writes down that it came off, in a book, with my name on it* (Ch 35); *then you'll have to keep coming, won't you* (Ch 38); and *Nineteen years. Four men … you are going to be a man who is difficult to be angry at, and in about two years that is going to be the most powerful thing about you and you are not going to have noticed it happening* (Ch 38, said in the laundrette on the Wednesday evening and heard by nobody but him).** She has had four men since the shop and has never had a record of any of them. **Enid Sarkis and Delia Frost both use the phrase *comes round* and they are not making the same argument.** Enid's is Chapter 35 and Chapter 38, Saturday and Wednesday evening: *the man that comes round*, *the four came round in a van*, *you're going to come round on a bus, and it isn't going to be for the company, and it isn't going to be for the form* — a man who arrives at a door. Delia's is Chapter 38 at six in the morning: *Then don't come. Not because I don't want you to. Because in a year's time you're going to be a name and I want to be the one who rang a number off a wall* — a man who answers a telephone. **An earlier note in this file filed Delia's line under Enid Sarkis; it is corrected here and the two are not to be merged.**
- **Delia Frost, 61**, over a takeaway on Carnation Row in Nine Bridges, hip unit ten weeks, telephoned the Spine at 06:05 on the Wednesday of week 13 and spoke for nineteen minutes, ending at 06:24. **Gets short of breath about four in the afternoon, twice, and it goes in ten minutes or a quarter of an hour, and she has told nobody.** **How she reached him, and it is one chain: she read the number on the noticeboard, rang it, and got a company in four minutes; somebody in a market told her the man who wrote the number up works in a relay shop on the corner; she rang the shop.** The book at the Exchange is the middle of that chain — the number reaches a company, the book reaches Marek, and a market reaches the shop. She was told she should be frightened of being alone with it in the middle of a winter and not of the plate, and she told him not to come, and she wrote herself into the Exchange book, which is **line 32, written on the Wednesday morning.** **Her sentence is the batch's most exact and it is hers, not Enid Sarkis's: *Then don't come. Not because I don't want you to. Because in a year's time you're going to be a name and I want to be the one who rang a number off a wall, and those aren't the same, and I can tell which one I am already.*** She adds, later in the same call, that she will ring the number again and that it will be him every time and that she does not mind. **Marek also routes her to a clinician, and this is now canon: he refuses to give a differential, makes her ring an out-of-hours number for Nine Bridges the minute the call is off the hook, reads it to her twice, tells her to say four words — *I get short of breath* — and not to mention the plate, and then rings the district's practice standards office himself at 06:50 and gives them her surname, her flat and the two times. She rings the out-of-hours number at 06:26 and is seen at eleven.** **This is a crisis route and is not a job, and it does not resolve Enid Sarkis, who is sixty-eight, has worn a plate every day for three years and is not acutely unwell.** He writes under the bench that a telephone number a stranger can ring is the whole of it and that he has been an idiot for a term. **This call is the proof that a number on a wall is a man standing behind it, and that not being the man in the room is not the same as not being the man everybody rings.**
- **The noticeboard number is now a public door.** Petar Vance priced the wall in week 12 as a sequence with a custodian; the actual price, found out in week 13, is that a man in a lane who needs one thing telephones it and is put through to a person. **That person is Marek Senn.** He wrote it down at the bench as the sixth form of a thing he has known since week four.

### The second dependence, and the deliberate handover

- **The recognition, Tuesday of week 13, at a bench at Civic Spine 7:** *I have a certificate in nothing. I have no licence, no assessment, no room, no list, no wage, no employer, and I am not anybody's responsibility, and I have been the fourth thing in three working buildings in a fortnight and I did it by standing on a bank and breathing, and there is no form on which a man can be recorded as having done it, which is the reason it worked.* **A key-holder without a certificate, arrived at by reputation, with no register on which the fact could be struck off.** Rafi Pell named the second half: *you can't hand it back, because there's no job to resign from, and if you give the work away you're just a man who used to stand on a bank, and that's worse.*
- **The mechanism, named by Corvin Ashby on the Monday afternoon of week 13 and not by Marek:** she came off three about a fortnight before Marek first came up the hill, and she came off four because a man stood on a bank for half an hour. *In about a year one of us is going to want to explain it to her and I want there to be a witness who can say that's how it happened, and I would rather the witness was you than me, because you are the one who did it.* **Marek's answer was the first time in twelve weeks he said a sentence he entirely believed and had no defence of: *I did not do anything.***
- **The handover is to three people he does not manage, and it does not work.** **Nkechi Osei** (who has to be told the truth about what he did, and is not grateful, and is right that anybody who finds somebody for her will have to be in the room, and that this makes it a bank again); **Asha Reed** (who takes the case and prices the cost out loud: on the first Wednesday of every month she will say in a first-year common room that the case is Senn's and the technique is nobody's yet, which makes her the woman who says the sentence about him, and she is nineteen and she is buying it); **Corvin Ashby** (who refuses, with the best argument anybody gave Marek in a term, because a job about a thing you do not understand is accountability, and because handing it to him makes him the word in his own ward, and because the day Marek stops coming the four of them and a rail is what she had before). **The fourth is Enid Sarkis and there is nobody to hand her to, and the correct answer is a person whose job it is, and there is no such person in this city.**
- **It does not work because the dependence was never the work.** Nkechi Osei is still on four at the end of the Wednesday. A woman rings a shop at six in the morning and gets nineteen minutes. A courier's balance step and a rail and a horn in a control post are the same object seen from three rooms.
- **Nobody is cured, nobody is forgiven, and the dependence stays.** Rafi counts to two in a kitchen now and says the habit does not give an exemption for the kitchen. The word *variant* is not forgiven. Nkechi Osei asked for a room with a bit of paper in it and somebody in it who can say no, and there is no such room.

### The formal review, and the recommendation

- **Hollis Pratt's formal review, Thursday of week 13, 09:20, the Institute's second-floor conference room, convened by the office.** Five at the table — Anneke Roos, Bo Halloran, Ines Sarr, Talia Venn, and Hollis Pratt — and **one at the back wall on a chair that had been in a store room since 2011, with nothing in his hands, having deliberately brought nothing.**
- **The findings, verbatim in substance:** each of the four holds was **properly made**, on that site's own authority, by a named person, reversible in a minute, and **none of them was a quarantine, a finding, or an accusation**, and in about a year somebody is going to write a paragraph about the week of week 8 and it is going to be wrong. **Each hold is no longer justified on any ground the office can put in a document** — not equipment, not safety, not a finding of variance, not the untraced licensor — **except one: the absence of a working non-hinged boundary release, performed by the person performing it, in a public room, with witnesses, and with no substituted cue used, followed or accepted inside the scored window.**
- **The recommendation, and its three riders, all said out loud:** that the four holds be lifted **if and only if** a working non-hinged release is demonstrated at a witnessed assessment convened by the department. **One: the recommendation is conditional on an event that has not happened, at a date that has not been set, by a person who has not been named, in a room the office has not seen. Two: the office cannot require a demonstration, cannot convene one, and cannot set a date, and if the department sets no date the four holds stand indefinitely with no review. Three: each site may refuse the recommendation today, in that room, without giving a reason, and no office in this city can make them lift it.**
- **The four answers. Bo Halloran: *Noted.*** That is a decision for today, and he wants a reason with a date on it before he takes the bucket down, and the cost is that he has to be seen to have set a condition he knows will not be met. **Ines Sarr refuses outright** — eleven people in a Thursday class and about forty through the door, not one of them insured by an employer, and she would rather be an old woman with a bucket in her hall than be right about a date; **Pratt records that her reason is the second strongest in the room and that it is stronger than the one the Institute is about to give.** **Talia Venn refuses outright, in writing, by the end of the day, and gives the strongest reason in the building**: on the Friday of week 12 a man's wrist came open in a lane, the plate came off and it is in the second drawer of a trolley in her vehicle, and it is going back on nobody, and if the hold is lifted it is still coming out, because she is the one holding it; and the honest reason there is no answer to that is that nobody is responsible for a fitting a young man out of a van does in a car park for nothing, and the only document there is about ninety households long. **She is the only person in the room whose hold has a person in it who has been hurt by it.** **Anneke Roos accepts, in principle and conditional on the same condition, and says out loud, before she has to, that she is doing it for the department's reasons and not for his** — because an office recommendation is the only sentence in the case that her department cannot dismiss as a grievance by a third-year, and she has forty-one people in a hall in four weeks' time. **That is the worst reason in the room and it is not stupid, and the prose says so.**
- **One further item, put in front of the deputy registrar by the assessor:** the network practice incident notification of week 6 carries category two — technique variance attributable to the trainee — ticked in a hand other than the candidate's and in a different batch of ink. It is on a file in the office and it is now on the record. Anneke Roos said, "Recorded."
- **The end-of-term panel's deadline, which is the mechanism of the whole movement.** The panel sat for the third time in a term on the **Thursday of week 12**, adjourned with no matter in twenty minutes, and **Professor Onyeka ruled that the end of term is the following day, that the panel will sit on a witnessed assessment if the department sets one, and that the department must notify the panel on or before the Thursday of week 13, and that if it does not the variance lapses at the end of term and the transcript line stands.** **The term therefore ends on the Friday of week 12, which is the same day as Tam's registrar's weight date, and the Thursday of week 13 is six days after the end of term and is out of term.** (Chapter 32 originally had Onyeka say *the end of term is today*; corrected to *tomorrow*.) She said it was a diary and not a threat. **Roos's reason for setting a date is the office, and the office's own timetable put the completed review on the Thursday of week 13, so the movement's climax is a diary appointment in a government office.**
- **The letter. Reference PD/13/158** (reference numbers roll weekly and 114 was a Tuesday of week 7: 121, 128, 135, 142, 149, **156**, 157, **158** — so 156 is the Tuesday of week 13, 157 the Wednesday and **158 the Thursday, which is the day Roos writes it**. Chapter 40 had it at 156, which is a Tuesday number on a Thursday letter; corrected. **Do not reuse 156 for anything raised after the Tuesday of week 13.**) The department convenes a witnessed assessment, **Tuesday of week 14, 09:00, room one of the practical building**, requirement unchanged and not the department's to alter, and — in the letter itself — **"the department records that room one is a teaching room; the department does not find that a teaching room is or is not a public room; the assessor appointed by the Continuity Office may determine the matter on the day, and the department will comply with that determination."**
- **The room problem, named in the review and again in the corridor by Rusk.** Out of term everything with a floor and a door in that building is booked to a summer course, a porter's office, or a caretaker's letter. The examination hall is contracted to the faculty and was booked in September and shaded in October by a different hand. **Room one has a strip light, a strip curtain, a whiteboard with the term dates on it, and about thirty plastic chairs that belong to a nursery.** Davan Rusk, **off the panel on a conflict of interest he created himself and disclosed in week 12, and who taught in room one for eleven years**, says one thing: *do not walk in there and look at it and decide it is fine. Make them look at it.* **Four public rooms in the district that could hold forty people belong to somebody who has already said no: the Cordage, the nine rooms above the community hall, a kitchen in an old kiosk in a former ferry waiting room, and a yard office off the Meridian with a kettle in it and a door open from six. THERE IS A FIFTH ON MAREK'S LIST THAT HE HAS NOT BEEN TO AND REFUSES TO NAME IN THAT ROOM**, because the minute he names it somebody in the building will write to them that afternoon. Hollis Pratt tells him it stays cold, and tells him to write the fifth room down somewhere and not there. **The volume therefore has a fallback room that nobody has refused and nobody has approached. If the assessor rules on Tuesday morning that room one is not a public room, it has to exist in a matter of hours, it has to cost something to open, and whoever holds it is not a panel and not a department.**

### The batch's other new people, and what they cost

- **Asha Reed, 19**, first-year on a maintenance-track course, at the Exchange since she was fifteen, has run a claim to eleven first-years in a converted common room over three days without telling Marek. **She does not defer, is unbothered by being nineteen, calls him *the third-year's problem*, makes him sit at the back wall, and says no to him in front of eleven people.** (*The fourth-year in the first pass was wrong; Marek is a third-year everywhere.*) **In the room Marek says the Sarr corridor was the seventh week and the cup of tea eleven days later, on the Friday of week 8; eleven days is the figure and it is the arithmetic, and Chapter 40's own Sarr says the door was shut *the week before*, not ten days before. Do not reduce it to ten.** Her argument, and she is right: the card is not lying, it is silent, and you cannot fix a silence by being right in a corridor. **Toby Ansell, 18**, gives the argument against her — *you are the hinge, not deliberately, that is what it is* — and **Rhoda Ilesanmi, 19**, asks the room the honest question and gets a division: the case is worth teaching and the technique is not teachable yet, and Marek is the man who has to say so.
- **The division is the batch's argument, and it does not resolve**: Asha wins the case, Marek refuses the demonstration, and the room is left with a fact and no practice, which is what everybody in the case has been left with since the Thursday of week one.
- **The Choir, again, refusing and unkind on purpose.** Marda Szabo's no is the best argument in the batch about publishing anything, and it is the argument that has been wrong twice in four years in the co-operative's own history.
- **Every refusal in the batch cost the refuser something**: Ashby gave the reason for free and lost the round's cover; Rafi's two-count happened in a kitchen and the habit does not get an exemption; Nkechi Osei refused to be rescued and is frightened of a Tuesday; Enid Sarkis put her wrist on a page in front of a stranger; Asha Reed bought her price; Corvin Ashby refused a job and lost a man on the pound; Talia Venn refused to know, and refused an office recommendation in public; Bo Halloran set a condition he knows will not be met; Darla Vint's silence at the end-of-term panel is the third time it has been counted and it is longer; and Mrs Ollerton gives Tam a card with a telephone number on it and says one of them will lose it.

### Corrections applied in review of batch 0004 (Chapters 31–40)

Recorded so that none of them is re-broken and so that nothing here reads as a fresh canon change. **No plot moved, no chapter was restarted, and no ending was altered.**

- **The end of the term is the Friday of week 12.** Chapter 32's Professor Onyeka said *the end of term is today* on the Thursday of week 12; corrected to *tomorrow*. The Thursday of week 13 is therefore six days after the end of term and out of term.
- **Talia Venn does not know the assessment date before Chapter 40.** The clause in Chapter 34 in which she calls the Tuesday of week 14 *the morning of your assessment, which I know* has been cut. There is no leak, and none is to be invented.
- **The narrow share has seven headings and three signatures**, including when it ends and how it ends. The end date is the day a person whose job that is looks at the arm, and the Friday of week 14 at the latest, whichever comes first; it is torn in half in front of him and neither half is kept.
- **Bea Nunn telephones the Spine at 11:50 on the Friday of week 12**, not 11:20, because she is reporting an ambulance call rung at 11:40. **The seventy minutes between the nurse getting to him and that call are now on the page**, said out loud by Talia Venn, written down, and signed by her.
- **Marek reads the Exchange book on the Friday of week 12 at about 11:10**, after Vance's call at about a quarter to eleven and before Bea's call at 11:50. Vance's line is line 2 and is dated the Friday. There is no figure of forty-one in the book anywhere: it is thirty-one by the Wednesday and thirty-two after Delia Frost's sheet.
- **The flood round is eleven days out when it is first named** on the Thursday of week 12, ten days out on the Friday, and nine days out by the Saturday of week 12. The figure *two days* is an error and is gone.
- **Chapter 37's morning runs in clock order** — the kitchen on Pell Street at 07:00, then the bench at the Spine at about 07:45 — and the notebook line about his own rule is forward-looking rather than a claim about acts already done.
- **Marek's retelling of the Sarr corridor is the seventh week and eleven days later the cup of tea** — the door shut on the Monday of week 7, the tea on the Friday lunchtime of week 8, which is eleven days, and which is what Chapter 36 now says. **The figure of ten was an error and is gone from the prose and from these files; a later pass must not put it back.** Chapter 40's Sarr says *the week before*, which is consistent.
- **The records request asks for the September open day in the third week of the current term**, not the term before last.
- **Delia Frost reaches Marek in one chain** — the noticeboard number, then the Exchange's book, then somebody in a market — and is routed to a clinician inside the call. The book gains a thirty-second line.
- **There is a fifth public room** on Marek's list that nobody has refused and nobody has approached, and he will not name it in a government office. It is the volume's only fallback venue.
- **Vey's refusal is a speech of some length and is the wrong reason; the figure *eleven* in older notes was the clock and not the speech**; *eleven* is the clock. **The card has been in Marek's coat since the Friday of week 11**, when Vey put it face down on the ledge outside the department office at twenty to three in the afternoon (the Thursday of week 11 is the day he opened the depot envelope, which is a different document and was a state-file conflation corrected in the second fix pass), and the telephone call is at 23:10 from a station he has been sitting in since about half past nine.
- **A form-initials convention is now fixed: Marek Senn signs a form M. S. Senn**, which is the order on the depot envelope in Chapter 26. Chapter 35's records request was corrected from *S. M. Senn*. **The same reversed order appears three times in finished batch-0003 prose (`chapter-0027.md:35,36,48`) and has been left alone; it is a known residual for a future pass and is not canon.**
- **Chapter 40's list of public rooms, the ending pull in `batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, and the Chapter 40 recap montage have been reworked** so that the volume keeps a venue and the ending prose is not six consecutive one-line paragraphs. Chapter 40's scene breaks went from seven to five.

### Corrections applied in the second fix pass of batch 0004 (Chapters 31–40)

A second, date-and-figure sweep over the finished batch after the first review pass. **No plot moved, no chapter was restarted, no ending was altered, and no canon was reversed.** Every item below is an arithmetic or attribution error, not a change of story.

- **The bus down the hill goes at six, not ten past six.** Chapter 32 had Corvin Ashby say the last bus goes at six and the one after does not go at all, and then had Marek take it at ten past six — the 06:10 ebb time had bled in. Chapter 35 and Chapter 38 both have six and are correct.
- **The gap from the Saturday of week 12 to the Monday-of-week-14 round is nine days** and was already right in Chapters 32, 33 and 34 (eleven on the Thursday, ten on the Friday, nine on the Saturday; the round is the 23rd). **Nothing was changed here.** A reviewer recomputed this as eight; the figure of nine is the correct one and must not be re-broken.
- **The Exchange book is at thirty-two lines in Chapter 40.** It is thirty-one by the Wednesday of week 13 and thirty-two once Delia Frost hands her sheet in that morning, and Chapter 40 is the Thursday. Chapter 34's *thirty-one going to be in it by the Wednesday* is a projection made on the Saturday and stands; Chapter 36's Monday count of thirty-one stands.
- **Oren Vey's card is from the Friday of week 11**, at twenty to three in the afternoon, when he put it on the ledge — Chapter 28. The figure *Thursday of week 11* was a conflation with the day Marek opened the depot envelope, and it has been corrected in Chapter 35's load-book page and in Chapter 39.
- **The document Marek is carrying in Chapter 39 is the photocopy and dated slip from Roos's office on the Wednesday of week 11**, so *a fortnight* and *the Wednesday of week 11* are correct there, not ten weeks and the Thursday.
- **Delia Frost's hip unit is ten weeks old**, not eleven: Chapter 34 has it at about nine weeks on the Saturday of week 12, three days before Chapter 38. Corrected in Chapter 38 and in the state files.
- **The flood round is four days out in Chapter 38**, which is the Wednesday of week 13; the round is the Monday of week 14.
- **Mrs Ollerton's clinic is off the Ninth Street**, where Chapter 24 puts it, with the same wall chart and the same 1988. Chapter 33's two *Crown Terrace* references are corrected. **Crown Terrace remains a real place** — Talia Venn took two statements from a woman there in Chapter 34.
- **The laundry collects on a Wednesday and a Sunday**, which is what Chapter 35 (a Sunday, at eleven) and Chapter 38 (a Wednesday, at twenty past seven) both show. The Tuesday-and-Friday version contradicted both.
- **Errol Nkemdi's line about the card is now about the week, not the date.** He read *the Friday of week fourteen* upside down and said *that's the week your Monday round is*; the earlier wording let two characters appear to state a false date as fact.
- **Ashby's hand-lettered notice says the round takes nineteen men on the Monday.** The Tuesday was in one sentence only.
- **Marek's four people in Chapter 35 name Ekow Nartey correctly** as the man who keeps the request book, and no longer list Talia Venn twice under a description that was hers.
- **The clinic's own stock is thirty-four units**, so the city figure in Chapter 35 is twenty-four in nine crates, twenty-two in four boxes and thirty-four in a clinic.
- **The narrow share is the only share in the batch, and Enid Sarkis's page now carries a date and an end.** She makes him put a date at the top and a line at the bottom saying the page is not a referral, does not go in a postbox or in the market book, and that if nobody has looked at that wrist by the end of the term then somebody is to ring her and say so in words, and that she will take the reason and not the apology.
- **The Cordage hold was placed at half past eight on the Friday of week 8**, before the day class, per Chapter 14. Chapter 40's twenty to nine is corrected. **The examination hall was booked by this department in September and shaded by the faculty's calendar in October**, which is what Chapter 19's record says; Chapter 40 had it the other way round.
- **Ines Sarr's realisation is dated to the eighth week**, which is what Chapters 36 and 40 both say, with the door shut on Marek the week before. *Four months ago* in **Chapter 29 of batch-0003** was corrected so the three chapters agree.
- **Asha Reed puts her card in the tin on the Monday night in her own kitchen**, not at the laundrette counter — Marek lives above that counter and it would have handed a first-year his address.
- **Chapter 36 says the way you do not end up the only key-holder**, not *become the hinge*. Canon separates the hinge (a technique) from the key-holder (a person), and Chapter 39 gets it right.
- **The third-year intake is in about four weeks' time** from the Monday of week 13, matching the state files' forty-one people in a hall.
- **Tam's own *six weeks* is untouched and unanchored to the operation**, as instructed. **The kitchen wages and the strap envelope are now on the page in Chapter 33**: Deb's kitchen on Sennet Row has had him off every board since the week he went in, and the brown strap envelope is still in the parts cage at the Spine under a note with his name on it, and nobody at that registrar's office knows it is there.
- **Chapter 40's clock mechanism is unchanged and is correct.** Pratt asks Roos what her position is *on the Friday of week 13* — the day after the notification deadline — and her answer is in the past tense about a lapse that happened on the Friday of week 12, which is what Chapter 32's deadline turns on. It reads as an error and is not one; do not "fix" it.
- **Talia's *an hour and a quarter* is her phrase and is correct as speech**; the underlying gap from 10:30 to 11:40 is seventy minutes, and the state files now say seventy. **Chapter 39's *half past six* for the Exchange back yard is correct** — it matches the 06:35 consent stamp in Chapter 24, and the ten past six there is the yard, not the sheet.
- **The night list in Chapter 39 is eight jobs**, not nine: six done and two out of area, which is what Sera's exchange requires.
- **Chapter 33's load-book line is *one Friday*,** written on the Friday of week 12 about a box he carried that morning.

---

## Verified in-world timeline of Chapters 41–50 (Movement V, weeks 13–15)

Use this before assigning any new date. Weeks run Monday to Sunday. **The only calendar date fixed anywhere in this volume is that the twenty-eighth is a Wednesday of week 10**, and the derivation is **week 10 = Mon 26 – Sun 1, week 11 = Mon 2 – Sun 8, week 12 = Mon 9 – Sun 15, week 13 = Mon 16 – Sun 22, week 14 = Mon 23 – Sun 29, week 15 = Mon 30 – Sun 6.** No month name appears anywhere in Movement V's prose except as a **season word** (*the autumn*, *in the spring*) and as the **September open day** and the **September intake**, which are canon nouns. **The term ended on the Friday of week 12**, so the whole of Movement V is out of term, and the corridors are lit all day and a room at four in the afternoon has *the light gone*. **Nobody says *the middle of a winter*** — that phrase was cut in review of Chapter 38 and must not return.

- **Fri wk 13, 07:00–17:00** — Ch 41. Six day jobs inside the district, including a fitting in Crown Terrace that takes him forty minutes and is right the first time. The department's headed sheet with *A public release on Tuesday / What has to be true / Four people, and the four of them have never been in a room together*. The grey card out of the fourth pocket and back in with a six-line parts tag on top of it. **The Exchange at ten past one; Dessa Kwan's one-word refusal.** Enid Sarkis on the telephone at ten past four. **The fifth room found on the terminal in the office at the Spine at half past five: Fell Road branch library, reading room, first floor, sixty, £14 the morning, £20 deposit, a box for *name of person responsible* plus address and telephone number, purpose in writing, no bookings by telephone.** The page is torn out and folded into the back of the load book. **It is still folded in the back of the load book at the end of the volume and has never been approached.**
- **Sat and Sun wk 13** — Ch 42. The nine-item plan written on the department's headed paper at about half past six on the Saturday evening at the kitchen table with the dryers going, and copied four times at the copy room after four, item three left blank on all four. **Rafi: Saturday morning at Pell Street, eleven pounds.** **Tam: about ten on the Saturday night, on the end of the bath, three changes, and the line in biro on the blank side of his own plan above the columns — COLUMN TWO IS WRONG … AND I AM NOT TELLING ANYBODY FOR A FORTNIGHT.** **Dessa: eleven on the Sunday morning at the counter of the shop, after checking she had not been brought to anybody.** **Reuben: half past two on the Sunday afternoon in the back of the Exchange, and he reads his three conditions out himself in the front of it at ten past four with the shutters up and about thirty people in the room.** Talia telephones at about ten past two on the Saturday afternoon. Asha Reed gives Toby Ansell's **name and not his number** on the Saturday evening.
- **Mon wk 14, 05:50–23:20** — Ch 43. The round: the van on the pull-off at ten to six, **ebb at ten past six**, Cutbank to the upper pound, **nineteen men** with Ashby saying ten, the bar, the two treads of the steps, the pound. **The knee on the flatbed at about twenty past seven** (Roy Ancrum, fifty-six) written on DPS/FS/4 as *unidentified*. **The bar goes off the lip at about twenty past eight** and Nkechi Osei goes down on the wet concrete; the whole event is about ninety seconds and eleven men see it. She gives him the second half at about half past nine. He is off the hill at about eleven, writes it on the Monday list at ten past eleven, does four of six, and **leaves two off and does not write them, and on the Wednesday of week 14 goes back to the list and writes both at the bottom with the time and his initials, which mean *not done*.** The load book at about ten past ten, items 55–57, and the three fault-form things at about two in the morning.
- **Tue wk 14, 08:10 – 12:05** — Ch 44–47. The door at ten past eight; the assessor in at five to nine; **witnesses at 08:00, 08:10, 08:15, 08:20, 08:40** (Rhoda and Toby in from twenty past seven); **Darla Vint turns the key at 09:00 and leaves the ring on the fire door shelf**; **Hollis Pratt's determination is made at about five to nine**, in the room, out loud; **the window opens at 42 minutes past nine and closes 1 minute 41 seconds later**; Rusk's word at **21 minutes past nine**, eleven minutes and more before the window; **Sera Quill's four seconds in the aisle at about 23 minutes**; the frame off the wall in about ninety seconds and into the corridor; **the panel rises at four minutes to twelve and reads the determination in the corridor**; **Tam's own release at eleven minutes past ten**; the plate out at twenty past ten and sealed by 10:20; Rhoda Ilesanmi in the corridor with a cold pack at about eleven minutes past ten; **Talia Venn comes up the stairs at twenty past eleven**, having been in Bower Street since ten.
- **Wed wk 14, 06:50–17:20** — Ch 48. In at ten to seven; the day book; **Roisin Keogh rings at twenty past seven**; the practice standards office over a pharmacy in Lattice Ward from about half past seven to half past nine, **with the return in Rhoda Ilesanmi's own words on the telephone and the four seconds in his, and Roisin Keogh telling him she will telephone the reading woman on the Friday**; **Petar Vance rings at a quarter to eleven**; the Exchange's book at thirty-four lines by about one o'clock, **with the R. Ancrum line of the Tuesday and the D. Kwan line of the Wednesday**; Asha Reed reads the first column at ten past one; **Corvin Ashby rings at about ten past four**; **Darla Vint comes into the practical building at about four**; Asha Reed at the counter at about twenty to five; room one at ten past five.
- **Thu wk 14, 09:20–13:00** — Ch 49. Darla Vint in the corridor at twenty-five to ten; **Rusk heard at nine o'clock and four minutes**; the department's part; the panel rises at twenty past eleven and reads out for forty minutes; **the corridor at twenty to one**. **The trust's letter is dated the Wednesday of week 14** and its condition three therefore has its tenth working day on **the Wednesday of week 16**.
- **Thu wk 14, ~13:10–16:00; Fri wk 14, 09:30–16:00; Sat wk 14, 11:30–18:00; Mon wk 15, 07:00–19:30** — Ch 50. The pull-off at ten past one on the Thursday. Three papers on the bench on the Friday — **the department's record (Darla Vint, half past nine), the Choir's proof (in the post, the Friday morning), the Quiet House notice (through the letterbox at ten past one)** — and the job list already on the wall since the Wednesday. Sera at about four on the Friday. **Talia's van in the lay-by outside the wholesale kitchens at about four on the Saturday, for about an hour and ten minutes, with the light gone.** **Dessa at the counter at half past eleven on the Saturday, Rafi at one, Reuben at about half past two, Tam at about half past five.** The copy of the card on the **Sunday evening**. **The ledger opens at nine on the Monday of week 15 and nobody comes**; the boy is at the bench at about half past seven that Monday morning while Marek is in the car park; Nkechi Osei at about ten past ten, which is about forty minutes after she came off the flatbed at the upper pound; the page written between about two and half past six in the afternoon; the ledger shut at about half past seven.

## Canon now fixed in Chapter prose (Chapters 41–50)

### The plan, the four consents, and the card

- **The plan is nine items and is not a technique.** *THIS IS NOT A TECHNIQUE. THERE IS NO NEW PRACTICE IN IT* is written in capitals at the top of the sheet. Item four is the load-bearing one — **nobody is asked to follow anybody, to time anything to anybody, to be listened to, to be looked at in order to find out whether it was good, to say a word that somebody else supplies, or to be brave** — and item nine is that the candidate does not see what any of them does before the Tuesday. **Item eight is the two-person line and it is the only part he could not write down before the day.** Item three is left blank on all four copies because it is theirs.
