- **Four separate written things, four people, one person one thing at a time, each of the four may end its own at any moment by word.** **Dessa Kwan's condition is anybody coming into the two feet behind her during a pass.** **Rafi's condition is nobody watching him to find out whether his step is a step, and his written line is that he does what he does with a bag.** **Reuben's three conditions are read out by him in the front of the Exchange and the name of the college comes out and Marek's does not.** **Tam's three changes are that the two of them who can stop it are the other four and not all five, that nothing of his touches their equipment, and that the cards go in the bin at the door.**
- **The disposal of the five consents is agreed on paper and never dramatised.** It is in two places and both are before the Tuesday: Tam's third change in Chapter 42, and item seven of the plan, that after the panel rises, or by eleven o'clock that night, whichever is first, the five cards are torn up in front of the five of them and there is no copy. **The word *cards* occurs in no chapter after Chapter 42, no room is ever named for the tearing, and nothing in Chapters 45, 46 or 47 shows it happening. Anything that depends on the consents having been destroyed is depending on a plan, not on an event.** (One thing inside the plan is not resolved: item two says four cards, one name on each, and item seven tears up five in front of five people. The fifth is presumably the boy's own and nobody in the volume says so.) What is on the page at the close is the consequence: Chapter 49's seventh heading says the share's sheet is torn in half in front of the four of them and neither half is kept, and **Chapter 50's ledger page is in Marek's own hand — the small upright hand he writes tags in — with the boy's handwriting showing in the card copy gummed in at the top of it, and three names against a date and not three signatures. No copy of any of the four consents exists anywhere in this city.**
- **The four consents expire at the door of the building on the Tuesday afternoon** — the plan's own term, not a scene. Anything in Chapter 50 that needs a person's name has to be asked for again, and is.
- **The grey card.** Chapter 41 considers it as the thing that would make two practices comparable and refuses it: *A key is a thing you are given. I would be making one.* He writes six lines on a parts tag and puts the tag in the fourth pocket on top of the card. **In Chapter 50 he copies it by hand, leaves the file scratch across the second beat in place because it is the only mark on it he knows was made on purpose by somebody, and fills the fifth line. He fills it wrongly first, in his own hand, with a full stop at the end of the third sentence, and the boy makes him leave the full stop off, and nobody will ever know that either. The original goes back into the fourth pocket under four spare-parts tags with the fifth line blank.** The card is still a tool and is still not explained.
- **The fifth room — Fell Road branch library — is still shut.** It is named to Talia Venn in a lay-by on the Saturday of week 14 and not in any government office, and she shuts it in one word: *Then it is shut.* Her reason is **not the fourteen pounds** — if it is fourteen pounds, somebody finds four hundred pounds in four years and opens it, and that is how a rule turns into a price. **The cost is a name, an address and a telephone number in ink in a public ledger on a shelf in a public building, and nobody who comes in can be stopped from reading the booking sheet.** It is a live Volume 02 question: **who takes the name out for the two thousand people who will sit in rooms that cost them one.**
- **The five tabulated things in Chapter 50**, in the volume's list order: a narrow Working Share with four people who can end it; **a boy who finished his own release and still cannot hold a kettle**; **four sites, one of them a mobile clinic and not a building, each still holding its own stock in the place the hold put it, all four having been offered a recommendation and all four having refused it;** a supply chain with a blank line in it; and a maintenance ledger in a repair shop with three names on a page and a review date on it.

### The room, and the determination

- **Room one is a teaching room and the department has said in writing that it does not find that a teaching room is or is not a public room.** Hollis Pratt determines it **on the state of the room, and the state of the room is a door and a key**: from nine until the panel rises the door is unlocked and stays unlocked; the corridor outside is not rostered and no member of staff may ask anybody what they are there for; anybody who comes through the door stays and their name goes in no list and this office will not make, keep or ask for a record of who was in the room. **He asks Professor Onyeka to minute that the effect of the third determination is that the district has created a public room in a teaching establishment for the length of a morning in order to hear a demonstration, and that this is a thing nobody in the district should ever have to do twice.** He decides it out loud, in the room, in front of the candidate, and says the candidate may stop him and is not stopped. **Ena Truslove**, of the Fold nursery, walks in at nine o'clock with a hand truck for thirty plastic chairs that are not the department's, and is the reason the room is public, and asks to stay.
- **The whiteboard's circled date is not rubbed off**, at about ten past five on the Wednesday afternoon, and the reason is given in the prose: *a room which says what it is for is the only kind of room anybody in this city can be shown.* **The board is bare after the Tuesday** — frame, perspex, bag of mass and rack all gone, the laminated card still clipped to the top of the plywood with two bulldog clips, the bell still on its bracket, and the frame's shadow still on the wall. **The chair is in a corridor and not a room, and the room is a room again at four minutes to twelve.**

### The four seconds, and the failure

- **Rusk's word is *scope*, at 21 minutes past nine, in the teaching shape, at the laminated card's pitch, with the small fall at the end, and he did not decide to say it and did not address it to the candidate.** He is a member of the teaching staff, **not a member of the panel**, off the panel on a conflict he created himself and disclosed in week 12, and **the chair has ruled that a member of a panel cannot be heard by that panel on a matter, and that where a seat is vacant and the member is alive and has asked to be heard he may be heard as a witness, and the panel may not sit with him in the making of a finding.**
- **Four people follow it, and they are named in the prose and not in the determination**: Ruslan Petric, 23, Group C, the second candidate on the Tuesday; two third-year note-takers, a girl of twenty and a boy of nineteen; and **Tam Arrick, who asked to be named in the determination and was not named.** **Dessa Kwan counted them herself in about two seconds and said four, and that two of the four had been in her own group at the Institute two years ago, and that it was the first time in her life she had seen a thing move through a room the way a smell moves.**
- **Sera Quill's call-out is about four seconds and is physical, and it works.** She names positions, not feelings: *take your hand off the chair and put it flat on the wall and stand there. I'm not telling you to be anywhere. I'm telling you where you are, because four of you are standing in the shape of a person who has been told a load is coming and there is no load, and I have watched four men get hurt this year by nobody being in a position to say there is no load, and I am in a position to say it and I am saying it.* **Nobody argues.** She then takes the fold-down frame off the wall in about ninety seconds, the bag of practice mass onto a trolley, and the whole rig into a corridor, and leaves it there until somebody in a position to write it down says she may put it back.
- **The rail is the front edge of the demonstrator frame, at knee height, on a steel bar, bolted into the Spine's board, and it comes across about four inches because the folded return contact behind the perspex had already failed once at 09:09 and nobody had been told.** It catches **Rhoda Ilesanmi, 19, first year, maintenance track**, on the inside of the left forearm from the wrist to about four inches below the elbow, which is about two hundred and thirty millimetres, and the wound itself is **about four centimetres of opened skin along the inside, from about two fingers above the wrist up**. **Both measurements are Chapter 46's own and they are two different things: the span the rail crossed her arm over, and the length of the cut. Do not collapse one into the other, and do not put the cut below the elbow.** **She sees it coming and gets her arm round about a second after it does.** Four stitches and a review in nine days. **Toby Ansell gets the notebook out from under the rail with two fingers and pulls her forearm along it in one movement.** The isolator goes over eleven seconds after the rail, and **somebody was counting and it was not Marek Senn.**
- **The four seconds are the time it took a room to stop listening to a rig and start listening to a man, and in all four of them he could not get a word into the air in a shape that would move anybody, and he was in a two-person line holding a release and the line was not there.** He cannot stop the practice because **stopping it was the third beat, and the third beat is the practitioner returning the next decision to the subject of the brace, and there is no brace, and the only thing there was to return the next decision to was himself, and he was not there — he had been on a board four feet away watching a rail go across a girl, which is the one thing he is actually good at, which is the thing that has ruined every room he has ever stood in.**
- **The line had gone slack and had not been dropped.** The far end was still held, on purpose, by a boy of twenty who had been asked to and had decided in front of a panel not to be the thing that let go. A voice came along the line saying *I am here* and **it would have worked, and Marek took it out of his mouth like a splinter.** He finishes it slower, with less, without accepting a voice. **The window ran 1 minute 41 seconds.**
- **The determination, read out in the corridor in the open at four minutes to twelve, on three words and not one**: **used — not used; followed — not followed within the window; accepted — not accepted.** The refusal of the two words is minuted as **the clearest evidence in the morning that no substituted cue was accepted**, and the chair records that **it would have been easier and better for the candidate to have accepted them.** The four seconds and the injury are recorded; **the candidate's own account is appended and is not part of the panel's findings**; and the equipment failure is **a folded copper return contact, the plate removed, sealed and bagged with a service number, held by the maintenance contractor, isolator locked off at ten seconds after the event.**
- **The plate is bagged in a corridor by a repairer with a puller and goes into a locked tray in Civic Spine 7 under service number 7-4-112, in Sera Quill's cage, and not into a department's store.** The folded copper comes away **whole, and in the shape of the load path, about four inches long and thinner than a nail head at one end.** The bag is labelled in chinagraph and **not read out**, because a service number, a wall, a shelf, a rack, a room, a time and a name is the entire system. **The strap envelope with Tam's name on it is in the tray next to it and has been since the Tuesday morning**, and Sera Quill has said she will hand over either object and say what is in the other tray.
- **Tam Arrick's own release is at eleven minutes past ten, outside the window, on the floor of room one, on his own feet, with nothing in either hand, in about eleven seconds**, in four sentences: *It was on me. It isn't on me now. Nothing about it stays. The next one is mine.* **Nobody in the room used a name, and the witness had said out loud the day before that it would not be one.** The panel records the room, the witnesses, that the performance **was not a practice**, and that the panel has no power in respect of Mr Arrick, **and the district woman of fifty asks that it be minuted that two members thought the opposite.**

### The decision, Chapter 49, in the form it must keep

- **The term practice-variance panel is not a board and is not an examination board.** It sat with three of four, Rusk's chair pushed in and nothing on it, and the chair said out loud that she had no power to fill a seat in eleven days and had tried.
- **The transcript line stands and is not amended**: *not completed. Fourth beat absent. Performance dependent on external cue.* **The Solo Seal is not granted, the panel has no power to grant it, the department asked for nothing, and the chair records that he did not ask for it in the room.**
- **The requirement of the ninth week is satisfied and that is a finding about an event and not a licence.**
- **The Working Share is issued by the department, not by the panel, and it runs on seven headings and does not use the word *scope*.** Purpose; duration (one term, expiring at five o'clock on the last Friday of the coming term, no provision for extension, any extension is a fresh matter); what stays (nothing — no echo, no trace, no retention, no derivation); who may stop it (**any one of the four named partners, and the partner whose own practice it is, and the holder, by word, at any point, without a reason asked and none recorded, and a stoppage is recorded as a stoppage and not as a variance**); who it is for (**Tam Arrick, Rafi Pell, Dessa Kwan and Reuben Daw, and nobody else, and the four of them were not named by the holder and were named by themselves and one of them in his own hand on a sheet he wrote himself**); when it ends; how it ends (the sheet torn in half in front of the four of them, neither half kept). **A separate fourth condition, signed by the deputy registrar and not by the department: the holder may not hold a practice alone on any equipment owned or held by the department, on any premises of the department, or under the department's insurance, at any time in this term, and the prohibition is not limited to Institute equipment and is not limited to hours.**
- **The four refusals are read into the minute in four sets of words** and are in the state files and Chapter 49 and are not to be paraphrased differently. **The chair's own summary is that the recommendation's condition is satisfied and four people have refused the recommendation in the same room, and that this is the most accurate description of this case the panel has read in thirteen weeks, and that the panel does not know what to do with it and is not going to pretend to.**
- **The licensor field is blank in the exhibit and blank in the minute.** 4,106 registered bundles, 940 blanks, 211 revised at least once since registration, counted with a pencil. **Four assessors.** The Continuity Office still declines to trace it, in writing, and that refusal is a fact of the volume. (The 211 is Chapter 19 in words, not digits. Checked in the review pass of batch 0006 and confirmed; it stays.)
- **The trust's six conditions** are read out in full before Anders Kolt says one thing about them, and Marek **objects to none and minutes the price of the fifth**: *in about six weeks a department in this city is going to be able to telephone him about a boundary release, and he will have to say no or he will have to say something, and he will not be able to tell whether they already know about him.* Kolt's sentence is that **condition three is not a punishment and is the only thing in the list that is a piece of work anybody in this city can do.** **The records request is not filed in this volume.** A woman from the trust told him in the corridor on the Thursday what was going to happen about it, and he has still not asked a single person what a failed condition does, and has written that down.

### Three accounts, and the ledger

- **The Institute's record of the Tuesday morning is four items and a reference number, and Rhoda Ilesanmi's name is not in it and nobody had to decide that.** It is the only one of the three that anybody will still be able to rely on in nine years, and **Darla Vint says so and will not be thanked.** A fifth item exists on the department's copy only: the chair's determination, made on the Friday, that the panel sat in a room which is not a teaching room.
- **The Choir of Practice's proof goes to press on the Friday of week 14 and goes to nine hundred addresses, and it contains: the origin, 2016, Nacre Institute methods group, the group did not record who wrote it; the revisions 3-08, 3-11, 4-02, 4-19; TRC-419-88 with three paragraphs of stated reason and no name; a ruled box headed UPSTREAM LICENSOR with nothing in it, ruled by M. Szabo in the fifth week, with her initials under it, and a printed request that readers treat it as a hole in the document; the four lines of the instructor script; the 2016 methods-group note; the fact that the manual is still 3-08; the service bulletin number; a four-sentence account of the Tuesday; the four refusals; the department's five conditions and the trust's six; the co-operative's own refusal of A. Reed and the shoebox vote of eight to three with the three noes named and the member described and not named; and, under *requests received and upheld*, M. Senn's own request with his own name and his own ground, and R. Ilesanmi's own words, printed because she asked for it in.**
- **THE BLANK LINE HAS PROPAGATED. The upstream licensor field that is blank on the bundle label is now blank in a ruled box in a co-operative's quarterly, drawn by a records clerk in plain language, and printed, because the co-operative will not invent it and will not print a guess. This is the furthest anybody in the case has got with the field, and it is further than the department got in four years, and it is not an answer.**
- **The Quiet House's account is one side, plain typeface, no logo, no name, no return address, three copies folded in three through a letterbox that carries a sign saying the shop does not take post.** It says the Institute's record of its own room is not a record of the room, **lists the five things the four items do not contain**, holds the assessor to his own sentence about not doing it twice, and **offers any person who was in that room a way out of a record with no form on it, given only an address to send the answer to** — a box at the post office on Weaver Street, and a line that a box is not a membership. **No Quiet House member is on stage in Volume 01, and Leo Marr has not appeared.**
- **The three accounts do not match, and none of the three is wrong, and there is no office in this city whose job it is to reconcile them.** That is stated in the prose and it is the chapter's argument.
- **The maintenance ledger is the trust's condition one: open to the department and to the trust, weekly, on a Monday at nine, for the term.** It is a shop's book with the shop's name on the front and **S. Quill, lead repairer** on the inside of the front cover. **It opened on the Monday of week 15 and nobody came**, and the department asked for a photograph of the first page and said it would come and get it herself. **A thing that is open one morning a week to a department and a trust and has one reader in it is not an institution, and he is not going to be the one who calls it one in the middle of a term.**
- **The job list is the trust's condition two**, in a plastic sleeve on the wall, *open to any member of the public, including work the holder does not do*, four lines, one of them with his initials at the bottom, **and the initials mean not done and everybody who has ever worked that bench knows it.**
- **RULE 1, in the ledger, is copied from Bea Nunn's board at the Exchange and the credit is on it.** Any person named in the book may ask for their whole row to be taken out, not just the name, and the name of the person who took the call, or wrote the line, or was in the room, stays in. **The line under it is the only line on the page that was entirely for him: Rule 1 does not stop this. Nothing on this page stops a fella saying something in a doorway, and there is no document in this city that stops a fella saying something in a doorway, and there is not going to be one, and everything else in this book is what is left.** This is the volume's last discovery and the handoff: **a rule that governs a page cannot govern a mouth.**
- **THE BACK WALL.** The bench at Civic Spine 7 is against the long wall, so a man standing at it has his back to the wall and his face to the door, which is the wrong way round for a man who counts exits. **It is the first time in the volume that the back wall and the work have been the same place, and he notices it at about seven o'clock on a Monday evening and does not write it down, because there is nowhere to write it that is not the ledger and the ledger is not for that.**
- **SERA QUILL'S MAINTENANCE WORK, as a transaction and not a reconciliation.** The department's demonstration equipment and the contractor's obligations on it came off the department's list on the Wednesday of week 14 and are hers for the term, with her name on both, and she asked for nothing and got nothing and wants that on paper. **She has taken custody of a cage and has lifted nothing**, and the word *quarantine* is one she will read back. **Five places in this city hold 4-19 stock and there are four holds, and the fifth hold is a padlock and a woman's word, and she says out loud that that is not good enough and has written it down anyway.** The label on the relay cabinet is printed and says **KIT 4-19 — DO NOT REISSUE**, with no name and no date on it, and it is not coming off until somebody gives her a reason in writing that she can read out loud. The four held lines are in one tray in a sleeve with no name and no date, **because a date would make it a museum and not a cage in four years.** Her warning goes into the margin of the first page **on the back of a form, gummed in, facing outwards**, because there is no room on the front and she is not a woman who writes in a gap in another man's page. **She has not said the name and she will not, and she has refused nothing new because she was asked for nothing.**

### What Chapter 50 leaves open, and it is most of it

- **The September register and the records request are not in a postbox.** Ten working days from the trust's letter of the Wednesday of week 14, which is the **Wednesday of week 16**. The request and Enid Sarkis's page are in the same drawer under a battery and a roll of solder. **Enid Sarkis's end-of-term line has been discharged and it changed nothing: he rang on the Friday of week 13, seven days after the date, gave the reason, and was not offered an apology, and nobody has looked at the wrist and nobody is going to, and he has not been up those stairs since the Sunday of week 12.** He has not asked what a failed condition does. **Nobody's job is still nobody's job.**
- **The second dependence is not cured.** Nkechi Osei came into the shop on the Monday of week 15 on her own time, uninvited, having heard about the list on the wall in four days, looked at the ledger, and **said it is not the thing she asked for, because there is nobody in it who can say no — there is a man behind the counter and he can say no and he says it all day, and if that were the same then every kitchen in this city with a man in it would be the thing she asked for.** She is on four, there are about nine rounds left before the year's out, and there is not one person in this city's job. **She also told him that a list on a wall reached Fell Road in four days where a number on a board would not have reached her in four years, and that one day one of these is going to be a place, and when it is a place it will be a place she has to come to, and she will come, and she will be the fourth thing in it.**
- **The five copies of 4-19 are not a policy and the padlock is not an institution.** Sera says so.
- **Reuben Daw's Saturday rate came off on the Wednesday of week 14** because a foreman said the word college in a doorway to four other men, one of whom said *since when* and ended it. **He is not angry at Marek and says so, and that he is not angry is the point.** He says the record reached him through a mouth, not a page, and that he cannot see how anybody writes a rule against that.
- **The fifth copy of SB-4-19-03 is gone.** It came off the second shelf of the Threadline depot on the Tuesday of week 14 on a stock docket signed in a hand that is not a driver's and not Vance's and not a depot hand, the fifth line in the van book now has that signature on it, the manifests were recycled the week before, and Vance's own new fact is that **two men have now come into that building in six weeks unaccompanied and the visitor column says the same four words both times, and he has had a service engineer on the district for eleven years and has never seen it say that twice.**
- **Asha Reed's price is half paid.** She reads the first column of the Exchange's book on a Wednesday by Bea Nunn's rule, which is hers and not a rule, and Marda Szabo is going to print that a first-year did it with a chair and a rule and no money out of a tin. **Her sentence is still to be said in a first-year common room on the first Wednesday of the month, and there is one voice in that room and it is hers, and she said on the Wednesday of week 14 that she does not know how to fix it and does not know whether it is fixable.**
- **Nell Senn does not appear in Movement V and is not written about.** The silence is unresolved and the count in the state files stands. **She is an ordinary sixteen-year-old and is not a node, an inheritance, or a device.**
- **Talia Venn's request for her own pages has a reference number and is answered in about a fortnight** — two thirds of what is asked for is other people's and she will not produce it. **The fifth room is shut and who takes the name out is unassigned.** **Thursdays, and if anybody asks, *we had an hour*, and nothing is written down anywhere.**
- **The Institute's manual is still 3-08 and Rusk still requisitioned the reprint himself in March.** Nothing in this volume repairs the paperwork.
- **No System panel appears anywhere in Movement V.** The interface has spoken **three** times in fifty chapters, in **Chapters 3, 7 and 17**, and has never once helped. (Corrected in review of batch 0005: Chapters 3 and 7 only had left the Chapter 17 panel — `> Three sources agree. Nothing is missing.` — out of the count. The panel marker `* * *` is used for nothing else anywhere in the manuscript.)

## Corrections applied in the review pass of batch 0006 (the volume close, Chapters 1–50)

A sweep over the volume-close document and these files after the close was written, and then a second verification pass over the sweep itself. **No plot moved, no chapter was restarted, no ending was altered, and no canon was reversed.** Every item is an arithmetic, an attribution, or a claim about what is on the page. The reason for the sweep is narrow and it is the whole value of `state/`: a number in a state file that is not in a chapter is a number a Volume 02 writer will put on stage.

- **Chapter 13's collapse is at four minutes to nine.** The 09:15 was in this file's timeline line and in the volume-close document and in no chapter. **The 06:45 at the start of the old range was in no chapter either.** The times the case turns on in Chapter 13 are the 07:10 arrival in the yard, the four minutes to nine, 09:26 and 10:05; the chapter also has the clinic opening at eight and a cold store at five in the morning, which are scenery and are not to be promoted.
- **The count of 4,106, 940 and 211 revised is the prose's own and was checked and left standing.** It is Chapter 19, spoken by Hollis Pratt, counted on a Sunday in the register with a pencil. **The 211 is written in words — *two hundred and eleven* — and not in digits**, so a search of the manuscript for the figure returns nothing, a reviewer reported it as a figure that existed only in the planning notes, and it was very nearly struck out of five files. It is in the chapter. It is Chapter 19 line 85. **Nothing has been removed from the count, and nothing should be.**
- **The five consents are disposed of in the plan, not on the stage.** Tam's third change in Chapter 42 and item seven of the plan both say the cards go; the word *cards* is in no chapter after Chapter 42 and no room is named; the tearing is not dramatised. **Anything in these files that said the five cards were torn up at the door of the building has been corrected to say that it was agreed in writing and never shown.** One thing inside the plan is still unresolved and is flagged rather than fixed: item two says four cards, one name on each, and item seven tears up five in front of five people.
- **Chapter 16 times a later attempt before an earlier one and this cannot be fixed here.** The success is *07:08* in the delivery docket in Tam Arrick's own pocket and the failed attempt at the strap is *four minutes past seven* in the same chapter and in Marek's load-book page, narrated after the success. **Treat 07:08 as the success and the strap attempt as after it.** The chapter is finished and reviewed; the repair is one time in two places and belongs to a later prose pass. **Chapter 16 is Movement II and is only ever listed under the Movement III technique arc because the arc runs through it.**
- **Oren Vey is on stage in person in Chapters 27, 28 and 29** — the depot corridor, the ledge outside the department office, and the back of the Exchange on the Monday of week 12 with about forty people in it — **and on a telephone in Chapter 39 at 23:10 from a station.** Any note saying he is off stage for the rest of the volume is wrong; what is true is that after Chapter 29 he appears once, on a telephone, and that call is the one that offers the job at the start of next term.
- **The load book has no total in weeks of its own.** The prose's figure for the run in **Volume 01** is **thirteen weeks**, and it is the length of the term rather than the age of the book; it appears in Chapters 41, 42, 44, 45, 47, 48, 49 and 50. The book is dated from **the Thursday of week 1**, when it came into the flat above the laundrette, and it is open at the Monday of week 15. **No chapter or state file gives it fourteen weeks inside Volume 01, and none should. Volume 02 deliberately moves to fourteen from Chapter 51 onward — see the note below — and the two figures are not a contradiction.**
- **The phrase *last approved civic sequence* is in Chapters 2, 4 and 7 and nowhere else in the fifty.** The last appearance is Chapter 7. Chapter 39 returns to the Marrow Falls page and its 04:20 row without the phrase, and must not be cited as an appearance of it.
- **The fortnight in Tam Arrick's line at the end of Chapter 42 runs to the Saturday of week 15**, which is **nine days** after the Thursday of week 14. **The count of nine is the same on either reading of the derived calendar**, and the calendar itself is not self-consistent about the length of the month it assumes, so the day-of-month is 4 or 5 and the gap is nine either way. **Chapter 42's closing sentence still says the term ended on the Thursday of week 14 with four days still to run on it, and that sentence is wrong on two counts** — the term ended on the Friday of week 12, and the fortnight expires on the Saturday of week 15. It is flagged in `workspace/volume-01/batch-0006/VOLUME-CLOSE.md` and not repaired, because the close batch writes no prose. **Every date in the hand-off table of the volume close is the one in this file; where the two ever disagree, this file is right and the chapter is not.**
- **Rhoda Ilesanmi, 19, first year, maintenance track, is named** in Chapters 36, 44, 46, 47 and 48, and appears in Chapter 50 as *R. Ilesanmi*. Any hand-off or state line that says only *a first-year's forearm* should carry her name, because the quarterly printed her own words at her own request and her name is the point of that print.
- **Two measurements of the arm, both the chapter's, and they are not interchangeable.** The rail comes across **about four inches** and catches the inside of the left forearm **from the wrist to about four inches below the elbow**, which is about two hundred and thirty millimetres; the wound is **about four centimetres of opened skin along the inside, from about two fingers above the wrist up**. A version of the rail line in this file gave only the four inches and lost the wound; both are now carried and neither is to be dropped.
- **The ledger page in Chapter 50 is in Marek's own hand**, the small upright hand he writes tags in, **with the boy's handwriting showing in the card copy gummed in at the top of it**, and three names against a date and not three signatures. The phrase *a man's handwriting* was doing the job and was loose about whose hand is on the page.
- **The honest ledger of Chapter 18 runs 00:20 to 02:30 on the Thursday of week 9**, not from 01:30. Chapter 25 has a different page, begun at a quarter to ten on the Wednesday of week 11 and finished at ten past midnight, and it also has four headings and fifteen numbered lines. **There are four load-book pages in the volume with four headings, two of them with fifteen numbered lines, and they are not to be merged.**

# VOLUME 02 — THREADLINE

## Verified in-world timeline of Chapters 51–60 (Movement I, weeks 15–16)

**Weeks run Monday to Sunday. Week 15 = Mon 30 – Sun 6. Week 16 = Mon 7 – Sun 13.** The term ended on the Friday of week 12, so the whole of Movement I is out of term: the Institute's corridors are lit all day and a room at four in the afternoon has the light gone. **No month-name and no month-date appears in the batch, and no year appears anywhere in it.**

- **Tue wk 15, morning, Cutter Street, then the bench** — Ch 51. Kolt's office is on the first floor of a building on Cutter Street with a brass plate and a working lift. **The trust's money is a bench slot, two days a week at the shop rate, £480 across twelve weeks, stopping at the end of the term whether or not condition three is met.** The six conditions are quoted **exactly** as Chapter 49 read them, including *and sends a copy of the filing reference to the district practice standards office* on condition three. **Load book 56 and 57, at the bench that night, door open, three lines and no headings.** Then **Ruslan Petric posts one hand-written sheet at the post office on Weaver Street on the Tuesday afternoon** and tells Asha Reed on the Saturday, not Marek.
- **Wed wk 15, 08:20 and 11:30 and about 14:30** — Ch 52. Vance's call, Rafi at the shop with a district training pack dated the twelfth, and Marek at the depot counter at about half past two. **Load book 58, written later the same evening and marked as not the same evening as the rest of it.**
- **Thu wk 15, about 11:00 and about 15:00** — Ch 53. The notice goes up on the board outside the practical department office. Vint at about half past nine in the copy room; Roos in the corridor by the copy room for nineteen minutes of a twenty-minute gap. **The briefing is on the Friday of week 15 at half past eleven and runs about two and a half hours, with eleven people in the room.** **No load-book entry for either day.**
- **Fri wk 15, about 10:20 and 13:10 and 22:00** — Ch 54. Talia's answer is given in the first-floor room in Bower Street in the morning; the telephone call is at **twenty past one**; the load-book page is at **ten o'clock that night** and is a full four-heading page, the longest since the Wednesday of week 13. **Entry 59.**
- **Sat wk 15, 10:30–12:30, and about 18:30, and about 20:20** — Ch 55. The drop-in runs **two hours** and **the back room is forty at every count in the chapter**, with the woman at the door saying *forty-one, one left* off a list, and **thirty-eight certificates are typed on the day** and a woman at a typewriter types them, and her name is called out to the room as well. **Dessa Kwan says on the telephone in Chapter 57 that forty-one people were in that room on the Saturday morning, and it is the same room with the late arrival, and no state file may call the headcount anything but forty.** **Kohl is on the yard on the previous Saturday and the pack is dated the twelfth of the month — the week before the Tuesday of week 14.** Dessa Kwan at the shop counter at about half past six. **Entry 60, written sitting on a wall off the Weir road at about ten past eight.**
- **Sun wk 15, about 10:10 and about 11:00, and 08:10 on the Thursday before** — Ch 56. Reuben telephones at ten past ten; the yard is off the Meridian Corridor past the grain terminal; the blue card is on the wall of a prefab yard office by the window. **Reuben's line goes into the Exchange's four-column book on the Tuesday morning of week 16 at about ten past eight, in the back room, with Bea Nunn in the chair and no meeting, and it is line 38.**
- **Mon wk 16, 08:50, 11:00, 11:20, 12:10 and 14:30** — Ch 57. The ledger is on the bench at **ten to nine**; Dessa Kwan is at the bench at about eleven; the row is struck through at **11:20**; Bea Nunn is telephoned at **ten past twelve**; the three calls are made at **half past two** and take **eleven minutes**. **No load-book entry; the date of the calls goes on the inside cover under the line about the first reading.**
- **Tue wk 16, about 16:20, and 22:10** — Ch 58. Vey in the shop. **The intake instruction went out in the week of week 8; eleven suppliers, four refused to send anything at all, seven came back within a fortnight, four completed the field and three are blank. The offer is filed as an offer held open and the holder of the offer chose to delay it, and that is minuted in Vey's file.** **Entry 61.**
- **Wed wk 16, about 09:40, 10:10, 10:10 and 16:10** — Ch 59. The common room on the first floor of the **Ninth Street block** — one of the four places in this city holding 4-19 stock — with eleven people at about a quarter to ten. **The Exchange meeting is ten to eleven at the Saltmarket.** The trust's condition three expires **at midnight, or on the working day, and no letter comes.** **Delia Frost's hip unit is thirteen weeks old; it was ten weeks old on the Wednesday of week 13, three weeks earlier, and ten is the figure in Chapter 38.** **Entry 62.**
- **Thu wk 16, about 17:10 and about 21:50** — Ch 60. Tam at the bench. **The letter with the course dates came from Deb on the Friday of week 15**, the day before the scene, which is why the envelope said *from Deb* in capitals and there was no conversation. **Entry 63, the first page in the book that is not about a debt he has incurred.**

## Load-book entry numbers, Chapter 39 to Chapter 63

**50, 51, 52** Ch 39 — **53, 54, 55** Mon wk 14 (Ch 43) — **Ch 50, Thu wk 14: a date written at the top of a clean page and nothing else, and the page carries no number** — **56, 57** Tue wk 15 (Ch 51) — **58** Wed wk 15 (Ch 52) — **59** Fri wk 15 (Ch 54) — **60** Sat wk 15 (Ch 55, on a wall) — **61** Tue wk 16 (Ch 58) — **62** Wed wk 16 (Ch 59) — **63** Thu wk 16 (Ch 60). **No entry for the Sunday of week 15 or the Monday of week 16, and none for the Thursday of week 15. Entries are numbered by occurrence and not by day, and a page may carry a date and no number: Chapter 50's is the only one in sixty chapters.**

**The run is continuous from 50 to 63 with no duplicate and no gap, and Chapter 39 is what fixes it: its last entry is 52, so Chapter 43's three entries are 53, 54 and 55.** In delivered prose they were numbered 55, 56 and 57 and collided with 56 and 57 in Chapter 51, while 53 and 54 appeared nowhere. The renumbering recorded in the batch summary was never reconciled against Chapter 43, and **this file was the reason the error survived: it carried "55 Mon wk 15 (Ch 50)" — a day Chapter 50 does not contain, since Chapter 50 runs Thu wk 14 to Mon wk 15 and the date-only page is in the Thursday scene.** Chapter 60's fourth-pass fix then read that figure as the chapter's own and put a nonexistent Monday of week 15 into the prose. **No chapter cites a load-book number above 52 in running text, so the correction moves three numerals and nothing else.**

## Canon now fixed in Chapter prose (Chapters 51–60)

- **What a failed condition does.** Four of the trust's six conditions lapse with condition three and **two stay**: the prohibition on demonstrating, teaching, assessing or assisting at any practice in that department on any premises of that department, and the list of the four names the department may put in front of any of them at any time without telling him. **A prohibition does not need a sponsor and a document a department received on the Thursday of week 14 is a document a department does not un-receive.** The ledger stops being open to a department and a trust and the list comes off the wall.
- **The reason nobody wrote to him.** He asked for the alternative not to be put in writing, because a thing in writing is a thing you can be given. **He would rather she never put the lapse in writing either, and has decided so.**
- **CONDITION SIX LAPSED WITH CONDITION THREE, AND THE VOLUME LOST ITS REVIEW.** Condition six is *a review in person at the end of the term, before a board that is not this panel, and the holder attends and is examined*, and it went on the Wednesday of week 16 with one, two and three, because it is the trust's. **It is the only documented basis in this city for a body being convened to examine him, and as of the Wednesday of week 16 nothing in the city has standing to convene it.** He notices it in the load book that night, four days before it happens, and the note is that he has got about fourteen weeks to find out whether a man walks into a room nobody has called. **MOVEMENT IV IS THE NOTICE AND THE PREPARATION AND THE QUESTION OF WHO CALLS THE MEETING; THE SITTING IS IN MOVEMENT V, AT THE END OF TERM TWO, IN THE WEEK OF WEEK 30. Movement II must not invent a sponsor and nobody rings him about it before week 30.**
- **THE FIGURE FOR THE RUN IN VOLUME 02 IS *FOURTEEN* WEEKS, AND IT IS DELIBERATE.** Volume 01's thirteen weeks was the length of the term, and the term ended on the Friday of week 12; Chapters 51–60 run a fortnight past that. **Fourteen is used throughout Chapters 51–60. The only surviving *thirteen weeks* in the batch is Delia Frost's hip unit in Chapter 59, which is a measurement and not a figure about the case.**
- **The three blanks and the sentence.** **The originating practice holds no record of it.** Three suppliers, three packs, three buildings, word for word, from three companies that have never spoken. It is also under the ruled empty UPSTREAM LICENSOR box in the co-operative's quarterly, written by M. Szabo, and the two have never heard of each other. **This is Volume 02's first real find and it is not a name.**
- **The four words.** **Deliveries — north — awaiting — signature.** On the carbon visitor column at the gate of the Lattice depot, on **eleven lines out of about four hundred**, two of them inside six weeks, one of them on the Tuesday of week 14 about four minutes after a stock docket for one item and one unit was signed in a hand that is not a driver's. **A filler is on every line; these are not on every line.** The column is never copied and never photographed; the depot's fire-safety photograph list is door, roller shutter, bollards, exit, and does not include the gate. **The Rotherham depot's column says the same four words fourteen times in a fortnight, which is nothing.**
- **Stock docket D/2214/6.** Dockets are destroyed on Fridays; Vance has been off since the Tuesday of week 14, so the number is still on the spike. **The destination is a box number and a post town that is not Nacre, and Vance has not written it down anywhere and is not going to.**
- **The bay at the Lattice depot.** A carton of booklets and **four sealed replacement instructor card sets, revision 4-19, in a brown sleeve**, taken on the Tuesday of week 14 and still in the bay with nobody's label on it. **The company stopped selling the 4-19 card in October**; the sets are on the shelf because three classrooms asked for a spare in week eight and Vance signed for them himself. **A card set is not stock and a signature line is not a decision.**
- **The district training pack, dated the twelfth.** Eleven pages, photographs, and on page nine, in a box, in a small print: **RELEASES CONFIRMED BY SUPERVISOR.**
- **Yvette Kohl, 46**, Threadline district safety and training supervisor. First appearance, Chapter 55. **She is good at it, and that is the problem.** She does a whole yard in about an hour and a half in the sun and does not once tell anybody to stop working. **She refuses the number twice — once in the room, on the grounds that four rooms full of competent people have stopped asking a question because a number came out of her mouth, and once to Marek afterwards — and says she gives it out only in rooms where somebody is writing it down.** She says the reason she is there is that forty men took a Saturday off work to be told they were not stupid and the alternative is that they find out on a Monday in a yard on their own. **She tells Marek, for nothing, that somebody who has been through a Saturday like that will stand at the top of some steps one day and wait for a voice, and nobody will ever connect it back. THE INTERVAL IS DELIBERATELY UNDATED AND NO CALENDAR FIGURE OF HERS IS ON THE PAGE: the eleven months and the fourteen months and the image of the men following her into a river were all cut in review, because every one of them belongs to the Chapter 75 midpoint.** **THE FIGURE ITSELF IS NOT ON THE PAGE AND IS NOT TO BE SPOKEN UNTIL CHAPTER 75.** **ALL FOUR ARE NOW SPENT AND SPENT ONLY ONCE, IN CHAPTER 75: the percentage, the eleven months, the fourteen months and the river. The river is the assessor reading a printed form out in front of about thirty people with forty men on the hard standing stopped working to hear it, and Kohl saying forty men would come into a river with her on a Saturday at a low water and that she could not stop four of them and would not want to try, and the assessor putting her own name against the line that says she did not say no. None of the four may be spoken again in this volume.**
- **The Saturday certificate.** A6, printed, name typed, no expiry, no assessment, no fee: **the named holder has completed a supervised refresher in the safe release of a load at height, and the holder is able to perform and confirm a release where the conditions for release are not within the holder's own judgement.** That clause is the third beat of a 4-19 card with a person substituted for the load. **Thirty-eight typed on the day; about nine of the forty-one do not have a job and four of the nine are on that side of the river; nine came on foot.**
- **The card in the Saturday card.** *Safe release refresher — two hours — free. Saturday of week 15, back room, Exchange, 10:30. Coffee. Booklet. Certificate at the end. No cost, no catch, no employer asked to sign anything. Bring nothing.* **The line that got forty people through a door is the one about the employer, and it is in the handwriting of the woman who wrote the card.**
- **Dessa Kwan's account of the room.** The back room is behind the kitchen and about four hundred people a week walk past it to get to a kettle. **Nobody voted. She has a key, Bea Nunn has a key, and about nine people in that building could let a company put a table in it on a Saturday and none of them would call a meeting about it, and that is not corruption, that is what a room is when the people who can open it are all busy and honest.** Her sentence, offered to Marek in a shop with a bag of washers: **write down that I told you the kettle was the room.**
- **Aurel Kosta's yard, off the Meridian Corridor.** Four sets of steps up to a wagon; a control post at the top of the middle one with a plastic horn in a cage; a prefab office with the door propped open, a kettle on a ring, a chair and a window. **About forty men, Monday to Thursday.** **The blue card is on the office wall by the window. It says LOAD SECURED — RELEASED BY YARD CONTROL, and where there would be a line for a name and a time there is nothing, and under the nothing there is a rule, and the rule says RELEASE ON HORN. The top corner is bent about an inch and a half where somebody sat on it, and the printing has cracked across the crease.** **This is Chapter 30's card and must not be reworded.**
- **The sheet next to the kettle.** A box for a name and a time, unfilled in the thirty-one years Kosta has been in that room, on a document that is neither a district document nor a school document, and he has never asked where it came from and has said it is the last sentence he will say about it in his life. **Oren Vey has read that yard's sheet for eleven years and has never once asked why the box is on the sheet and not on the card.**
- **Aurel Kosta.** Fifty-eight. **Thirty-one years on that yard, twenty-six of them driving a shunter.** A bad shoulder out of 2019, third wagon, a shunt in crooked, off for two years, never right since, and he never went to anybody because you are on the Saturday list and if you go to anybody about a shoulder you come off the list. **There is no form for it anywhere.** A foreman's report went in that year, with a district number on it, saying *manual handling*, and it is in a drawer in an office and it is about nothing. **A piece of A4 with his name on it is in a box in the yard office with the tea bags in it and nobody has read it.** His sentence: **if it is not written down then it did not happen, and that is fine, right up until the day somebody wants it to have happened, and then it did not, and there is nothing to argue with.** He told a stranger in a repair shop doorway in week 12 and has chewed on the foreman's report for three weeks and told nobody, because the second he asks about two things he is a man with a notebook.
- **His mate Vic.** Technical side of a college twenty-two or thirty years ago, a fitter. **Vic did the old way — said it out loud to himself on the wagon and took his hands off — and has never had a horn, and is a stationmaster now.** Kosta's warning, and the volume's argument: **the old way is not as safe, and the new way has a very good record for a reason, and both of those are true, and you are going to have to write something that holds both of them, or you will be doing what everybody else does, which is pick one and shout it.**
- **The boy of nine weeks at the control post.** Nineteen, a hi-vis a size too big. Before the Wednesday a card went up he worked. On the Tuesday he held a shutter on the top set for another man and **looked up at the post the whole time and nobody said anything**, and the man came round and they got it done and went home. **He stands at that post now like he is waiting to be told he was allowed to have been on those steps.**
- **Reuben Daw's line 38.** Four hundred words, his own, block capitals, signed *R. D.*, entered on the **Tuesday morning of week 16** at about ten past eight in the back room with Bea Nunn in the chair and no meeting — three days after the yard visit, which was the Sunday, and one day before Bea Nunn read it out. **It is the thirty-eighth line. Petar Vance's Saturday-refresher card, dated the Friday of week 15 and his second line in that book, goes in at the first item of the meeting on the Wednesday of week 16 and takes the count to thirty-nine, and Ruslan Petric's own words go in at the fourth item, after that, which takes it to forty. THE BOOK IS AT FORTY LINES AT THE CLOSE OF THE MEETING ON THE WEDNESDAY OF WEEK 16 AND NOTHING LATER MAY CALL IT THIRTY-EIGHT OR THIRTY-NINE.** **Bea Nunn read it twice in full in front of eleven people on the Tuesday morning, which she does not do with the short ones, said *noted*, and then said a rule she has never stated: nobody take that card out of the book.** Four of the eleven noticed. Reuben did not. **She said it a second time on the Wednesday morning, about nine seconds after Petric's, to a room that had heard it once already, and the day is the Tuesday.**
- **R. Ancrum / Roy Ancrum, 56**, rang the Exchange on the **Tuesday of week 14** — not the twelfth week — asking whether the number on the wall was about a college drill, and was told it is a service number, it is not a fault, and if it was about a room then the room is a department's room. **The line is in his own words, signed, with *Call taken by B. Nunn, who wrote the rest of it down* under it.** He was one of three people Talia Venn spoke to on Fell Road on the Friday of week 12, off the top of the September receipts list. He is also the man whose knee went on the second pound on the Monday of week 14 and is written on DPS/FS/4 as *unidentified*.
- **Talia Venn's answer.** The Friday of week 15, in a first-floor room in Bower Street, to a man whose title is *continuity and returns*. **Two hundred and six pages; sixty-one are hers.** The word on the form is *refusal*; the word she used is **withheld pending notice**. His sentence, verbatim: *I am not asking you to break a confidence. I am asking you to build a machine that can do it without you.* **She said no to the mechanism and yes to a count, and gave a number per month for as long as the number is the question, and said the day he asks for anything else she will give the same sentence in writing.** **In about four weeks he will ask for the count to be evidenced and she will refuse again, and she told Marek on the Friday in advance so that he would not turn up, and he said it back to her word for word.**
- **Roisin Keogh telephones Talia Venn on the Wednesday of week 17** about a statement on the practice on the round. **Talia can then say honestly that she has not seen a round, because there is nothing in the book to give them — which is why the row cannot come out.**
- **The Working Share's fourth condition, quoted in Chapter 53 from Chapter 49**: *the holder may not hold a practice alone on any equipment owned or held by this department, on any premises of this department, or under this department's insurance, at any time in this term, and the prohibition is not limited to Institute equipment and is not limited to hours.* **The trust's condition four says the same thing in different words and is read off a sheet by a man called Anders Kolt.** **On the Wednesday of week 16 the trust's stops existing and the department's does not, and Marek Senn worked out on the Tuesday of week 16 that he would rather have had two bars across the door, and asked for it to be written down somewhere before somebody else put it on a piece of paper.** Anneke Roos's insurance officer said the clause now says the only person in the building who is any use with a fourth beat cannot teach a fourth beat, and that the answer is for him to stop being twenty-two.
- **The practical refresher notice.** *Practical refresher — revision 4-19. Monday of week 19, practical hall, 09:30. All students and staff holding a current 4-19 card. Attendance is not compulsory. Attendance is recorded.* **Forty-one names**, the last three a man of about sixty, a woman with her name spelled with a hyphen, and Davan Rusk. The sheet goes into a file that goes to the regional office at the end of the term with the marks and the failures, with a column for *reason given* and a tick if a member of staff does not ring. **In September thirty-one of Rusk's hundred and eleven third-years did not attend the first session after the half term, and the column is why nobody found out for a year.** Darla Vint **could** give those thirty-one names in ninety seconds and will not, and says she has watched him not ask eleven times in fourteen weeks.
- **The Friday briefing.** Half past eleven, a room off the corridor with a lectern, eleven chairs and a whiteboard, about two and a half hours, **eleven people, no equipment, nobody asked to do anything.** Rusk put revision 3-08 on the lectern face down, turned it round, and said the number out loud before he said anything else. **Page four of the Threadline booklet is not in the manual.** Rusk will not read it out to eleven people and told Marek to read it to them himself on the Monday of week 19, from the front, in the teaching shape, and Marek said no. **Ines Sarr's Exchange card is about this room: a college in a first-floor room in Nine Bridges on the Friday of week 15 for two and a half hours with eleven people in it, and what happens to a person who turns up.**
- **The ledger's second reading.** Monday of week 16, ten to nine, then a woman at eleven. **Dessa Kwan's row was *D. Kwan, 9 sec, hot pass, tested, no name for the practice* and the whole row came out, not the name, at 11:20, in one stroke, in pen, with Marek Senn's name in the fourth column as taker and no copy made or kept.** He wrote under it that **the row is not written down anywhere else in this city** — the first sentence he has written in fourteen weeks that has made something less findable. **Her reply: then you have just made my row permanent, and I would like you to notice that, because I have been in a repair shop for eleven years and nobody has ever made one of my rows permanent by refusing me a copy of it.** **Reuben Daw is one of the four in the department's document and not one of the three on the ledger page, and the reason is that he said *I have got no form* in a market on the Sunday of week 13.** **Bea Nunn's name went on the page as well**, because she took the first call into that book in the twelfth week and it is a different building and the rule does not care; she was telephoned at ten past twelve and did not take hers off, and said not to be rung about a book again for about a fortnight. **Five names in the book after the Monday: the boy, Rafi Pell, B. Nunn, and Marek Senn twice.**
- **The three telephone calls on the Monday afternoon of week 16 took eleven minutes** and were made because Bea Nunn's rule says the taker's name stays in. Rafi counted to two out loud in a doorway in daylight and said a rule you only use when somebody else isn't in it is not a rule, it's a favour. Reuben Daw asked which row and was told it was not his. **Tam Arrick said don't ring me about the book, ring me about the course.**
- **Oren Vey's four sides.** Technician, district service, Lattice. **Four days, Monday to Thursday. Grade 2, spine rate plus an on-call allowance. Reporting to W. Dunleavy, district service manager. Start Monday of week 19. Runs to the end of the department's term, which ends on the last Friday of week 30. One month's notice, on the front page.** The fifth clause, put in by Vey and not negotiable: **the company's own 4-19 course, both modules, in the first month, Monday and Tuesday, on the clock, no fee, and at the end of it a card with his name typed on it that does not expire, which says the holder is competent to confirm scope to a learner** — the sentence on Davan Rusk's own certificate. **Vey did not write it. It is eleven years old.** The four sides are brought back on **the Friday of week 18** and there will be nothing different in them.
- **The term is twelve weeks: Monday of week 19 to the last Friday of week 30.** This is the same length as the term Volume 01 ran, and it is the reason the job is a trap: **the working share expires at five o'clock on the Friday of week 30 and the trust's conditions end at the same moment, and a thing which stops on a date is not a job, it is a gap with a wage in it, and the only person in this city who has ever handed him a gap with a wage in it is a woman on a rail.** **THE OUTLINE'S MOVEMENT HEADERS STOP AT WEEK 24 AND ITS CLOCK TABLE PUTS THE REVIEW AT "THE END OF THE TERM, THE WEEK OF WEEK 19", WHICH CANNOT BE THE END OF ANYTHING. THE RESOLUTION ADOPTED: the review in person is sat at the end of term two, in the week of week 30, and the outline's clock-table entry is a slip for the Monday of week 19. Movements IV and V therefore run to week 30.**
- **Meridian consolidation point, lot onward.** Threadline's own words, in an intake summary filled in by a stores clerk because whoever sold them the lot did not name itself. **It is a place and not a supplier. It is a customer's, not Threadline's, and about forty other lots came off the same van, and no one who works for Oren Vey has ever been inside the building.** He gave the building and not the box number, because a box number is a person's mail and a building is on a van. **The licensor field is therefore blank on a third document.**
- **Vey's rota.** **Marek is on Tuesdays and Wednesdays, and nobody is in at seven on a Monday after the twenty-third of the month**, which is how the Monday at ten past six is kept clear. **The cost of that kindness is named by Vey himself: Marek will be on a yard off the Meridian in a fortnight, and the district that writes those cards is a customer of Vey's.** Vey's last sentence in the shop: **the job is a lanyard with my name on it and it will get me through a door a great many people in this city cannot get through, and I have decided I am not going to be embarrassed about it, because a door is a door and I am not the worst of the people who want one.**
- **Asha Reed's sentence, said in a first-year common room on the Wednesday of week 16.** Eleven people, nine of them first years, one voice. *The case is Senn's and the technique is nobody's yet.* Nine asked a *what do I do* question, which is not a good question. Toby Ansell asked whether there is anything in it that can be taught and she said **no**, out loud, in a room where she is the only voice, and about four of them looked at the floor. **She said out loud that she still does not know how to fix it and does not know whether it is fixable, and has now said it three times and it has not stopped being true.** **Marek was in the corridor for forty minutes and did not go in, and she told him she saw him and will not be the one who says it out loud for him.** **She refused, in one sentence, to be made the person who notices** — because in six months somebody in a depot will be able to come and find her, and they will find her because she will have been named somewhere by somebody who was trying to help.
- **The Exchange meeting, Wednesday of week 16, ten to eleven, about twelve people, four minutes an item, no vote for anybody, Bea Nunn in the chair and a tin.** Six items: Vance wants the Saturday refresher on a different day and does not care which company runs it; a boiler in the Cordage; **Ines Sarr's fourth card about the room**; **Ruslan Petric, standing, not sitting down**; **Reuben's line 38 read out in full, four minutes and eleven seconds, afterwards nobody asked a question about it, and that was the correct number of questions**; and **Ena Truslove of the Fold nursery offering thirty plastic chairs that are hers to anybody who wants a Saturday, and saying that those chairs went into a college on the Tuesday of week 14 because they were hers and Friday had come and gone, and nobody from her nursery was asked and nobody has been thanked.** **Bea Nunn's answer to the room, said through a door: not *who is responsible for it* but *who would take the name out for them*, and about four answers in the room, none of which is an answer to that question.** Marek answered *you would* from the corridor and did not come in, and was entered in the book as **M. Senn, in the corridor, at about a quarter to eleven**, because the rule does not have a word for what he was and she is not making one up at sixty-one.
- **What came back through the box on Weaver Street.** A playing card, three words in ballpoint on one side, **It was not there.** No name, no second side, no way of finding out who looked. **Ruslan Petric's reading of it: it is not a lie and it is not a removal; it says they checked, and the only thing in the record about him is that a second candidate followed a word, and there is nothing in it to take out, and they are not going to pretend there is, and he asked.** He then said the second thing in the first person with his name at the end, and **Bea Nunn said *nobody take that card out of the book* again about nine seconds later, to a room that had already heard her say it once on the Tuesday morning of week 16, in the back room, with nobody from a college in it.**
- **The department's 4-19 course.** **Monday and Tuesday of week 20**, a training place in Lattice off the roundabout, the district's mark and a company's name on the door, both modules, the card and the practical, **£440 for the two days**, certificate at the end of the second day, and it is the same course the district has run for eleven years and the one on page nine of the Saturday pack. **To an employee it is on the clock and free; to anybody else it is £440 and Oren Vey has no power to change it.** The *employer* box on the form is empty and a woman read Tam Arrick back *not in employment* in a nice way and then said *that's fine, most of the ones on this one are*. **Tam posts it on the Monday of week 19 so that it sits in a box for a week, because he is not posting a thing on a Friday and finding out on the Tuesday.**
- **THE HINGE, AS IT STANDS IN CHAPTER 60.** A practice with a person in it, a practice with a word in it, and a certificate that makes the word portable, permanent, and free of the room it was learned in. **It was said in fourteen words by a boy of twenty who cannot hold a kettle and who does not know he said it: *If somebody rings me at work in four weeks and asks me to do a release, I'm going to be the only man in the room who's got a card.*** **It is a detection and not a technique, it has no name of his own, he cannot say it in a shape anybody will hear, and it releases nobody.** In the load book he wrote, and did not resolve: *whether the fourth beat is a thing that can be written down at all, or whether it is only ever a person who is in the room* — and then, on the same page, the only line on it that is his: *a certificate is the exact opposite of a mouth. It is a page that says a mouth happened.*
- **Oren Vey's refused offer, and Marek's refusal of it, Chapter 58.** Vey says he can put a man behind a counter in a building a district office does not own and a card in a boy's hand saying his share has lapsed, and that it would cost the boy nothing and nobody would ever know — **and then says he is not going to be the man who buys a boy's silence with a wage, and is telling Marek that instead of telling him what he would have done.** Marek: **you have just offered me a way of being paid for somebody else's harm, and I have been looking for one of those all term, and it took me four seconds to see it and nine to be sure, and the nine were because you said it out loud instead of putting it in a clause.** **The price minuted in Chapter 49 was Tam Arrick's and it stays Tam's.**
- **What he does not know.** He does not know what the fourth beat is, and the fifth line of the card has four sentences in Tam Arrick's hand in a blue biro with no full stop at the end of the third one, **and if Tam had asked him he would have had to read him his own handwriting out of a book and then write it down again to give it to him, and the writing down is the part that is the machine.**
- **Delia Frost, 61**, over a takeaway on Carnation Row in Nine Bridges. **Hip unit thirteen weeks old. Gets short of breath about four in the afternoon, twice, and it goes in ten minutes or a quarter of an hour. She has told one person, on a telephone, on the Wednesday of week 13, and has not walked into the shop about it, because a shop is a shop and a man behind a counter is a stranger however many times he has been kind to you on a telephone.** **He is four miles away and is not told, and there is nobody in this city whose job it is to be told, and there was not going to be one in September either.**
- **The number forty-one.** `state/continuity.md` carries a standing warning that there is no figure of forty-one in the Exchange's four-column book. **The book is at thirty-eight lines until Petar Vance's goes in at the first item on the Wednesday of week 16, thirty-nine after that, and forty at the close of the meeting.** The two forty-ones in this batch are **the Institute's refresher notice in Chapter 53, forty-one names on the sheet**, and **the Saturday in Chapter 55, where the back room is forty at every count in the chapter, the woman at the door says *forty-one, one left* off a list with a tick that is not a name, Dessa Kwan says on the telephone in Chapter 57 that forty-one people were in that room, and thirty-eight certificates are typed on the day.** None of the three is the book. Recorded so that nobody later reads the wrong forty-one.

## Corrections applied in the repair pass of batch 0001 (Chapters 51–60)

**Thirty-one items. No plot moved, no scene cut, no ending altered, no canon reversed.** Full list and reasoning in `workspace/volume-02/batch-0001/SUMMARY.md`. The ones a writer must not reintroduce:

- **Chapter 56's yard card was not Chapter 30's yard card.** The draft had invented a card on a *control post* reading *LOAD MOVEMENT — SIGNALLED RELEASE — SUPERVISED*. **Chapter 30 is canon and the card is on the wall of the yard office, blue, bent at the TOP corner, and says *load secured — released by yard control* with no line for a name and a time at all, and under the rule, *release on horn*.** Chapter 56, Reuben's line 38 and Chapter 58's version of the yard sheet were all rewritten to that. **A card with no name field is better evidence than a card with a supervisor's signal on it, and the draft had thrown away the thing Volume 01 had already given us.**
- **Chapter 56 gave Reuben Daw thirty-one years on a yard.** He is twenty-two; Aurel Kosta has the thirty-one years.
- **Chapter 56's Kosta had "eleven weeks"** to work out the foreman's report. **Three weeks**, and he met Marek on the Tuesday of week 12.
- **Chapters 54 and 57 both put R. Ancrum's Exchange line in week 12.** It is **week 14** — Chapter 48 has Marek reading it out on the Wednesday of week 14 as a Tuesday entry.
- **Chapter 57's Tam had the department's telephone on "a Wednesday of week twenty" and "about eleven days"** from the Monday of week 16, which is four weeks. **Chapter 60's "six weeks" — twice, in the speech and in the load-book quotation — corrected to four weeks**, agreeing with the dated clock and with Marek's own "about four weeks" three pages earlier.
- **Chapter 60 had two versions of Tam's exit side by side**, one posting on the Wednesday of week 20 and one on the Monday of week 19, with two sentences repeated almost word for word. Consolidated to the Monday of week 19. **"That yard" had no antecedent in the chapter and is now "a yard".**
- **Chapter 57's Dessa Kwan had Kohl's sixty per cent.** Kohl refuses the number twice in Chapter 55. **The figure is the Chapter 75 midpoint and is not spent in week 16.**
- **Chapter 57's ledger name arithmetic did not add up.** Reconstructed from Chapter 50, where the page carries three names against a date: three before, two named partners after, plus Marek twice and Bea Nunn once, five in all. **Chapter 60's "three names against a review date" corrected to two, plus his own twice.**
- **Chapter 51 said he had written the line down again "every morning for a fortnight"** on the morning after the day he first wrote it. **Five mornings.** The trust's £480 was "across eleven weeks"; the term is twelve weeks, so **twelve**.
- **Chapter 58's "in 1998" is cut.** A year cannot be reconciled against Chapter 30's thirty-one years and the manuscript's calendar, which does not reconcile. **No year is used anywhere in this batch.**
- **Chapter 58's four words were said "on the Wednesday morning of this week"** from the Tuesday of week 16. **Last week.**
- **Chapter 58's yard sheet had "a field for a name that nobody fills in."** There is no such field on the card; the sheet has a box for a name and a time and the card has neither, and that difference is the point. **Oren Vey has never once asked why the box is on the sheet and not on the card.**
- **Chapter 58 had a paragraph of narration about Marek opening with a quotation mark and no closing one.** **Chapter 57 had a stray triple apostrophe.** **Chapter 59 had a line left dangling mid-word** (`"Marek—"`). All three fixed.
- **Chapter 59's Delia Frost sentence told her she had not told "the woman she rang the number about"** — a person you cannot tell about a number you rang in order to reach them. **She has not walked into the shop.**
- **The duplicated "the week of week" tic** — five instances in this batch and one in Chapter 49, the only one in Volume 01 — is gone from the batch, **and Chapter 49's was corrected too.**
- **The load-book entries were renumbered** so the Saturday of week 15 has a number, moving three later entries by one. **This was never reconciled against Chapter 43 and left a duplicate at 56 and 57 and a gap at 53 and 54; Chapter 43's entries are 53, 54 and 55.** See the run table above.
- **Quotation-mark parity was checked in all ten chapters against Chapter 49's four-paragraph speech.** Every remaining odd count is a multi-paragraph speech under the manuscript's convention — every paragraph opens and only the last closes. Chapter 58's was the only genuine defect. **The equivalent claim for bold marks was made in the same list and was false; see the fourth and fifth passes below.**

## Corrections applied in the review repair of batch 0001 (fourteen items)

**An external review of the two passes above. No plot moved, no scene cut, no ending altered.** Full list in `workspace/volume-02/batch-0001/SUMMARY.md`. The ones a writer must not reintroduce:

- **The Exchange's four-column book has ONE count, and it is a chain, not a number: thirty-eight lines until Petar Vance's Saturday-refresher card goes in at the first item on the Wednesday of week 16, thirty-nine after that, and forty at the close of the meeting, when Ruslan Petric's own words go in at the fourth item. Reuben Daw's yard line is thirty-eight and it went in on the Tuesday morning of week 16 at ten past eight, in the back room, with Bea Nunn in the chair and no meeting.** Three files had three different answers and one file asserted two of them in the same paragraph.
- **Bea Nunn's unstated rule — *nobody take that card out of the book* — is dated to the TUESDAY MORNING OF WEEK 16.** She says it in the back room when Reuben's line goes in and again at the meeting on the Wednesday, about nine seconds after Petric's. The Thursday and the Monday were both drafts' errors and there is no scene on either day.
- **The figure for the run is fourteen in Volume 02 and thirteen in Volume 01, and the two are not a contradiction.** Volume 01's thirteen is the length of the term, weeks 1–12. **The one surviving *thirteen weeks* in the prose of Chapters 51–60 is Delia Frost's hip unit, which is a measurement.**
- **The Saturday of week 15 has three numbers and all three are true: the back room is FORTY at every count in Chapter 55, the woman at the door says *forty-one, one left* off a list, and Dessa Kwan says forty-one on the telephone in Chapter 57. Thirty-eight certificates are typed on the day.** There is no figure of forty-one in the four-column book, then or now.
- **Chapter 59's clock closes exactly: the common room holds eleven people at about a quarter to ten, Marek is at the door at ten past nine, it empties at about ten minutes to ten, Asha Reed tells him at ten past ten that he is ten minutes late, the Exchange meeting ran from ten to eleven, he is in the corridor by the kitchen for about forty minutes, and Bea Nunn writes him into the book as *M. Senn, in the corridor, at about a quarter to eleven*, which is forty-five minutes.**
- **Chapter 58's load-book entry names Oren Vey, who is forty-one, and the yard's box is on a wall and on a sheet and not on a card.** *What fills in a box on a post* is gone from the manuscript.

## Corrections applied in the fourth and fifth passes of batch 0001 (four items)

**The fourth pass was a mechanical sweep for claims about the past that the surrounding pages contradict; the fifth was an independent review of the fourth's own repair and of its claims. No plot moved, no scene cut, no ending altered.** Full list in `workspace/volume-02/batch-0001/SUMMARY.md` and `reviews/volume-02-batch-0001.md`. The ones a writer must not reintroduce:

- **Chapter 60's reach back to a clean page is anchored to the THURSDAY OF WEEK FOURTEEN, not the Monday of week 15.** The beat is `chapter-0050.md:378` and Chapter 50 is the Thursday afternoon of week 14. **Chapter 50's Monday of week 15 scene is the maintenance ledger opening at nine and the load book is not opened in it.** The Monday of week 15 came from this file, which carried *55 Mon wk 15 (Ch 50)*, and it has been corrected. **Do not date that page to a Monday.**
- **The load-book numbering is one continuous run and Chapter 43 is inside it.** Chapter 39 ends at 52; **Chapter 43's three entries are 53, 54 and 55**; Chapter 50's page carries a date and no number; Chapter 51 has 56 and 57. **There is no entry 55 on the Monday of week 15 and there never was.** `workspace/volume-02/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` carries the corrected run, and **Ch 61's entry is 64.**
- **Bold-mark parity in all ten was claimed clean and was false: Chapters 58 and 59 were odd.** Each had a load-book entry whose closing `**` was a single `*`, so a bold clause ran on past its own entry — in Chapter 58 into the next paragraph, in Chapter 59 through the scene break into Delia Frost's opening. **Counting only `**` is not a sufficient test; a full count of `*` per line found six more of the identical construct** at Chapters 54, 58 and 59, where a label-style entry (`*Do not know.*` and its three siblings) ends in a lone `*` that opens an italic nothing closes. Chapter 54 has the correct form three lines above one of them. **All eight are closed and no line in the batch now carries an odd number of `*`.**
