# VOLUME 02 — MOVEMENT II (Chapters 61–70)

## Verified in-world timeline of Chapters 61–70 (Movement II, weeks 18–20)

**Weeks run Monday to Sunday. Week 18 = Mon 21 – Sun 27. Week 19 = Mon 28 – Sun 4. Week 20 = Mon 5 – Sun 11.** Out of term throughout. **The term is twelve weeks, Monday of week 19 to the last Friday of week 30.**

- **Fri wk 18, about 16:00, and about 17:30** — Ch 61. Vey brings the four sides back in a clear wallet, in the same chair. **He signs at about ten past five.** The induction pack comes with him and **page four** is read standing up at the bench and goes in a drawer under the bench. **Nkechi Osei is telephoned at the bank of the Weir at about half past five.** **Entry 64.**
- **Mon wk 19, 07:00, 07:20, about 10:00, 09:25, 09:30 to 10:40, and 11:00** — Ch 62, *Through Wired Glass*. **There is nobody in the depot on a Monday and there has not been since Friday week 18.** The practical hall runs **09:30 to 10:40 with forty-one people in it**; he parks at 09:25, sees the room through a wired-glass panel at 09:35, **is on a bench at the north end under the fire door for the first forty-five minutes and in a chair at the back for the last twenty-five**, and is not asked to speak in seventy minutes. **Rusk puts the supplier's booklet up at ten past ten and reads page four twice; Marek goes in at a quarter past ten, for the final practice block.** Darla Vint speaks to him in the corridor after. He does a call in Saltmarket from eleven, with a quarter of an hour in the van first. **Entry 65.**
- **Tue wk 19, 07:20, about 10:00, 12:20, 14:30** — Ch 63. Vance at the depot floor at 07:20. Ashfields at about ten. **Talia Venn telephones the shop at 20 past twelve and Marek Senn takes it in a van on a forecourt in Nine Bridges.** **Entry 66.**
- **Thu wk 19, about 17:10** — Ch 65 (and only Ch 65). **The hand set is put on the bench at about ten past five with nobody in the building.** The calibration is still out of date and he has still not told a person.
- **Wed wk 19, 08:15 to 11:00** — Ch 64. The yard off the Meridian. **The box on the sheet is written in at about ten to ten**, in the prefab office, and the boy is at the post by the time the van leaves. **Entry 67.**
- **Thu wk 19, 10:00, about 10:41, about 11:00, about 12:10, 15:30, 16:20, 21:50** — Ch 65. **The release confirmation is at 10:41 for pallet 9; hers was pallet 11.** The form is filled in on a tailgate in the rain at about eleven. She is taken to hospital about eleven and comes back into the compound in a wheelchair at about half past three. **Entry 68.**
- **Fri wk 19, 10:00 to about 12:10, and 16:30** — Ch 66. **Bower Street: the practice standards office is on the third floor; the continuity-and-returns office from Chapter 54 is on the FIRST floor of the same building, and Marek Senn HAS NEVER BEEN INSIDE THAT BUILDING — Chapter 54 says so in its own first sentence and he was told, on the Saturday of week 14, in four sentences, not to be helpful. The two offices have never been in the same corridor and he has never met a soul who works on the first floor.** He is twenty-two. He is at the bench at half past four. **Entry 69.**
- **Sat wk 19, 10:00, 10:30, 14:00, 16:00** — Ch 67. Rafi at the door at ten. **The postbox on the corner at about half past ten.** Reuben Daw on a fish office gate at two with a man waiting for his fags. Dessa Kwan at four. **Entry 70.**
- **Sun wk 19, 16:10, for nine minutes, and about 22:00** — Ch 68. One telephone call. **Entry 71.**
- **Mon wk 20, about 10:00, and Tue wk 20, 08:15 to 16:20** — Ch 69. The roundabout at ten on the Monday, not slowed down for. The canopy on the Tuesday; **Kohl arrives at ten past nine and works for two hours and ten minutes; the register is on a trestle table from about half past nine to about twenty past one.** **Entry 72.**
- **Wed wk 20, 09:30, 10:00 to 11:00, and Thu wk 20, 09:30, 11:10, 12:20, 21:10, 23:40** — Ch 70. **The certificates were typed at about ten past four on the Tuesday, so the boy's card is two days old on the Thursday and he has not taken it out of a drawer once.** **Two rooms at a quarter to ten on the Wednesday; Marek Senn is on the depot floor at ten past nine.** **The boy telephones the depot at about half past nine on the Thursday** and gets a machine, and rings the shop four times. Marek Senn calls him back at about twenty past twelve from a forecourt in Nine Bridges. Talia Venn rings the shop at ten past nine that evening. **Entry 73.**

## Load-book entry numbers, Chapter 50 to Chapter 73

**50, 51, 52** Ch 39 — **53, 54, 55** Mon wk 14 (Ch 43) — **Ch 50, Thu wk 14: a date on a clean page and nothing else, and the page carries no number** — **56, 57** Tue wk 15 (Ch 51) — **58** Wed wk 15 (Ch 52) — **59** Fri wk 15 (Ch 54) — **60** Sat wk 15 (Ch 55, on a wall) — **61** Tue wk 16 (Ch 58) — **62** Wed wk 16 (Ch 59) — **63** Thu wk 16 (Ch 60) — **64** Fri wk 18 (Ch 61) — **65** Mon wk 19 (Ch 62) — **66** Tue wk 19 (Ch 63) — **67** Wed wk 19 (Ch 64) — **68** Thu wk 19 (Ch 65) — **69** Fri wk 19 (Ch 66) — **70** Sat wk 19 (Ch 67) — **71** Sun wk 19 (Ch 68) — **72** Tue wk 20 (Ch 69) — **73** Thu wk 20 (Ch 70).

**The run is continuous from 50 to 73 with no duplicate and no gap, and Chapter 61's entry is 64 as the batch prompt required.** One entry per chapter in Movement II. **There is no entry for the Monday of week 20, and that is correct: the chapter that contains it is set on the Tuesday, and the day is in the entry as a day he was not in a room.** **Entries 68 and 70 are about somebody else's day — Sondra Mbeki's Thursday and Tam Arrick's and Reuben Daw's Saturday — and entry 70 is the second page in the book that is not about the writer, after entry 63 in Chapter 60.**

## Canon now fixed in Chapter prose (Chapters 61–70)

- **The job.** Technician, district service, Lattice. **Four days, Monday to Thursday, on a rota, not five fixed days.** **The depot floor is Tuesdays and Wednesdays; Mondays and Thursdays are road days, and on a Monday after the twenty-third of the month there is nobody in the building at seven.** **Grade 2 at £13.40 an hour, thirty-eight hours, £509.20 a week before tax; on-call £46 for a week on the rota; one weekend in four, three on-call weekends in a twelve-week term.** Start Monday of week 19; runs to the last Friday of week 30; one month's notice on the front page. **Nothing is arranged after week 30 and Vey has not offered to arrange it.** Reporting to Wyn Dunleavy. **THE FIFTH CLAUSE IS NOT NEGOTIABLE AND IS NOT SATISFIED: Marek Senn did not attend the Thursday intake of week 20, and he has not told anybody, and he has decided to ring Oren Vey on the Friday morning of week 20 and say so.**
- **Vey's reason for the rota, in the open, twice.** *I have never asked you why a man who has been to a flood round on about seven Monday evenings needs a Monday evening, and I did not ask because I did not want to be told, and I have decided I would rather be the man who did not ask.* And, in the same speech: **I am not buying a man who agrees with him. I am buying a man who will write down what he finds, and the two are not the same job, and the second one is the only one of them that is any use to me, and it is the one that is going to cost you money.**
- **There is no clause anywhere in this company that lets an employee stand in a room alone with one of its plates and run a practice** — not alone, not with a customer present, not for four minutes, not in a training room. **Vey asked his own standards side in writing and was refused in four lines, and he says the clause is no because of what happened in this city before any of them were working.** **This is the same prohibition as the department's fourth condition, from a different author, in a different room, and neither is a trade.**
- **PAGE FOUR OF THE COMPANY'S INDUCTION BOOKLET, quoted once and quoted accurately in both Chapter 61 and Chapter 62: a four-colour diagram of a beam, a sling and a man under a load at head height; four numbered steps; and a line in capitals — RELEASE IS GIVEN BY ANOTHER VOICE. DO NOT RELEASE ON YOUR OWN JUDGEMENT.** It is not in any manual and never has been. **IT IS NOT THE CROWN CLAUSE AND IT IS NOT A SEED. It is the same shape as a school card with an instructor in it and a Saturday card with a supervisor in it, and the third beat is the same third beat and the fourth beat is the same fourth beat, and the good engineering and the hinge are the same shape, and nobody in this batch is proved wrong about either.**
- **The certificate's small print. The first clause is quoted four times (Ch 58, Ch 60, Ch 62, Ch 69) and the second — *the holder is competent to confirm scope to a learner* — is quoted in full twice (Ch 58 and Ch 69).** *the holder is able to perform and confirm a release where the conditions for release are not within the holder's own judgement. **The holder is competent to confirm scope to a learner.*** **Eleven years old, on Davan Rusk's own certificate, which is in a drawer in a house in Ashfields and has never been produced.** Rusk read it out on the Monday of week 19 and put his own name against it.
- **THE INSTITUTE'S FILE, THE MONDAY OF WEEK 19.** A company booklet's page four is now in a college's file, in a college's words, dated the Monday of week 19, with a member of staff's name on the front of it saying that he read a supplier's page and recorded that he did. **There is no form for a member of staff putting his name on a supplier's page, because it has never happened in that department before.** The attendance sheet's *reason given* column is now to be filled instead of the tick — Rusk's change, and the deputy registrar's yes.
- **The small card on the side of a plate's housing, quoted in full once (Ch 62):** **4.0, 4.0, 4.0, HOLD 4. RELEASE ON THE WORD. IF NO WORD, WITHDRAW AND REPORT.** **This is the hinge, on a plate, in a customer's storeroom, in Marek Senn's own hand, in about four minutes, and he cannot teach it, may not demonstrate it, and is not asked to perform it anywhere in the batch.**
- **The yard card, Chapter 30's card, unchanged and re-quoted in Chapter 64: LOAD SECURED — RELEASED BY YARD CONTROL, no line for a name and a time at all, RELEASE ON HORN, bent about an inch and a half at the top corner, crease gone white, printing cracked across it.** **IT IS ON THE WALL OF THE PREFAB OFFICE AND NOT ON A CONTROL POST.** The sheet next to the kettle has a printed box for a name and a time and **it had been empty for thirty-one years, and on the Wednesday of week 19 Marek Senn wrote the date, the time and his own name in it, in the small upright hand he uses for tags, because a boy of nineteen handed him a biro with his employer's name on the barrel and asked whether he might.** The boy is filling it in, nine entries by the Thursday of week 20, all in his own hand.
- **The company instruction pinned over the corner of that sheet, put up eleven days earlier, on the Thursday of week 18, and not there on the Sunday of week 15:** hand up, hold, do not enter the path of the load, wait for the company representative's word, and a certificate holder's name into the release register. **THE DISTRICT'S CARD IS STILL ON THE WALL UNDERNEATH IT AND NOBODY TOOK IT DOWN AND NOBODY HAD TO.**
- **THE BOY AT THE CONTROL POST, eleven weeks in:** says *we get two now* about two men with lanyards before either has spoken. **He has been on that yard for twelve weeks and has stood at that post for three, and every part of him dates from the Wednesday a card went up on a wall**, and has worked out that the horn and the sheet are two different things; and is filling in the box, and does not know whether the man he was waiting for was the right one, and nobody is going to ask him whether that was a good idea. **A man of fifty-eight worked out in about four seconds, standing next to Marek Senn, that the price of that box will not be paid by Marek Senn or by him.**
- **THE INCIDENT FORM, Company incident record, fifteen boxes, a version number in the corner: Site. Date. Time of event. Activity in progress. Technique card in use — revision. Card holder involved. Card number. Name of the person who confirmed the release. Employee number. Time the confirmation was given. Nature of injury. First aid. Attended at hospital. Reported by. Supervisor.** **There is no box for the confirmation that was not given to this person, and no box that distinguishes a release from a load, and the form was filled in correctly in about four minutes on a tailgate in the rain.** *11 — no confirmation given* was written on the activity line at the instructing supervisor's request and because it is true.
- **Sondra Mbeki, 39, six years on that route. THE PERSON THE CHAPTER 75 MIDPOINT'S INJURY HAPPENS TO, and the injury is this one, on the Thursday of week 19, a fortnight before the number is read out.** A courier, a subcontractor, and not a plot. Two tendons and a wrist, an operation, a sling, a bus to Bower Street and fifty minutes of it. **She has no card and now has a record saying so, put there by the injury, at a telephone call at about eleven on the Thursday.** *Is there a form for a woman who has not got a card* — *there is a box for it, I wrote in it, that is all the use that box gets.* **She liked it. That is the sentence nobody expected and it is hers.**
- **Pauline Ncube, 38,** company safety supervisor, six years, **good at her job and not a villain and not the author.** She wrote the line about confirmations being valid for the load named in them and says the line is hers, and correct, and no use, because the operator hears the word and not the number. She did nine that morning and eight were right and one was right and about somebody else. She asked for the note saying a woman has no card to be taken off her file and stopped asking when it stayed.
- **The licensing-complaint route, worked out on the page in a room.** Three things: a named holder, a specific act, harm to a person relying on that holder's certificate or in that holder's care. **A company is not a certificate holder. A certificate is the thing a person has and can lose, and the route to the only thing that would let the injured party complain is a two-day course at £440 and two days of a body she does not have.** **No institution is invented: the district practice standards office exists and a district licensing process is a process and not a body with a name.**
- **Dilys Fenwick, 51,** district practice standards, thirty-one years, **third floor of a building in Bower Street** (the continuity-and-returns office is on the first floor of the same building, he has never been inside the building, and the two have never shared a corridor). **Kind, correct, and unable, and all three are correct, and her own true sentence is that the person who caused the injury is not the problem, because if she were, it would have been fixed in nine minutes on the Thursday afternoon, and you do not fix a hole by removing the woman.** She has been asked whether any of the Saturday cards can be established eleven times in nine years and has never once had a name, **because the woman at the door had a list with a tick and not a name.**
- **The company's list of course holders, with names on it, exists, is in the depot building, and Marek Senn has said out loud that he could see it.** It is not read, not copied and not described in any chapter. **It is refused to a district contractor in Chapter 68 and not read in Chapter 69 when it is on a trestle table four feet away for four hours.** The licensor is still unnamed and the blank field is still blank on its three documents.
- **THE EXCHANGE'S FOUR-COLUMN BOOK IS AT FORTY LINES AND NOTHING LATER MAY CALL IT ANYTHING ELSE.** The one item on the Wednesday of week 20 is **not entered**, and Bea Nunn gives the reason: *the rule is the caller's words, in the first person, and a signature at the end, and I am not going to write it down for you and call it yours.* **Thirty-eight until Vance's on the Wednesday of week 16, thirty-nine after it, forty at the close of that meeting, and forty now.** The Asha Reed common room is the same day at the same hour in a different building. **Marek Senn was on a depot floor at ten past nine and was in neither, and that had stopped being a decision some time in the second week of the term.**
- **Ena Truslove's thirty plastic chairs have gone**, in a van, at about eleven on the Saturday morning, collected by a woman with a district badge and **a list with a tick on it and not a name**, for a Saturday in a room on the third floor of the Bower Street building that has stood empty for eight weeks, for about twenty women on a district's books. **Ena said yes in about four seconds, nobody from the Fold nursery was asked, nobody from the Fold nursery is going to be thanked, and it is the third time.** And **Dessa Kwan did not go out, which is the first time in eleven years, and she says so and asks for it in her own words.**
- **THE COURSE, Monday and Tuesday of week 20, a building off the roundabout in Lattice with a district's mark on one half of the door and a company's name on the other.** Both modules, £440, twenty-eight people on the Monday, twenty-one certificates typed by a woman called Maureen whose name is called out to the room, one typed twice and the spoiled one kept. **Marek Senn's intake was the Thursday: eight to four, and he did not go.** On the Tuesday the practical was held on **a district yard off the Meridian that is not the yard with the blue card**, hired by the district from the company, with a district assessor observing.
- **Yvette Kohl refused the number a third time and the figure is still unspoken.** *I have a number and it is real and I say it in rooms where somebody writes it down, and every time I have said it to a person on their own in a doorway I have watched a question go out of their face.* And, on the canopy: **you want to know whether it is the thing you have been looking at since the summer, and if it is, then you have spent fourteen weeks on a thing that a good woman did on purpose, in eleven years, with a clipboard.** **She is right about the two hours and ten minutes and about the competence of the people in her room, and she is not proved wrong about anything, and the accident-reduction figure, the eleven months, the fourteen months and the river were all still unspent at the end of Movement II and all four belonged to Chapter 75. THEY ARE NOW SPENT, IN CHAPTER 75, AND NOWHERE ELSE.**
- **Petar Vance** put Marek Senn on the course itself in his first week, because it is his job, and has said so; **the Thursday intake list is the department's own and went up in week 19, and Wyn Dunleavy put Marek Senn on it himself without asking Vance.** Vance also put them both on the yard off the Meridian for the Wednesday and said so, told him once and only once in a lay-by what a corridor is going to do to him, and stood two feet from a sheet with an unfilled box on it for four minutes in eleven years' habit and did not look at it once. **Wyn Dunleavy put Marek Senn on the Thursday list himself and did not ask Vance, and does not know whether that was a kindness or a line in a book, and Marek Senn is not going to ring a man to find out.**
- **Aurel Kosta, 58**, thirty-one years, **is on a different yard off the Meridian for a Wednesday and a Friday because the district lends yards, and watered a strip of concrete by a fence for two hours and ten minutes and forty more minutes after the canopy was empty so that he did not have to stand in it**, and did not look up. **He asked the question nobody has asked in eleven years — why is the box on the sheet and not on the card — and got *I do not know*, and called it the first true thing anybody has said to him about that wall in about eleven years, and then told him, in the office, with the kettle on, that it will not cost Marek Senn and it will not cost him and it will cost a boy of nineteen.** He was not on that yard when the first four words happened and he is not the man they happened to. His shoulder and the foreman's report are unchanged and still asked about by nobody.
- **THE FOUR WORDS.** Out, Wednesday of week 14, a doorway, a foreman on his way to his car talking past a man of 22 who had been on that yard nine weeks: **he's at the college now.** Back, Saturday of week 19, the same doorway, the same man, **Errol, who is not a bad man: you'll want the card.** And the arithmetic behind it: **six Saturdays back, one to get the card, five.** He was six and he is five. **No rule was broken in either direction, and the man who says it does not have to say it either, and it is arithmetic and not a discovery.** The Saturday refresher that comes to a yard on a Saturday morning is not on anybody's clock, because a Saturday man is not on a clock.
- **The price minuted on the Thursday of week 14 is PAID in week 20, in full, by Tam Arrick, and by nobody else.** A district office in Crown Terrace telephones the company's depot on a Thursday morning when there is nobody on the rota, and the boy has only ever had the number printed on a card. **He is asked where the release standard came from so it can go in a file, and he says *the card*, and she asks no second question, and he is believed, and he cannot say the true sentence, and he will not be able to on the Friday.** Nobody reassigns the price. Marek Senn's Chapter 68 refusal is a different thing and he pays for it himself.
- **The course form, posted on the Saturday of week 19, in a buff envelope, carried to a postbox on foot.** **The *employer* box empty on purpose. A declaration box — any condition, injury or medication affecting your ability to work at height — empty on purpose, with his forearm, his two tendons and his finger that does not close all of it available to write in it. £440, a photograph, a signature, and no employer.** He stood at that box on the Monday morning of week 19 and could not do it and got the bus to the end of the road and walked back, and told Marek Senn and nobody else.
- **The man at the Exchange with *continuity* in his title, and *none present* in a box in a regional office in Talia Venn's own hand**, and **nobody had rung her about it for a fortnight as of the Tuesday of week 19, and Chapter 68 opens with the call coming on the Friday of week 19.** **A form went to a regional office on the Wednesday of week 17 with a box that says *was a licensed holder present or within reach at the material time*, and no name in it, and the answer is *none present*, and the question now is how a resonance nurse on the Saltmarket route knows that.** She can name a man in about two seconds and will not, because that turns a member of staff into a fault and a boy into the reason. **THE INJURY ON THE TUESDAY OF WEEK 14 IS CANON AND IS NOT TAM'S TO WEAR: the front edge of the demonstrator frame, at knee height on a steel bar, came across about four inches and caught Rhoda Ilesanmi, 19, first year, on the inside of the left forearm from the wrist to about four inches below the elbow; she got her arm round about a second after it did; there are four stitches and a review in nine days. Toby Ansell is the boy in the second row and he pulled her forearm along the rail in one movement getting a notebook out from under it. TAM ARRICK'S TWO CUT TENDONS ARE A SEPARATE AND EARLIER COST, FROM A DRILL IN THE FIRST WEEK, AND HE WAS AT THE FAR END OF A LINE AND WAS ASKED TO LET GO AND DID NOT.**
- **The return-path register.** **Two documents about the same building in two offices: a regional return with *none present* in a district nurse's hand, and a department file with a member of staff's name on a supplier's page, dated the Monday of week 19. In about four years two people are going to try to join them up and one of them is going to manage it, and she has asked whether he and she are on the same side of that and he has said he does not know, and that is the first straight no he has given her about a fact instead of a principle, and it is the one that costs nobody anything.**
- **Talia Venn has been asked for three things in a year and refused three times, and the fourth refusal is the one she wanted.** *This is the one I wanted. That is the whole of it. There are nine of them and none of them was that one.* **She has asked him nothing about the job for ten days, on purpose, and says so in Chapter 70 and that he has had to stop himself twice.** **We had an hour. We still had an hour, and he noticed the tense and said nothing about it then and has not since, and it is the only sentence either of them has said in fourteen weeks that has moved.**
- **The review.** **Nobody has asked him about it in eleven days, in a shop, a depot, a car park or a corridor; he has not told one person that he does not know who calls it; condition six lapsed with condition three on the Wednesday of week 16; it is still sat at the end of term two, in the last week of that term, and there is nobody with standing to call it, and he has written the number down twice this month and done one single thing about it. NOBODY RINGS HIM BEFORE WEEK 30 AND NOBODY IN A DEPARTMENT MENTIONS IT.**
- **The second dependence, uncured.** Nkechi Osei, 33, **is on four on the Friday evening of week 18 and has told nobody.** *I have not changed the number. I have changed who is standing on the bank when it goes wrong and whose letterhead is on the form, and there is a very large difference between those two things and it is the only difference available to anybody in this city at the moment.* She asks for one sentence and it is smaller than he expects: **if you cannot come on a Monday, tell me on the Friday, in a sentence, before the Monday, and not afterwards.** There is still no person in this city whose job it is to be told that she is on four.
- **The flood season: about seven rounds before the year's out, every one a Monday at ten past six, nineteen men, one bar, a set of steps at the Cutbank, and the round is on the Monday and not on the Saturday.** The Monday at ten past six is clear on his rota and he has promised to say on the Friday if he cannot come.

## Corrections applied in the writer's pass of batch 0002 (Chapters 61–70)

**Twelve items. No plot moved, no scene cut, no ending altered, no canon reversed.** Full list in `workspace/volume-02/batch-0002/SUMMARY.md`. The ones a writer must not reintroduce:

- **Chapter 64 had no load-book entry at all and the run would have gone 64, 65, 66, 68 — a gap at 67, the chapter the batch turns on.** **Entry 67 is now on the page** with six headings, and it is the only entry in the batch whose stated reason is a pen.
- **Chapter 64's entry and the body of the same chapter disagreed about the count of working buildings (four in the body, five in the entry).** The body is the canon figure from Chapter 60. The entry now reads *four working buildings in six weeks and I have now been it a fifth time, in a fifth building*.
- **Chapter 70's entry gave Tam Arrick a loss of six Saturdays and a bed in a ward.** The six Saturdays are Reuben Daw's. Rewritten.
- **A drafting artefact survived into the delivered prose** (`*COSTELLO [sic] — no. KOSTA — did not look up.*` in Chapter 69). Corrected.
- **The district's practice standards office was on the first floor of a building in Bower Street**, which is Chapter 54's continuity-and-returns office, and would have merged two canon offices into one. **It is the third floor of the same building**, and the chapter says in one sentence that they have never been in the same corridor.
- **The thirty chairs' destination was a room off the roundabout**, which is the course building, and would have implied a company runs the district's Saturday. **It is a room on the third floor of the Bower Street building that has stood empty for eight weeks**, and the same floor as the office that could not take a complaint. Chapter 70's Ena Truslove and Chapter 67's Dessa Kwan were both corrected to it.
- **Chapter 66 had Dilys Fenwick use the words *the fourth beat* with no way of knowing them.** Marek Senn now says the phrase once in the room, having announced that he is about to, and she takes it up four minutes later. **It is the only time in the batch that the case's vocabulary leaves the book and enters a district office, and it leaves it in a woman's mouth.**
- **Chapters 62 and 70 carried the flagged *the week of week* tic.** Removed.
- **Month-names and a month-date.** *in November* (Ch 61), *in June* twice (Ch 67), *in the second week of September* twice (Ch 68, Ch 70) and *since September* (Ch 68) removed. *in September* in Chapter 62's Rusk speech is now **the September intake**, one of the two permitted calendar nouns. A month-date on the hand set's calibration card is now *the second Monday of last month*.
- **A pronoun slip:** Chapter 66 had *the one they have not got* for Sondra Mbeki. Now *she*.
- **Chapter 61 had given Nkechi Osei a forecast that is not hers and is close to Yvette Kohl's**, including the phrase *eleven months after it*, which is one of Kohl's calendrical figures and was cut from Chapter 55 in review because all three belong to the Chapter 75 midpoint. **The forecast is gone; Kohl's Chapter 55 forecast is now the only one of its kind in the case, and Nkechi spends her leverage on the thing she is actually owed.**

## Corrections applied in the repair pass of batch 0002's independent review

`reviews/volume-02-batch-0002.md`. **The review's headline finding was that this file, `state/current.md` and `batch-0002/SUMMARY.md` all certified a second repair pass of twenty-four items and recorded several of them as done when the delivered prose did not contain them.** Movement III would have inherited the corrected facts as settled canon while the chapters said the opposite, which is the most dangerous class of defect in the fleet. **Twenty-one items were wrong or self-contradictory in the prose and every one has been repaired in the chapters, not in the file.** The ones a writer must not reintroduce:

- **THE THIRTY CHAIRS WENT TO A ROOM OFF THE ROUNDABOUT IN THE PROSE, IN BOTH CHAPTERS.** It is **a room on the third floor of the Bower Street building that has stood empty for eight weeks a year**, for a Saturday, for about twenty women on a district's books — the same floor as the office that could not take a complaint, and Chapter 66 says so on the page. **The thirty-chair fix above was recorded as applied to Chapter 70's Ena Truslove and Chapter 67's Dessa Kwan and had been applied to neither.** The *form* legitimately still goes to a room off the roundabout: that is the course building and it is where a course form is read.
- **CHAPTER 66 PUT MAREK SENN INSIDE A BUILDING CHAPTER 54 SAYS HE HAS NEVER ENTERED**, and put the continuity-and-returns office on the second floor when Chapter 54 puts it on the **first**. **He has never been in the building, he was told on the Saturday of week 14 in four sentences not to be helpful, and the two offices have never shared a corridor.** He was also **a man of fifty** in the same chapter that calls him the twenty-two-year-old.
- **THE INCIDENT FORM HAS FIFTEEN BOXES AND ONE WORD SAID OTHERWISE.** `chapter-0065.md` read *eleven boxes long* — the sentence the chapter's title and its whole arithmetic rest on, and the one word *fifteen always fit and forty never do* breaks on.
- **THE FOURTH CONDITION, COMPLETE:** *may not demonstrate, teach, assess or assist at any practice in that department, on any premises of it, at any time, and it is not limited by hours, and it does not have an expiry date on it.* **Chapter 63 had it as *at any time in this term, and it does not have an expiry on it*, which is a self-contradiction and put an expiry on the one condition the outline makes non-tradeable.**
- **THE DAY-MAP ARITHMETIC, ONCE AND FOR ALL. Week 15 = Mon 30 – Sun 6. Week 16 = Mon 7 – Sun 13. Week 17 = Mon 14 – Sun 20. Week 18 = Mon 21 – Sun 27. Week 19 = Mon 28 – Sun 4. Week 20 = Mon 5 – Sun 11. Taking Monday of week 16 as day 0: Friday of week 18 is 18; Saturday of week 19 is 26; Sunday of week 19 is 27; Monday of week 20 is 28; Tuesday of week 20 is 29; Wednesday of week 20 is 30; Thursday of week 20 is 31.** Therefore: *seventeen days* for the yard wall (not eleven), *ten days* and *five days* and *three weeks* in Chapter 67 (not three nine-day intervals), *nine days* for the signed contract spoken on the Sunday (not eight), *five working days* of depot tenure on the Sunday, *two days* for a boy's card on the Thursday (not three, and not since yesterday), *nine days* for the Monday of week 19 spoken on the Wednesday of week 20, *ten days* for Talia Venn's silence about the job, *four weeks* is correct for the gap between the offer on the Friday of week 15 and the refusal on the Friday of week 19, and *four weeks* is correct for the Saturday drop-in seen from the Friday of week 19.
- **CHAPTER 62'S CLOCK.** 09:30 to 10:40. **Forty-five minutes on a bench, twenty-five in a chair**, the booklet and the speech heard through a door, the room's own count **forty-one people**. *Ninety minutes* is gone. **The chapter is retitled — *Twenty-Five to Ten* → *Through Wired Glass* — because the old title described a clock that could not exist.**
- **CHAPTER 63 WAS SPENDING CHAPTER 68'S BEATS.** The prompt reserves for Ch 68 the district statement Talia said *and* the Rhoda Ilesanmi return, and Ch 63 delivered both on the Tuesday call. **Ch 63 now says Keogh rang on the Wednesday of week 17 and that she gave an answer inside four seconds, and does not say the answer.** The return, the box, *none present*, the n/a distinction and the ringing from *continuity* are **Chapter 68's alone and are delivered for the first time there.**
- **CHAPTER 68 CLAIMED A FIRST TIME THAT WAS NOT A FIRST TIME, and stated the access asymmetry out loud, which the prompt forbids.** The list was named five days earlier, on the Tuesday, in Chapter 63. **The intervals are now *five days* and the sentence no longer says whose access it is** — only that he has seen the list and will not give a name off it.
- **CHAPTER 66 REPLIED TO A LINE TWELVE LINES LATER.** **Dilys Fenwick's observation about the hole in the design now comes first, Marek interrupts it, and her *that is the first time anybody has told me the reason in thirty-one years* lands where it belongs.** She also takes up *the fourth beat* **after** he has said it and refuses to write it in a district file.
- **CHAPTER 69'S YARD PRACTICAL WAS DATED THREE WAYS** — *tomorrow* on a Tuesday morning, an orphan line reading *Tomorrow. Not today.*, and a scene played and logged as the Tuesday. **The booking is on the sheet as *training hire, Tue* and the day is the Tuesday of week 20.** *The Sunday before last* was a Sunday two days earlier and is now *Sunday*; the Chapter 68 call happened **in a shop in Lattice Ward and not in Nine Bridges**; and **a load-book entry inverted the content of the refusal it was recording** — *I could not see it* for a list he could see and would not hand over.
- **LOAD-BOOK HEADINGS AND THE *about N* HEDGE.** Eight of ten entries ended on *Do not know*, which is a tic and not a motif; three were varied. **Eleven duration hedges were thinned across Chapters 65 to 70. The tic is thinned, not fixed, and is carried into the Movement III prompt as a live instruction.** Three entries that re-argued their own chapter's dialogue now close on something the scene did not contain.

## Two things flagged, not fixed, for the reviewer

- **Two years survive in Movement II, both inherited from delivered prose, and the batch rule says no year anywhere.** **`March`, once, in Chapter 62**, where Davan Rusk says he paid for the printing of revision 3-08 himself out of a teaching budget with eight credits in it — `chapter-0053.md`. **`2019`, once, in Chapter 64**, in the load-book reference to Aurel Kosta's shoulder, the shunt in the third wagon and the foreman's report that says *manual handling* — `chapter-0056.md`. **Both were kept deliberately, because removing them would have made Movement II contradict the two chapters that established the facts. Every other month-name and every other year is absent.** A later writer may cut them in both Volume 02 locations at once; they may not be cut in Movement II alone.
- **A state-file error was found while reading and is corrected below, not in the prose.** `state/character-state.md` credited **Rafi Pell** with losing six weeks of Saturdays on the Wednesday of week 14. **It is Reuben Daw's loss** — `chapter-0050.md:122` and `:276`, and the Volume 01 close. **Rafi has a new Saturday on a yard off the Meridian at twelve pounds and a card in a plastic sleeve, and he says out loud and quickly that the six weeks are a different loss by a different man and he will not pretend to somebody he got it back.** Chapter 67 uses the correct version.
