## THE CANON DECISION THIS MOVEMENT MADE, and it supersedes the outline's milestone line

**The sitting of the review is on the last Friday of week thirty, which is day 102 on the interval map, and Chapter 90 is on the Friday of week twenty-four, which is day 60. THE REVIEW IS THEREFORE NOT SAT IN CHAPTER 90.** `outline/volume-02.md` carries a milestone line reading *Ch 90 — the review. She is in the room as a witness and not as his.* That line is superseded by the same document's deviation 9, which says that Movement IV is **the review's notice, its preparation and the question of who calls a meeting nobody has to call**, and which dates the sitting to the end of term two in the week of week thirty. The outline's alternative sketch dated the sitting at the end of week nineteen, which cannot be the end of anything, and the outline's chapter map put Movement V in weeks twenty-two to twenty-four, which the prose overran; the prose is the authority.

**All consequences, recorded:**
- **The sitting is in Movement V, in the week of week thirty, on the Friday, at half past ten, in room four on the second floor of the Ninth Street block.**
- **Milestone 3 is spent there and not before: she is in the room as a witness and not as his, and she does not intervene once.** Chapter 90 does not spend it; it plants it, in the form of a nineteen-year-old choosing to speak for herself and refusing to have him speak for her.
- **Nothing before week thirty may put Talia Venn in that room in that role.**
- **No institution, sponsor, convener or panel is invented.** Condition six lapsed on the Wednesday of week sixteen and took the only documented basis for a body being convened to examine him. **The review has been called by a form, on a Friday morning, in about nine seconds, by a woman booking a room, and nobody has standing to call it and nobody chose it.**
- **Nobody rings Marek Senn about the review before Chapter 90, nobody in a department mentions it, and no office is invented to convene it.**

## Canon now fixed in Chapter prose (Chapters 81–90)

- **THE ASK, AND THE ANSWER, IN THREE SECONDS.** Four sentences in a corridor at ten past seven on the Monday of week twenty-two. **Petar Vance would have said yes on the first morning; what he has been waiting three weeks for is whether Marek Senn would ask.** **A SERVICE CALLOUT NEEDS A VAN, A METER BAG AND A MAN WHO CAN LOOK AT A RIG. A RELEASE CALLOUT NEEDS A CARD. The distinction is live and has teeth and it is the batch's engineering.**
- **THE BOX NUMBER REFUSAL, three parts and one mechanism.** Vance will not write the box number down, will not write *lot onward* on a company sheet, and will not ring the customer for a site contact. **A box number on a company sheet stops being a gate and starts being a record, and a record gets a number that is ours, and a number that is ours is a reference, and a reference is a thing that gets kept.** He has been a fitter for nineteen years, has driven to about four hundred sites and could not name one now, and that is not a failing. The sentence he writes is **Meridian corridor, customer site, service**, which is true and is on about nine hundred sheets in that building and contains nothing anybody can find him in. **Marek Senn is now the person who wanted that sentence.**
- **A BOOKING IS A GATE AND A GATE DOES NOT CARE WHO IS ON THE INVOICE, and the whole of the batch's horror is in the same paragraph.** A hi-vis with a clipboard comes out of the cabin and does not do the thing hi-vises with clipboards do, because the booking has already done it. Nobody looked at the lanyard, nobody asked for a card, nobody asked whether he had done a course. **The only condition in the whole process was a line in a grid with a date on it, written by a fitter who did not think about it for a second.**
- **THE OUT-OF-HOURS LINE IS HIS FROM THE MONDAY OF WEEK TWENTY-TWO**, and it is a mobile on a rota of four: Petar Vance, Denny Molloy, a man off sick, and Marek Senn. **The fourth man has not been answering it since about half past four on the Wednesday afternoon of week twenty-one because of the bundle, and Vance is going to keep leaving him on it, and the only thing which puts it back is somebody noticing he was still on it.** A shutter in a Saltmarket lock-up, a plastic cap on the left-hand end of a barrel, nine minutes, and a woman who asks who to ask for next time.
- **DAI PRITCHARD, nineteen years, the fourth man, named in Chapter 88 and not before.** He did not answer it because of the paper, he read his own line on a counter in a canteen on the Wednesday morning of week twenty-one, and he sat in his van for about an hour and decided he was not going to be a man you could ring. **He has not resigned, has not stopped doing his days, has not been rude to anybody, and is not being unreasonable. He decides on Sunday night and telephones on the Monday, the Monday of week twenty-five.** Marek Senn's answer to him, in eleven words of mechanism: **a company is not a wall — it is four men on a rota and a woman in an office and about nine hundred people who have never read a page of anything, and a piece of paper with a man's name on it is a decision about whether a company can send an invoice.** He said it was the first time anybody in that outfit had said the wall and the rota in the same sentence.
- **THE CONTINUITY OFFICE LETTER, four paragraphs, a reference number, signed on the Sunday of week twenty-one and posted on the Monday and on the mat on the Tuesday of week twenty-two at ten to seven.** The count is 4,106 / 940 / 211 / four assessors, **two of them part time**, and the part-time half matters not for workload but because there is nobody to contradict a sentence said in a corridor on a Tuesday. **The last line says: this letter records a telephone conversation, it is not a determination, it creates no obligation on any party, and it should not be produced in any proceeding without this office being asked first.** **It goes in the drawer under the bench with page four, and there is a pencil line on the drawer front with the reference on it, and a pencil line on a drawer front is not a document.**
- **THREE THINGS IN THE DRAWER UNDER THE BENCH: the letter that says it should not be produced, page four of a company's induction booklet with no page before the page on it, and a booking confirmation for room four on the last Friday of week thirty. Two of the three carry a reference number. Nothing may be produced out of that drawer to anybody.**
- **RAE TOLLEY, one of the eleven.** She has read a line with her name and her induction date and her site and the word NONE typed into it, has never had a condition in her life and has never worked above a ladder, has counted four copies and been told about a fifth in a car park, and has already sent a written request for one page of her file to a private address and to a district office in Crown Terrace in four minutes of temper. **She wants it destroyed, in writing, from an address, and that is coming: the letter going to all eleven says the note we hold about them is destroyed at the end of the term on request in writing, and it is not a policy.** She also wants to know whether NONE is a true thing or a lazy thing, and is refused in eleven seconds and then given the true one: **there is a box and nobody has asked you anything, and the box got what it got because asking is a telephone call and typing is a key on a keyboard.** She has decided she is not going to be one of the eleven who rings a company back and has asked for that to be written down somewhere, and it is.
- **TALIA VENN'S RETURN, on the Tuesday of week twenty-two, at eleven: *the requested material is not held by a company.* Eleven words, and a box underneath it that says *reason if not held*, and nothing in the box, and a date and a signature.** The reason is the fifteen words in a district minute from the fifteenth week and it is not in the return because if it is, then in about two years a regional office has both halves. **Her own arithmetic, said out loud: a woman does not get to keep two facts she cannot explain in a year without becoming the woman who keeps facts.** She has one about a review nobody has standing to know and one about a blank box, and she has told him both and can hand neither to anybody. **Her eleven-line answer to Roisin Keogh went at twenty past eight on the Tuesday morning, before anybody asked her to, dated in her own hand.**
- **THE FIRST LIE IN THE CASE, and it is about a person.** On the Tuesday morning she asks whether he is going to be in a room on the Saturday, having told him on Sunday that she had worked it out and was not going to tell him, and having said out loud that she was going to ask anyway and was not going to be a woman who says she did not. **He says no, in one word, in a second and a half, with no reason.** He was on the third floor from ten past ten until half past one on the Saturday. **He tells her on the Wednesday of week twenty-four, at about ten past ten at night, after eleven days, and she says it is the worst possible time to say a thing like it and the only time he has got.** Her instruction: **do not lie to me about a Saturday again — not because of the case, because if you lie to me about one more ordinary afternoon then I will not be able to tell the difference between the part of me that is careful and the part of me that is proud.**
- **RUSLAN PETRIC'S FORM, WITH NO TARGET ON THE QUESTION, AND NOBODY FROM THE COMPANY IN THE ROOM.** The question: *we hold a training bundle for a supervised release sequence, reference 4-19, modules one and two. We hold a supplier's assembly record. On that record, the field for the originating practice is blank. Please state what your organisation holds in relation to the practice from which that sequence was derived.* **No supplier's name, no lot, no date range — on purpose, so that a man cannot write a sentence only true about a target.** Oren Vey said he had been asked to trace the field four times in nineteen years and had not traced it, and that if he had put a target on the form a man could write a sentence that would be true about the target and quoted in four years.
- **THE INTAKE INSTRUCTION, ARRIVED AT INDEPENDENTLY BY A THIRD YEAR, IN ABOUT NINE SECONDS, AND IT IS THE WHOLE OF VEY'S REPLACEMENT.** **If the answer is that they hold nothing, will you write it down in the person's own words, unedited, with the date and the name of the person who asked, and a copy to me, and nothing in an intake system that has got a reference number on it that I cannot see.** The operations director wrote it down in a car and read it back. **Ruslan Petric said yes and said the cost out loud first, in the right order, so that it could not be used against either of them later: a document with his name at the top of it and nothing else, in about four years, read as a company asking a student about a practice and getting a blank; and being the fourth thing in a room; and the reason he is doing it anyway, which is that a question has come before an answer for the first time and nobody took a vote.** He did not fill it in on the day and did not fill it in on the Thursday.
- **THE SHEET THROUGH THE BOX ON WEAVER STREET, Friday of week twenty-two at about four minutes to five.** Four lines and a signature in ballpoint on a playing card, cut out of a box in his own rucksack and written at a counter in the practical building with four people watching. It asks the company to answer in its own words, on its own paper, with the date and the name of the person who asked, and to tell him which box, **and if you cannot answer it, I am asking you to say that you cannot, in the same way, and I am asking for that in the same envelope, because a company that says it holds nothing has told me something and a company that does not reply has told me nothing and both of those are worth having and only one of them is worth having by Friday.** His line from the Wednesday of week sixteen — *the difference between saying it to a postbox and saying it to a room of people is about nine seconds long* — is reused deliberately and it is his.
- **NKE OSEI'S MAN, AND THE FOREMAN ON THE MERIDIAN, AND THE NINE WORDS, and the fact that nobody will ever join them.** A man of about twenty-six stands on the wall leg from the Monday of week twenty-two and has not been told what he is standing in, and asks nine words on the bank and is told it is a thing that had happened to him, and has not asked a second question. **Ozzie Wray, a foreman of about thirty-four on a kitchen line in Nine Bridges, eleven years in the job, told Ruslan Petric on the Wednesday afternoon of week twenty-two in nine words that the word is for the pallet that is not ready, and neither man made a connection and neither man ever will.** A foreman on the Meridian has never wanted a card and has never been asked for one in nineteen years. **Marek Senn's load book says it: a person who is good at a thing and has never once been asked where they learned it will describe that thing correctly in about nine words, to a stranger, forever, and will never connect it to anything, and that is the whole of why the shape is stable and why it is good engineering.**
- **THE SITE, AND WHAT IS ON IT.** Meridian consolidation point, lot onward, off the corridor. About four days of rigging outstanding, a gate that asks for nothing, nine men who are not the company's, a prefab office with a desk, a chair, a heater, a kettle and a filing tray.
  - **A proximity switch on gate two with a cracked ferrite clip behind the gland, four months old, invisible from the front**, and the sheet reads *ferrite clip, cracked* and not *proximity switch intermittent*.
  - **A laminated card on the control post at the top of the run, two cloudy cable ties, one with a small buckle somebody put there, four steps in a typeface that has been out of use in this city for a very long time, and the line RELEASE IS GIVEN BY ANOTHER VOICE. DO NOT RELEASE ON YOUR OWN JUDGEMENT. No name, no date, no company mark, no version number. It has been there at least twenty years. The foreman's fella said it was there when he came. Nobody has ever asked what is on the other two cards by the office and the man who put them up is not coming back.** The foreman's men do what the man on the radio says and wait when he does not say it, about nine times out of ten that is the right call, and he cannot write down the tenth time and has been there nine years. **Marek Senn did not touch it. He wrote *ask* on a docket and then wrote the word out.**
  - **A pale pink carbon goods-in sheet, nine deliveries, four months, and every one of the nine signatures a printed name in a typeface in a box**, which is what a system writes when a man on a wagon signs a sheet and a desk reconciles the two and the desk wins.
  - **The assembly record, printout, nine pages, top of the tray, brought out because it was in the way.** The upstream field is blank. *Meridian consolidation point, lot onward* is typed about two inches below it in a stores clerk's hand. **A box number is written in biro, in a different hand, by a different person, about two years ago, because a man on a wagon asked where the thing came from.** It is the only address left in the case.
  - **A barrier with an aftermarket safety edge and a dip switch set two positions out, fixed in nine minutes and written up as *advised customer in writing*** — a customer's part, a customer's gate, and the first true thing he has put in a company's document in five weeks, which he stood over for a second and considered taking out and did not.
- **THE COST OF NOT WRITING THE BOX NUMBER DOWN, and it is the first thing in the case he could be called for.** A company technician goes to a site because there is a fault, so if he goes back without one then somebody in a depot gets told there was a fault at that address on a date. **So he booked a lamp with about eleven months on the ballast that is going to fail in a fortnight, and the tray is the company's and goes at the end of the year, and the foreman said he would keep it and that he was not doing it for Marek Senn but because in about four years somebody will come through that gate with a different company's name on their lanyard and ask where the gear came from.** A piece of paper in a rucksack pocket holds four lines that are not a number and not a name and not an address. **He has told nobody.**
- **YOU LOT ARE QUICK THIS YEAR.** A foreman of about forty-four, nine years on site, genuinely and uncomplicatedly pleased that a man in a lanyard has come to look at his gear, telling a joke about a man in a fluorescent vest with a clipboard who could not say what he had come for and got told to get off his site. **There is nothing wrong with that sentence. It is the best possible outcome of a system in which a person with a lanyard comes and looks at a thing, and it is the sentence the whole of this case exists to make impossible, and the reason it is impossible to argue with is that neither man is lying.** It is in the load book and not in the chapter.
- **NIA BRAMHALL, about thirty-one, training diary for the district, about nine hundred of these to book between now and the end of the term.** She reads three questions off a sheet and then stops reading and asks a fourth off her own head. **Have you ever had a fourth beat at all.** And the answer to that, unedited: **nobody has, that is what the course is** — about four hundred people have done modules one and two and the number who came in with a fourth beat already in their hands is zero, about a third of them have a story like his and two thirds have nothing and think they have something, and she has watched about four people not hear that it is the good news. **She is not the author and is not a villain and is not lying and is going to be right for the rest of her working life in a way that does not help.** She also says, unasked, that a card says what you can do and what you can confirm to somebody else, that it is not a certificate that says you cannot work without it, that people work without it every day and are perfectly safe, and that there is a foreman on the Meridian who has not got one and has never wanted one and would tell you it is a waste of a morning. **And a warning about a competitor's weekend course with a beautiful piece of card worth nothing, and a request to ring and report it.**
- **RAFI PELL SAYS YES, AND HE GETS IT, AND HE IS DELIGHTED.** Two days, a Tuesday and a Wednesday of week twenty-four, eight to four, on the clock, a card typed and issued at the end of the second day, free, a form of identity required. **A card is a condition of a release callout, and a release callout is not twelve pounds an hour, and that is the whole of the reason he rang on a Friday afternoon rather than on a Sunday night, and he told the woman that in the first ten seconds without being asked and said that if that was the wrong reason she could hang up.** Nobody has forgiven him the word *variant* and he has not asked anybody to.
- **THE STATE OF *NOBODY HAS ASKED ME FOR ANYTHING*, WHICH HE ASKED MAREK SENN TO HOLD AND WHICH HE RELEASED HIMSELF.** He named the minute: about four minutes past six on the Friday evening of week twenty-two, when a woman read three questions off a sheet and then stopped reading and asked one off her own head. **He said he is not doing it because of Marek Senn, and that he cannot be a man who is not asked for anything and is on a list.** He said it in the first person with his name at the end, as he has done twice before, and told Marek Senn he has never once done it to make a room go quiet. **He counted to two in the middle of the shop without being asked and without meaning to.**
- **THE BATCH'S TECHNICAL CORE, said out loud for the first time since Chapter 75, to a man in a doorway and not to a room.** About eleven parts, which the boy in the room counts by clause and not by word: **scope, he is in scope, I confirm your scope, do not release on your own judgement, if I do not say it you withdraw and you report it.** Said about nine times a day in a room on a trading estate by a woman with a lanyard and eleven years of a job, and the wait is the practice and not politeness. **And the batch's structural finding, which is not a discovery and is his: the card is not what is doing the work. The card is the receipt, and a receipt is what you show a company that has stopped answering its telephone, and the entire edifice is standing on about nineteen men in this city who have never been issued anything and have never once been asked to account for it.** He may not say it in a room and does not and is not asked to. **It is in the book and not in a company.**
- **THE INSTRUCTOR'S SENTENCE, said to a room of about eleven and given to Marek Senn word for word in a doorway.** **Most of you are going to go out of here with a card and you are going to be safer, and it is not because the card makes you safer, it is because the card is the only thing in this city that puts a person between you and a decision you should not be making on your own at two in the morning. And in about four years somebody is going to tell one of you that all of this was a paper exercise, and I want you to be able to say that you were in a room on this date and a woman with a lanyard said it out loud, and you will be able to say it because there will be eleven of you in it, and eleven is not a number anybody can be alone in.** **Marek Senn has been trying to build that number for a term out of four people who can end a share and a woman in a sling and a first-year's forearm, and the arithmetic has never once produced eleven, and she produced it in one sentence at no cost to herself, and he is not going to be the one who says so, and the load book calls that the most expensive thing anybody has ever not said to him.**
- **THE CARD MADE AT A KITCHEN TABLE.** A woman of about thirty-one, four nights, a kitchen, a lamination, and a strip of tape across one corner. **Not a copy of anything** — not off a company's booklet, not off a district's pack, nobody sent it to her. Four steps, and they are correct, and the line at the bottom says *WAIT FOR THE WORD. IF NO WORD, WITHDRAW AND REPORT*, and she is right, and the reason she is right is that the worst thing on a shift is the pair who both go at once, and she knows that from the floor and not from a document. **The same slot, eleven years old, portable, free, eleven pence, the name in it a woman of thirty-one who made it at her own kitchen table and is nobody's, and by next spring about four hundred pockets, and nobody has to sell one to anybody and nobody has to author it.** Marek Senn stood next to it with his hand on the table and said the four steps were right and said the line at the bottom was the best sentence on the table, and the woman was pleased, **and the load book says that was the worst four minutes of his term, because he was kind, and being kind is the shape the slot has when it is made by a man.**
- **AND, AN HOUR LATER, IN THE SAME ROOM: a strip of notepaper on the floor by the low bed that says ONE OF YOU SAYS IT OUT LOUD, AND IT IS NOT THE SAME PERSON EVERY TIME, AND SWAP OVER.** No logo, no date, nine seconds at the start of a morning, written by a woman of about forty-four for her own Saturday. **Two of the same shape in one room, an hour apart, one made by a woman of forty-four for her own room and one made by a woman of thirty-one at her own kitchen table, and the whole distance between them is that one has been read out loud about thirty times and the other one by nobody.**
- **DESSA KWAN'S NINE HUNDRED WORDS ARE FINISHED AND ARE NOT GOING ANYWHERE.** Written in week twenty on a Tuesday, a Wednesday and a Thursday, about a room in Nine Bridges and a man at the front of it and what happens to a person who goes in. **She is never going to hand it over to Marek Senn and the reason is the best thing anybody has said to him in a term: the second a document exists, the room it is about becomes a thing that was done, and that is a man in a room who got somebody hurt with her name on the front of it, and she put it there, and the thing she was trying to do was stop that room being a room.** And: **she has been afraid of a document in the correct direction for the first time in her life, and he has been careful in the wrong direction for a term, and every document he touches comes out shaped like a case.** He did not say thank you, did not say that was fair, and did not tell her she had the wrong end of it, and she noticed all three.
- **GRATITUDE HAS GOT NOWHERE TO GO, SO IT DOES NOT MOVE, and this time he is the other half of the sentence.** He carried thirty plastic chairs down from the third floor of a building in Bower Street and is the first person who ever has. Ena Truslove counted them and said it is the fourth time the chairs have gone and the third time she has said yes in about four seconds and that she has not been thanked once, and that the district woman carried eleven up herself last week and did not mention it and the eleven are hers and not the nursery's and there is no box anywhere for the eleven. **She says to him: you are not thanked either. Good. Then that is two of us.**
- **ENID SARKIS'S LIST, five lines, and the fifth line is a fall.** Four lines in a small round slanted hand on an A4 sheet folded and unfolded until it has gone soft: cannot carry a full kettle with the right hand, left hand only, started about the same time as the plate; cannot get the top off a jar, screw driver and not a can opener, and I want that on the list because it is the kind of thing people think is a joke; cannot put a coat on the hook by the door, and the hook is too high and it was too high before the plate; cannot hold a plate and a fork at the same time so I do not eat anything I have to cut. **A fifth line in a different ink, added about ten days ago: she went down on the stairs on the Sunday before last and did not tell anybody because there is nobody to tell, and this piece of paper is the only place it exists in the world.** The cause is not a fall: **the fourth step from the bottom is loud and somebody has been coming up and down those stairs at about six in the morning for four weeks, and that is him.** She is sixty-eight, the plate has been on every day for three years, and she is not going to be examined about a step.
- **WHAT SHE ASKED FOR AND DID NOT ASK FOR TWICE.** Somebody whose job it is, once, for a wrist like hers. **She asked on the Friday of week thirteen, seven days late, and he told her the answer before she had finished and then gave her the reason, and she said she would take the reason and not the apology, and not asking for the apology was asking him for the only thing she has wanted for three years, which is somebody to be told.** On the Sunday of week twenty-two she told him she was not going to ask again and that she could see he had something ready, and what he had ready was a piece of paper with a name on it and there was no name on it.
- **LEAVE THE DATE OFF IT.** He asked to copy the list out and said why badly and she said yes, and he wrote five lines in his own small upright hand, **and she told him to leave the date off it, and the reason is that if there is a date on it then in about four years somebody is going to use that date to say it has been three years and not looked at, and a date turns a woman's patience into a company's exposure.** No date at the top, no date at the bottom, about two inches of the docket left empty on purpose. **It is in the drawer under the bench. That is the only time in a term he has copied a document about a person on purpose having understood exactly what it would do in four years, and the only time anybody has authorised it.**
- **THE TWO WOMEN ON HER COUNTER, and the system, which is us.** One on the Tuesday with a shoulder that has not been right since before the term and a whole routine about the bag and about forty Tuesdays of silence. One on the Friday who does not come any more, and went to a clinic in Crown Terrace and was put on a list, and Enid Sarkis does not know what the list is for and has not asked, **and told him that is not restraint, that is her not wanting to be a person who asks, because she is sixty-eight and has two bad knees and does not have the energy for another person's answers.** She has written down neither of them and has told him both, for herself, and said the reason out loud: she wanted to. **There is no system in this city. There is a woman under his own flat and a man in a shop and about two hundred thousand other people each carrying two and telling nobody, and the only reason he knows about his is that she asked him in week thirteen because she had nobody else and had been carrying hers for about a year.**
- **SEVEN PEOPLE HAVE NOW INDEPENDENTLY INVENTED THE SAME PROTECTION AND NONE OF THEM HAS MET ANOTHER.** A woman of sixty-eight with a list and no date on it. A woman of twenty-seven with nine hundred words in a drawer. A co-operative with a ruled empty box headed UPSTREAM LICENSOR. A records clerk under that box who does not know the three suppliers exist. A man of twenty-three with a sheet through a postbox at four minutes to five. A first-year who will read two hundred and forty words out loud and let somebody else transcribe them badly. A foreman of about thirty-four who described a confirmation in nine words to a stranger and will never know what it was. **Marek Senn has met all of them, has not put them in a room, and has refused to call it a pattern, because a pattern gets a reference number and a reference number tells the people who wrote in it that they wrote in it.**
- **THE EXCHANGE, the first Wednesday of week twenty-four, and the forty-first line.** Six items, four minutes each, a tin, eleven people. **Ena Truslove read her own card out in full, in block capitals, signed, and the book went from forty lines to forty-one and does not move again this term.** A boiler. **A man of about fifty-two in a district jacket said there is no such thing as a district, there is a technique and it is being handed round and there is nobody putting their name to it** — a college in the summer, a district on the Saturday, a company on the Saturday, a man on his crew, a woman on his crew going in a fortnight — **and eleven people went two seconds quieter and nobody said a word, and Marek Senn could not answer, and the only sentence that would answer begins *a man who is profitable from it told me in advance*, and there is no version of that which does not put a man's name in a room.** Bea Nunn said item four. Ines Sarr's item got four answers and one of them was worse and right: the last person in that room is always somebody who has already been hurt, and you cannot hire that, and in four years somebody will read a file about that room and the word they will use is culture. **A card about a first-year's forearm was read out in full and entered with no name in the first column, because a boy did not want to be named.** Bea Nunn's own item: every one of about four hundred cards in nine years was a thing somebody was frightened of, and the one on Monday was annoyed rather than frightened, and she does not know what to do with it and is not going to build a process, because a book that gets a process for every kind of card in it becomes a system, and a system is a thing a room can be asked to fill in, and she is sixty-one and will not be here to find out. **A district office asked for everyone in the district who has done modules one and two in four years, and Bea Nunn refused, and the reason is the best anybody has given in this case in a term: the day this room is a place where a register lives, the two hundred people who put a card in this tin for nine years because it was the only room they trusted find out that it is a source, and she loses it and does not get it back. The tin is not a registry and the book is not a register.**
- **MAREK SENN'S OWN CARD IS IN THE TIN.** About seventy words and a signature, in the first person: **there is a card on a control post at a site off the Meridian that nobody in twenty years has asked where it came from, and there is a card on a table in Bower Street that a woman made at her own kitchen table, and they are the same card, and there is nobody in this city whose job it is to say so.** Read at the first Wednesday of the month, which is the Wednesday of week twenty-eight, in four minutes, Bea Nunn's name in the fourth column and his in the first, in his own words, with a date he has not written and she is not going to ask him for. **She said it is the first card in that tin in nine years that has come in from a man with a job, and she would rather he had put it in a tin than in a corridor.** **The book does not move before the first Wednesday of the month, which is the Wednesday of week twenty-eight on the four-week spacing, and nothing in Movement V may enter an item in it before then.**
- **ASHA REED'S SENTENCE, said standing up, nine first years and one voice, and it is the fourth time.** *The case is Senn's and the technique is nobody's yet.* **And then: in three weeks four separate people have asked her whether they should be in a room, and she has said no four times, and the fifth person to be asked about a room in three weeks is her, and she is nineteen, and she is a student, and she is not a body in a room for anybody.** And the sentence she worked out on her own: **if anybody in this room is ever asked to put their name on a piece of paper about this, and there is nobody in the room above you, then the answer is not no. The answer is who is the last person in this room and are they findable, and if you cannot answer that in about four seconds then you are not being asked, you are being used, and the difference is about a sentence and you can check it.**
- **THE REVIEW, AND THE MECHANISM, AND THERE IS NO SPONSOR.** A booking clerk on the Ninth Street block at eleven minutes past ten on the Friday morning of week twenty-four: **there is a booking on the system for the last Friday of the term at half past ten in the morning in room four on the second floor, it has determination on it, and he is down as the person it is about, and she does not know what it is about and books nine rooms a week and four of them are about a boiler.** He asked for the reference in writing, which nobody has asked her for in about four years, and **the name on the form is not a name, it says *practical department*.** Three boxes, the middle one ticked, and somebody who thought about it for about a second and a half and then went back to a form with about nine things on it. **No terms of reference, no convener, no panel list, and a date of half past ten rather than ten because the form says half past and she always goes with the form.** He said the true sentence, did not explain it, did not fill it in, and got the reference by email at about ten past two **and printed it, which is the first thing he has printed in six weeks and he printed it for exactly the wrong reason, which is that he wanted a piece of paper in his own hand on a thing with a reference number on it.** **The distance to it: sixty-one days from the Sunday of week twenty-one, sixty from the Monday of week twenty-two, and six weeks from the Friday of week twenty-four.**
- **THE CHAIR.** There is a chair at the table with his name on a card on it, because the booking clerk is not the person who lays the chairs out on the day, and if he rang on the Friday morning and asked for a chair he would be a man who cannot sit down and in about four years that would be in somebody's notes. **So he rang the booking office at ten past three and asked for the chair to be laid, and a woman he had never spoken to typed a line on a booking in about four seconds and said she had never done that in four years and had not thought about it for more than about four seconds and would like that on the record in the same way he would like it.** **It is the first thing in a term that has cost him nothing and the only thing he has done in a term that is not a refusal.**
- **RHODA ILESANMI, NINETEEN, TWO HUNDRED AND FORTY WORDS, AND THE SIXTH PROTECTION.** She came into the shop at half past two and said she is not going to say no to her own return being read, and that she decided it in about a second, and that the reason is that **if she says no then the only thing anybody gets to say about her forearm is a sentence somebody else wrote about her, and that is how the whole thing got started in week fourteen.** She has written two hundred and forty words in the first person and is not giving them to him and not giving them to the department and not posting them anywhere. **She is going to read them out on the Friday, and if anybody wants it in a file they can write it down while she is saying it, and then the record is theirs and not hers** — because **anybody can refuse to hand over a document, and handing one over is what everybody in this city thinks the brave thing is, and the second it is in a file it is a thing that was done with a title on it, and about four years later somebody reads the title and never gets to the two hundred words.** She knows somebody will get nine words wrong and one of the nine wrong in a way that matters, and that is the trade. **She has decided in advance that she is not going to be sad about it in that room and has asked for that to be in a book, and she is not going to be the one who says his name, because his name is not her forearm and she did not ask him for it.** And: **you are not going to be in that room, because the person it is about is never the person who gets a seat, and there is no rule about that anywhere and I have looked, and I looked on Monday in a library for about an hour and I am telling you that because I am nineteen and nobody in this has told me I could not look.**
- **THE INSTRUCTION FOR THE LAST FRIDAY OF THE TERM, given to him by Talia Venn on the Friday evening at a quarter to six, which he rang on a Friday, which she has not done in fourteen months, and she said so before he could.** **If anybody in that room says the word *evidence* to you about a box somebody else filled in, say that you were not in the room and you have not got anything, do not explain what you have got, and do not say *I know who was*, because in about two seconds it becomes the only sentence anybody remembers and it finishes the college.** Then: **ring me on a Tuesday and tell me about it afterwards and I will not be a woman about it — and I will be a woman about it in about four years and that is a different Friday.** And why she is not going to be in the room, in two parts: a mechanism — a nurse explaining a first-year's forearm in front of the first-year, and in four years a return read as a case with two names on it — **and then the other one: she has been waiting about four months for a person to say *I do not know who calls it*, and she said in week twenty-one that she would know the moment she heard it, and it turns out she did not want the review, she wanted to be the person who knew there was a review, and that is nearly the same thing and it is not the same thing.**
- **THE BATCH'S SHAPE, in one paragraph at the foot of the last entry.** Eight things in ten days: a door, a form with no target, a free course, a card made at a kitchen table, a strip of notepaper on a floor, a five-line list with no date, two hundred and forty words to be read aloud, and a booking with a chair laid by a stranger who had never done it in four years. **Not one of them cost anybody anything. Not one of them was voted for. Every single one of them arrived. A prohibition does not need a sponsor and a review does not either, and a review has been called by a form on a Friday morning by a woman booking a room, and Marek Senn has got a chair.**

**Eleven items, all caught in the writing pass against the day map, the interval map and the previous batch's review. No plot moved, no scene cut, no ending altered, no canon reversed.** The ones a later writer must not reintroduce:

All five repaired in the prose, and **every load-book paragraph now closes its italic label inside its own line, which is the manuscript's convention and which the previous batch established.**

## Three things flagged, not fixed, for the reviewer

- **The batch is 51,400 words, which is 4% above Movement III's 49,500 and 5% below Movement II's 54,200, and the range across the ten chapters is 4,059 in Chapter 85 to 5,953 in Chapter 88.** The first draft was 40,000 words and about 11,000 were added, in seven blocks, in the places the previous batch's own note named as honest: inside the out-of-hours rota, inside the leak's consequences, inside a service callout, inside a Saturday at twelve pounds, inside a room on a third floor, and inside a kitchen at a table. **No block was added to a load-book entry and no block was added to make a chapter longer; the two longest additions are the Continuity Office reading in Chapter 82 and the site induction and the control post in Chapter 84.** **If a reviewer wants a shorter batch, the two least load-bearing blocks are the market exchange in Chapter 89, which is the fifth time the belief about the technique is on the page, and the second out-of-hours call in Chapter 81, which is a consequence of a consequence.**
- **The batch's whole argument is delivered by seven people who never meet, and the risk is that the reader takes that for mysticism.** It is not, and the load book says so in four places, and the last entry names all eight arrivals in a paragraph. **The reviewer should check that none of the seven is treated as an insight rather than a decision, and that no word appears that would put them in a room.**
- **The *Do not know* heading is at two of ten, down from five, and eleven consecutive entries are now mostly about Marek Senn rather than about other people** — the opposite of the drift Chapter 80 of batch 0003 admitted to on the page. **The correction was asked for and has been made, and the volume's own method is other people, so Movement V should let two or three entries go back.**

# VOLUME 02 — MOVEMENT V, THE VOLUME CLOSE (Chapters 91–100, weeks 25–30)

## Verified in-world timeline of Chapters 91–100 (Movement V, and the term's end)

**Monday of week 16 is day 0, and the whole of the arithmetic is this and must not be re-derived:** Sunday of week 19 is 27. Wednesday of week 21 is 37. Friday of week 21 is 39. Sunday of week 21 is 41. Monday of week 22 is 42. Friday of week 22 is 46. Friday of week 24 is 60. **Friday of week 25 is 67. Wednesday of week 28 is 86. Friday of week 29 is 95. The last Friday of week thirty — the sitting, the recall and the end of the term — is 102.**

- **Ch 91 is day 63**, three days after the Friday of week twenty-four and **thirty-nine days** before the sitting.
- **Ch 92 is day 64.** **Ch 93 is day 86**, twenty-three days after Chapter 91, twenty-six after Chapter 90, and **sixteen before the sitting.**
- **Ch 94 is day 87** and **Ch 95 is day 95, which is exactly seven days before the sitting.** **Ch 96 is day 98, four days before. Ch 97 is day 99, three days before. Ch 98 is day 100, two days before. Ch 99 and Ch 100 are day 102.**
- **The Exchange and Asha Reed's sentence run on four-week spacing from the Wednesday of week sixteen: 16, 20, 24, and therefore 28, and NOT 27.** The Wednesday of week 28 is day 86 and the Wednesday of week 24 is day 58, which is exactly twenty-eight days. **The next first Wednesday is the Wednesday of week 32, which is day 114, which is twelve days after the last Friday of week thirty** — and that is the whole reason Bea Nunn's book closes for the term on the Wednesday of week 28 with a man's shoulder in the tin.
- **Weeks 26 and 27 have no chapter and no load-book entry, and that is a fact of the batch, not a gap.** It is written into Chapter 94's opening: three weeks in which twenty-eight jobs were done, twenty-six of them right first time, and nobody asked him anything.
- **The term runs Monday of week nineteen to the last Friday of week thirty and the term and the review are the same day.** The job was arranged to the same Friday and nothing was arranged after it.
