NINE of the ten are about somebody other than Marek Senn, and the one that is about him is the failure.** The *about N* duration hedge is 424 constructions across 58,100 words, forty-two a chapter, 7.3 per thousand words, from thirty-four in Chapter 93 to sixty-five in Chapter 100, by the counting rule in `workspace/volume-02/batch-0005/SUMMARY.md`.

## THE THREE CANON DECISIONS MOVEMENT V MADE, and they supersede the outline

1. **THE SITTING IS ON THE LAST FRIDAY OF WEEK THIRTY, AT HALF PAST TEN, IN ROOM FOUR ON THE SECOND FLOOR, AND MILESTONE 3 IS SPENT IN CHAPTER 99 AND NOT BEFORE.** The outline's relationship-milestone line *Ch 90 — the review* is superseded by the outline's own deviation 9. Chapter 90 carries the notice, the reference in writing, a chair laid by a stranger, a first-year choosing to speak for herself, and the one sentence he is forbidden to say. **The sitting is Chapter 99, and the milestone is spent in nine words: *I am correcting a number, and I am not intervening.* She is in the room as a witness and not as his and she does not intervene once.** Nothing before Chapter 99 may put her in that room in that role, and no institution, sponsor, convener or panel is invented — the whole point is that a woman booking a room called it by accident, correctly, in about nine seconds on the Friday morning of week twenty-four, and that on the Friday morning of week thirty a woman of fifty-eight with a folder rang the practical department and was told they do not know what the form is, and wrote it down.
2. **THE EXCHANGE AND ASHA REED'S SENTENCE ARE ON FOUR-WEEK SPACING FROM THE WEDNESDAY OF WEEK SIXTEEN AND THEREFORE FALL ON WEEK TWENTY-EIGHT.** The Wednesday of week 27 is the third Wednesday of a month and is not a first Wednesday of anything. **The book is at forty-two lines at the end of the term and does not move again this term. A man's shoulder is in the tin and enters as line forty-three at the first Wednesday of week thirty-two, twelve days after the last Friday of the term.** Bea Nunn declined to open the book in between and will not open it on a Friday.
3. **THERE IS NO SUCH THING AS A NON-HINGED MAINTENANCE SEQUENCE, AND THAT IS A FACT OF THE WORLD AND NOT OF THE OUTLINE.** The series sketch's volume climax has Marek and Talia demonstrating one. Volume 01's guardrail is that he cannot invent a practice, Rafi Pell has no fourth beat at all, and the volume's end state is Stage 2. **So the demonstration is built in Chapter 92 out of existing things and is found not to exist, is attempted in public in Chapter 97, fails, and the finding is brought by four other people in Chapter 98, and he sits in a room and watches.** Talia Venn is not in the demonstration room and that is correct and is not a hole: she is in room four on the Friday as a witness.

## Canon now fixed in Chapter prose (Chapters 91–100)

- **DAI PRITCHARD, NINETEEN YEARS, AND HIS DECISION.** Sunday night, and the Monday morning at ten past eight, and he gets it in the right order before Marek Senn can say anything helpful. **He is answering the telephone. That is the whole of it. Not a grievance and not a resignation and not one bad word about anybody, and he said the words not a grievance and not a resignation himself, which means he thought about them and decided to arrive before he was accused of both.** The second half of the decision, which he delivers in about eleven seconds: **he wants the card.** Nineteen years, no card, never asked for one, and the reason he stopped answering on the Wednesday of week twenty-one is a line in a canteen with his name on it and a box somebody else typed. **And the true sentence Marek Senn gave him in week twenty-four came back as a document: a company is not a wall, and the way out of a wall is to be issued a card by it.** He asked for one thing and it was not a favour: to say on the telephone that he is not refusing the line. He said it, it went in the book with the time on it and no name on it and no reference on it and no company on it, and the load book says: **that is the second time anybody has said the right thing first.**
- **THE FIFTH CLAUSE, AND WHAT GRADE TWO COSTS.** Nia Bramhall, in a depot office, has tried twice to book him on a release course and cannot, and the reason is a four-field course-exception record in the company's own system in his own handwriting whose fourth field is reason and says he has not got a reason he is willing to give. **And she tells him the price before she tells him the rule: I am grade two, grade two does not go out on a release callout, grade two does not get the release rate, and if I am grade two for the whole of next term then the only thing that has happened to me this term is that I have been given a lanyard.** Two and a half times the shop rate, four days a week, about nine hundred pounds over a term. **He has known that figure since the Monday of week nineteen and has never once written it in the book, and writes it in Chapter 91 in a sentence about somebody else's shoulder, and four more times in the last ten days of the term.** And her last sentence: **a man who will not say why is a man who has not got a reason, and a man who has not got a reason is a man who is going to be in a room on his own one day with a card in his pocket and a thing he cannot do, and there is nobody in this building whose job it is to notice that, and I am a training clerk with a diary.**
- **DENNY MOLLOY, THIRTY-NINE, AND FOUR SENTENCES IN PENCIL.** He comes into the shop at about eleven on the Monday morning of week twenty-five, standing, refusing to sit down because if he sat down it would be a meeting, with a company's sheet in a plastic wallet asking him to confirm the accuracy of a note they hold about him. **The company is not being brave; it is frightened, correctly, because about two hundred people hold a copy of a line about eleven of its own workers and it cannot leave an unverified note in a file.** The cost: **to correct a note you must confirm it, and confirming it puts his name against a shoulder for good, and he is behind on his rent, and the note is the only thing that will give him his shift back, so he cannot refuse it and he cannot accept it.** He says: *then it's a good job for me.* That is the whole of what nineteen years of not being asked has produced, in one sentence, on a Monday morning. **Four true sentences, the fourth of which is that there is nobody whose job it is.** He asks Marek Senn to write it and is refused, and the reason is the true one — a document is only ever a receipt, and a receipt is what you show a company that has stopped answering its telephone, and the second his shoulder is written down by Marek Senn there is a document about his shoulder with his hand on it. **Then Denny Molloy writes it himself, in pencil, on a carpenter's pencil, in a slow block hand, in about a minute and a half, and does not sign it and does not date it**, and the reason for the date is a mate at the same firm who dates everything and got done out of about six weeks over one, and the reason for the signature is that his wife has a drawer. He hands it over and says *keep it or bin it* and says at the door: **you keep it in a drawer, and in about four years you'll be the fourth thing in a room, and I'll be dead, and my shoulder'll be true.** **The load book records that this is the eighth and that he is not counting it, and gives the reason: the other seven made a thing to keep a thing out of a company and this man did not make a thing.**
- **THE DEMONSTRATION, BUILT AND FOUND NOT TO EXIST (Chapter 92).** A rig on a hard standing off Fell Road, borrowed in four seconds off a man he has queued behind nine times. **Three versions. A person calling the four steps and the word. A card on a post with two cable ties, taken down inside four minutes. A recording of his own voice into a clamped speaker, which works first time and is worse, because a recorded voice is a company and it belongs to whoever has the telephone.** The finding: **there is a person who hands over the release, a card that hands over the release, and a recording that hands over the release, and all three have a person in the middle of them, and the only difference between mine and a company's is that mine is cheaper and I have not put a price on it.** He builds it anyway, and on the back of his own card he writes *there is nothing here that a person has to hand over*, and then tears it in half in front of the foreman, because he had understood while writing it that he was not going to be able to stand next to it either.
- **AUREL KOSTA, FIFTY-EIGHT, A FOREMAN, A PENCIL, A SHOULDER HE WILL NOT NAME.** Sent to look at a thing by a man in a lanyard and tells Marek Senn what he has agreed to and nothing else. **Four things.** One: it works, it is better than the three men he has had this year, and if you had turned up with a card and a clipboard and told him it was a company standard he would have believed you and been wrong and never known. Two: there is nobody in it, and in about four years a card is going to say the maintenance is the same whether anybody is standing there or not, and it will be true, and about nine men on his crew will believe it, and he will be sixty-one and still standing on a yard at eleven at night. Three: **you have spent a fortnight building a hole in a thing the shape of somebody with a lanyard on, and in about four years they will learn that the fourth beat is somebody in a lanyard, and the day they learn it you will not be in the room, and the hole will still be there, and the next man through it will not know what shape it is.** Then *then why are you building it*, and the two answers. **And the nine words, which he has said about four times since week twenty-one to three different people and nobody has ever asked him where he got it: they have taken the voice out and left the shape of the hand, and the hand is still doing what it was told.** He looks at his own left shoulder while he says it. Marek Senn asks which shoulder and is refused, and the refusal is the chapter: *if you start collecting people's shoulders you will end up in a room with nine of them and you will not be able to do your job.* **The shoulder is not written down anywhere and Marek Senn writes in the load book that he is not going to, in his own hand, so that in about four years he cannot pretend to have forgotten he was asked and refused.**
- **TWENTY-SEVEN YEARS AND THE TENTH TIME, AND OZZIE WRAY'S NINE WORDS ON A SPEAKER.** The foreman has never been able to write down the tenth time and gives the reason: **a man who writes down the tenth time has to be able to say what he did instead, and he has never known.** Then a telephone on the speaker at about ten past one, because the foreman was eleven feet away on a ladder and Marek Senn wanted both of them to hear it and could not think of a way of doing that which was not dishonest. **Ozzie Wray, thirty-four, on a kitchen line in Nine Bridges: the word is for the pallet that is not ready.** And the finding: **he is doing the fourth beat. It is the same thing with the slot not cut in it, because nobody has ever cut it in, and the only difference is that nobody cut it, and you cannot put it in a room, because the moment you put it in a room you cut the slot, and you cut the slot by making a person watch.** The foreman says twenty-seven years, and says he has not written down the tenth time, and says **and I am going to think about it on Monday**, and puts his hand up when he says it, which he had not done before.
- **DAWN TRELOAR, FORTY-FOUR, WITH A CLIPBOARD, AND THE MECHANISM OF A PUBLIC ROOM.** She fills in *technician attended site, rig in use, no fault identified* and does not fill in the box marked *work observed*, because **in nine years in about nine hundred yards there has never once been a technician doing anything a person in a room could tell you was safe or not safe.** And then the sentence that the whole of Movement V is built on, given to him for nothing, standing about nine feet from a rig: **in about two weeks there is a session in a hall with four hundred people in it, and I am on the list to go, and I have been on about nine of those lists in three years, and there is no woman at that company who books them who has ever once asked me whether I want to be on one. And when I asked her in the spring why we do it that way, she said it was easier.** **Easier than cancelling them.**
- **ASHA REED'S SENTENCE, FIFTH TIME, AND THE SENTENCE UNDER IT.** *The case is Senn's and the technique is nobody's yet.* Five times, unchanged, said standing up, and then: in four weeks five separate people have asked her whether they should be in a room, she has said no five times, and the sixth person to be asked in four weeks is her, and she is nineteen, and she is not a body in a room for anybody. **And the poster: a company name top right, district service, module three, open session, the Tuesday of the last week of the term, a hall on a trading estate, four hundred places, no charge, bring a form of identity, and at the bottom on a line by itself in the same size as everything else, *maintenance sequence without a supervisory cue — the technician on the kit*. No name anywhere.** And the sentence the volume is built on: **there is no name in it, and it is the only thing in nine months I have seen written down that has a hole in it the shape of a person and no person in the hole. And the day a company makes a room public about a technique that nobody owns, then the room does not belong to the case and it does not belong to the technique. The room belongs to whoever printed the poster.** And the sentence about his name, which she says as a criticism and which is correct: **as long as it is Senn's, nobody has to look at it, because there is a man, and he is twenty-two, and he can be the fourth thing in it.** **He has never told her he heard it and he has not worked out what to do with it.** Rhoda Ilesanmi from the floor: *they have told me I am on a list for the last Friday of the term and there is no list.*
- **THE EXCHANGE, THE FORTY-SECOND LINE, AND WHO DO I RING.** Five items, four minutes each, about nine people, the tin. **Marek Senn's own card read out in full — seventy words, his name in the first column, B. Nunn in the fourth, and the date field left empty, and she says in front of a room that she is not going to ask him for one and that she said that in this room a fortnight ago and has thought about it every day since and is not changing her mind in front of a room.** The book goes from forty-one lines to forty-two and does not move again this term. **And then a man of about fifty-one asks the only question anybody has asked in that room in nine years: who do I ring.** Six years of a card, nine courses, and six years of thinking about what he would do if the word does not come, and nobody to ask. **Marek Senn has the answer and does not give it, because the answer is about four hundred and six people and the answer would come out of a lanyard in a room with a market outside the shutters, and about two hundred people who have put cards in that tin for nine years would find out in a fortnight that the man with a card is the source.** The card goes in the tin and the line does not enter this term. **And then a card that was not in the tin at the start of the afternoon, in an envelope, from a market stall on the Saturday, and a man of thirty-nine did not want his name read out: nineteen years on the same rig, nobody has ever asked me about my shoulder, I found out I had one from a piece of paper I did not write, I am not asking anybody for anything.** She writes his name in the first column and hers in the fourth and **stops with the pen on the page and does not write a date and does not ask and does not look up for two seconds, and then says that is the last line this book gets this term.** And the two refusals that close the meeting: **she will not put the man of fifty-one and the man of thirty-nine in a room together, in front of nine people, in the fourth minute, because the day this room starts doing that it is a scheme, and a scheme is a thing that can be asked to produce a list** — and her own item, declined for the second time in a term, with the reason: **a thing I write about this book and then put in this book is a line that gets read out at the next one, and in about four years somebody is going to want to know what this room thought about itself and I am not going to be a document about it.**
- **THE FOURTH DECISION, AND THE POSTER, AND WHY A COMPANY'S SLOT IS CHEAPER THAN A NAME (Chapter 94).** A young man in a depot corridor stops him with *are you the technician on the kit*. Three wrong things and one right one. A telephone to a training diary: a change to a programme item goes on a form with about four lines in it for a reason, and **nobody has ever put a reason in that box that was a person saying I did not agree to be in this.** A form, filled in on the Monday night — *I did not agree to this and I have not been asked, and I am not able to say who put me in it, and I do not think anybody knows, and if the item is removed there will be no session and if the session is removed four hundred people will not be told anything, and I do not know which of those is worse and I have not got anybody to ask* — returned on the Wednesday morning with **no action — item is a company item.** A conversation with a district service manager of fifty-two in a parts cage, who gives the mechanism in four sentences: **a name on a programme is a person who can be asked whether they agreed to it; a job on a programme is not a person and cannot be asked anything; and the difference is that a programme with your name on it is a programme you can be removed from, and a programme with a job on it is a programme that happens. And the two sentences are about nine inches apart on a printed page and about eleven years apart in the world, and the poster has put them next to each other because a person in a building wrote *without a supervisory cue* and thought it was a selling point.** He also says he read the programme and read the time slot, knows what four minutes is, has done nothing about either, and would like to say he checked and will deny it. **And then the fourth decision, made on a stool in about ninety seconds: he lets his name stay off the programme, because if the item comes off then a hole gets filled in eleven seconds by a woman with a lanyard and eleven years of a job.** And the discovery, which is the movement's: **he has stopped wanting a room in which to say a true sentence and has started wanting an audience to be given one, and a room is a thing he is in and an audience is a thing he is doing to.** And in the load book: **a prohibition that is not there when he wants it and is there when he is frightened is not a boundary, it is a preference, and I have been living on it for a term.**
- **OREN VEY OWED A REPLY, AND HE GOT IT, AND IT COST BOTH OF THEM.** A telephone on the Thursday afternoon of week twenty-eight. The report is a market in the back of the Saltmarket on the Wednesday of week twenty-four, about eleven people, a man of fifty-two in a district jacket, and eleven people going two seconds quieter and nobody saying a word, and Marek Senn in the room not able to say one word of the answer. **The answer he gives: there is no group, and there is no such thing as a name for it, and the reason there is no name for it is that it is not a thing anybody did — a woman of twenty-seven with nine hundred words in a drawer in a kitchen in Saltmarket, a man of thirty-four on a kitchen line who will never be asked where he got it, and a woman of sixty-eight with five lines on an A4 in a front room. Those three have never met and if you put them in a room tonight one would be frightened and one would be proud of it and one would not understand the question. And then the sentence: the belief is a category, and somebody wants a category because a category can be written on.** Oren Vey says he was wrong for about two years, and that the thing he was wrong about is that he thought the belief would go away when the people harmed by it were told the truth. **And then, unasked, in about nine seconds: in about three weeks a second person is going to say it out loud in a different room to a different audience because he has been asked to, and he is going to be paid, and it is not going to be him, and he knows who it is and he will not tell him. And: I am going to be able to use what you have just said and you are never going to know whether I used it or not, and I have decided that is the correct way for this to go and I am not going to pretend it is decent.** Marek Senn does not ask the name twice, and the load book says that restraint is correct and it cost him the only thing he has ever wanted in this case. **And then the number, given for nothing: there are four hundred and six names on that list, and about nine hundred of those four hundred and six have never been asked a question about a practice by a human being and will not be asked one tomorrow either, and a man who is going to be ambushed is not a safer man.** And in the entry: the woman who counts them is on both sides of the number and has been asked three times this week by three people who are not her to cancel a session and has not, and he has never once asked her a question and has written that down so that he cannot later say he did not know.
- **RAFI PELL'S FIFTH LINE, AND THE TRANSIT SEQUENCE CARD, AND THE TWO NAMES (Chapter 95).** A booth about four metres by three in a transit hall in Nine Bridges, a heater, a rack of timetables nobody has reorganised, and a man of about sixty who keeps **a book of grey transit sequence cards for people who lose them** — *two of them, were you after; two is for a person who is thinking about it, one is for a person who has got a thing to do and not anybody to say it to* — who says the fifth line has been blank for longer than he has been in the building, that the reason is not a mistake and he does not know it, that about a hundred and forty go out a year and nine come back filled in and he has never seen one of the nine, and **do not sign it, everybody signs, and then it is a document, and a document has a date, and the date is a day a company could use.** Rafi Pell counts to two out loud in a glass room and the man looks at the ceiling. **The fifth line, in four minutes, in his own hand: I do the step without the count. If the count does not come out loud, I do not use the step. And it is not a fifth step — the four steps are the steps and the fifth line is him finding out whether he needs the word from outside at all, and the answer is no, and he has known that for two years and has never found out whether it is true, because you cannot find out without trying it on purpose. And if he gets it wrong, he falls over a rail with no card in his hand saying a man confirmed him, and he gets up.**
- **THE WAGON SHARE, AND NOBODY TOOK A VOTE ABOUT HIM.** Nine people, a lock-up off Weaver Street, a bench off a skip, a third of a van, about nine hundred a year between them, eleven days in, twice on the bench. **It is not the Exchange and it is not the Choir and he said both in that order and has thought about the order for about four hours and he is right about it.** **Nobody took a vote about him: if they had taken a vote it would be a thing that had been voted on, and a thing that has been voted on can be voted on again, and the next vote would be about whether he does it on a Saturday or a Sunday. The bench was voted on, the van was voted on, the rent was voted on, and the only thing in the room anybody would call a document is nine names and what everybody pays.** Nia Bramhall said write it down and bring it in, and he said no, and she said whose system, and he said yours, and she has not asked him again.
- **THE TWO NAMES WHO CAN END IT, AND WHY MAREK SENN IS NOT ONE OF THEM.** **Gus Haldane, who shouted keep it up the rail at him when he was nineteen and is at the fish market every day at seven in the morning, so that ending it means standing in front of him and explaining, which is on purpose. And Nia Bramhall.** **And not Marek Senn, and the reason given in about nine words: if you are one of the two then in about four years somebody can ask what it is and I will have to write it down, and two names on the front of a card become a company's document about a practice.** The company is a piece of a reason he could survive. **The reason is that the boy has written the fifth line and he has not written anything, and in about four years one of the two men in it will be able to say where the fifth line came from, and it will be me, because I have been in a term of rooms.**
- **ELLIS MARSH, THIRTY-ONE, WITH A STUTTER, AND THE ONE SENTENCE HE PRACTISED FOR A FORTNIGHT.** It came out whole. **They put the word NONE in a box about a hand. And nobody has ever asked me about my hand.** He signed it on the Wednesday and rang the company back because Rae Tolley said she was not going to, and he needed the hours. **I signed it, and it is accurate, and that is the whole of what is true about it and I have never said it out loud before. And I have got to be able to say that was a trade and not a tragedy, and I am not going to be one of the ones who got proud of it, and if anybody comes and tells me it was brave I am going to say something I have not got ready. My old fella will. He'll do it at Christmas.** Marek Senn says two sentences, both about him, and gives him nothing else. **And then: he has got my card on a sheet, he has read it about nine times, it is in a file in his kitchen under a tin of tea, he has not put it anywhere, and he told me it exists and said he knows I am the fourth thing in it and is not going to put me in it, and he put the phone down.** The load book: the count of people he has met who have invented the same protection was six and there is a seventh he has never met, and **as of about ten to one on the Friday afternoon there is an eighth person holding a piece of paper about a slot, and he made it nine by handing it to nobody, and this is what it looks like when it works.**
- **THE HALL, AND FOUR HUNDRED AND SIX, AND THE REASON THE ROOM HAPPENED (Chapter 96).** Second floor of a building on a trading estate, about nine hundred chairs in rows of ten, a banner with a strapline he reads nine times and cannot finish. **At the door, a woman of about twenty-six with a printout and a box of cards in shrink wrap: cancelling it is nine thousand two hundred pound and running it is two thousand, the hall is booked either way, nine hundred lunch tokens are ordered, if you cancel the lunch you do not get the money back, and if you do not cancel it four hundred people arrive and there is nothing for them. And then the sentence: it is not that it cannot be cancelled, it is that everybody who could cancel it is one person who is not in a room with anybody, and cancelling it would be that person deciding four hundred people were wrong, and there is no meeting where that decision gets made, so the decision does not get made, and the room happens.** He is offered a card and does not take it; *that's not my desk; I'm not going to be the one who has your name on a list.* Four passes round the room counting exits, a man of about thirty-one who says **everybody counts them and nobody says, and if you say it in this room you are a safety officer and if you say it in a canteen you are a worrier**, and about twenty-five minutes later a piece of masking tape on the frame of the back door at eye height, which is not a regulation and means somebody counted and did not want to say so. **The programme has no four minutes on it at all** — there is a slot from three to half past and the four minutes are in a running order on a clipboard — **and the small print says attendance does not constitute a certification of competence, and the sequence shown is illustrative, and nothing shown constitutes an endorsement of any practice not issued by the company, and it has been in every one of these since the spring because somebody in legal read a complaint about a Saturday course, and it means that on Tuesday he can do absolutely anything at all and every person in that room has been told in writing that it means nothing.** Four hundred and six arrive over about an hour and ten minutes and he asks about eleven of them what they are here for and **not one of them is there for the case**: a man of twenty-seven because it is on the invoice, two women of forty because it is on their course, a man of fifty-five for the lunch, a boy of nineteen because the card rate is different on a Tuesday, a woman of thirty-four with a hip who came on her own time with four lines on a piece of paper that is not a form. **And thirty metres away in row nine, on the aisle, on the left, with a pencil and a company form on his knee, a man of thirty-nine with a shoulder, and neither of them moves.** **And the chapter's finding, which is Asha Reed's sentence used as a test rather than a maxim: who is the last person in this room and are they findable — and the answer is a job, and a job cannot be asked whether it agreed and cannot be found afterwards, because there are about four hundred and six people in this city who are a technician on the kit and only one of them is on a list at a door, and the list has a tick column and a card column and neither of those columns is a person.**
- **THE PERMANENT SENSORY SENSITIVITY, IN THE ROOM, AND THE FAILURE (Chapter 97).** **The word is *confirmed*.** It starts at about four minutes past ten in the morning and by eleven o'clock it is going nine, ten, eleven times a minute, counted twice on the back of a docket: thirty-one rows of ten pairs, about a third of the pairs at the release at any moment, a certified holder says a sentence of about eleven parts, and the sentence contains the word once, **and it is the only part of it that arrives in his own head as a word.** The mechanism, stated and not staged: **it came in about week twenty, in a room, through equipment that was carrying something for somebody else, and it is not pain and it is not fear and there is nothing to look at, and in a room where a card holder is confirming somebody he cannot keep a sentence of his own; every time the word comes it takes the end of whatever he is in the middle of and puts a different end on it, and leaving the room is the only thing that has worked, and leaving the room is not available at three o'clock on a Tuesday because his name is not on the programme.** He has told nobody, has checked the fourth condition twice hoping it would stop him, and it does not, because a hard standing off Fell Road is not Institute premises. **He takes the microphone. He says in the first ten seconds that he is not going to do what the programme says. He says the reason in about thirty seconds, to four hundred and six people, for the first time in his life.** A man in a district fleece says *nerves*. A woman in the second row says *have you tried the demonstration before, love*, and about nine people laugh kindly. Somebody says *sit down, mate*, kindly. **There is no shape in that room for a man who cannot do a thing: there is a shape for a man who is frightened and a shape for a man who did not prepare and a shape for a man who did the work wrong, and all three are the same shape, and there is no shape for a man who has brought a working thing to a room and cannot put his own sentence down inside it.** He says it a second time and says it worse, because he is embarrassed, **and the second one is the one that is in the record.** He is not disbelieved, which is different and worse. **He is the reason it failed, and it is not a rail across an arm, and nobody was hurt, and the rig is on a trolley in a depot with a piece of masking tape on it.** And the reason he is not believed is the finding: **a man who cannot do a thing in a room is a shape the room already has four of, and a man who cannot keep a sentence in a room while nine people are confirming releases is not a shape anybody in this city has ever had, and the difference between those two is the whole of the demonstration and neither of them is a lie.**
- **DAVAN RUSK, FORTY-SEVEN, TWENTY-TWO YEARS, IN THE THIRD ROW, ON HIS OWN TIME ON A TUESDAY, NINE SECONDS AFTER THE MICROPHONE WENT DOWN.** He gave his name, which he has not given in a room in about nine years, and nobody in the hall knew who he was. **Four things.** One: he is right and I do not know what he is talking about and I am not going to pretend I do not. Two: what was said this afternoon is not a thing that has happened before in his experience in a room, and he has been in about four thousand rooms, and that does not make it true, it makes it new, and a man of twenty-two has just been called nervous in front of four hundred people and I do not want him to be the fourth thing in a room for the rest of his life unless somebody says the other half of it. Three: **the fourth beat has been taught as somebody else's word since before he came into that department, not as a shortcut, not as a company thing, as the safest piece of engineering he has ever been taught, and he still thinks that, and a person who is taught to finish on somebody else's word and does it four thousand times and then goes out on a Saturday at two in the morning on a wet bridge with nobody to say it to is not being unsafe — he has watched him do it and he has done it, and that is not a danger, that is a man doing a four-thousandth repetition of the only completion he was ever given.** Four: **he has never met anybody who thinks the fourth beat is a good idea, not one, in about nine hundred students, and he has asked twice in writing in about nineteen years and both times the answer came back with a reference on it and nothing else in it, and he has never put the nine hundred on a form, because the day he puts it on a form it is a survey and a survey is a document and a document is a thing a person can be pointed at for the rest of their life, and he has not got anybody to put that in front of.** And the load book's whole reading of it: **he has not got anybody to put in front of, and that is the only reason he could say it, and the man who has spent fourteen weeks not saying one true sentence in a room has been doing it for exactly the opposite reason.**
- **THE PROOF, AT ABOUT HALF PAST THREE, FROM FOUR PEOPLE (Chapter 98).** A first-floor office above a shop. Wyn Dunleavy from nine; the operations director up from the city in a coat that has been on an aeroplane; a woman of about twenty-six with a laptop who is the woman who put masking tape on a rig. **No agenda and no convener and she types exactly.** Four people come in over about nine minutes, one behind another, **and none of them came together and none of them knew, and Marek Senn is by the window.**
  - **NIA BRAMHALL, thirty-one, a training diary, no sitting down.** A stock docket, face down, one finger on the top third of page nine: *Meridian consolidation point, lot onward*, on a **venue** line, on a delivery, at the back end of the summer, typed by a stores clerk in a different system about two years ago, **with the field for the originating practice blank in the same typeface as three blanks nobody in that room has seen, and eleven lines on the page with a lot reference and not an address**, and a number that belongs to a place and not to a company. Her reason, checked twice on the stairs: **I book rooms, and four hundred and six people went into that hall because a diary had them in it, and on Tuesday a man stood up with a microphone and could not do his four minutes, and I have been a clerk for eleven years and nobody has ever told me what was in a room I booked.** And: **I have never once seen a lot on a venue line and I have never asked about one, and I want it on the record that the not asking is mine.**
  - **RUSLAN PETRIC, twenty-three, a card in a plastic sleeve and no document folder.** The card he put through the box on Weaver Street on the Friday of week twenty-two at about four minutes to five came back on the Monday, eight weeks, unedited, dated, with his name on it and a copy to him. **Four sentences of the company's own answer are the Continuity Office's four sentences word for word — a blank field is a record of an absence, a record of an absence is the easiest thing in the world for somebody to fill in wrongly, and in four years somebody will fill one in and will cite this office** — out of a letter that is in a drawer under a bench and that says in its own last line that it should not be produced in any proceeding without that office being asked first. **And he says: I have just said so for him, and I did not know that was what I was doing when I stood up.** His reason: **a document that nobody carries is a document that did not happen, and now it is on a table in a room, and in about four days somebody is going to put my name at the top of a company page with this sentence on it, and I would like it on the record, in the woman's words, that I knew that when I put it in the box and I did it anyway.**
  - **RAE TOLLEY, one of the eleven, a visitor book in a plastic wallet, and two hours on a cold hard standing next to a barrier on the Monday morning.** **THE FOUR WORDS ARE AWAITING RELEASE.** A facilities contractor's gate, a man who has been there six years, a column for the word that says why you are on site, and a **laminated sheet with about nine words on it and no name, no date and no company, which the man has never asked anybody what any of the nine mean.** Eleven lines out of about four hundred. **The whole of the shape of this case in four words: the load has arrived and the letting of it is somebody else's job.** And what she did about it: **she did not copy one line off a wall, she wrote it out in her own hand in a docket of her own, from about nine lines, out loud, at a gate, with the man saying it, because she is one of the eleven and she has read that bundle six times and has watched what it did to nine other people, and she has a page of her own that she has not given anybody and is never going to, and she is the last person in this city who should be walking away from a wall with a sheet in her pocket.** And the last sentence: **it is not going to be me and it is going to be somebody, and the somebody will be paid.**
  - **DENNY MOLLOY, thirty-nine, a carpenter's pencil, a site diary he will not open.** **Wednesday, second week of this month, twenty-second. A card in a plastic sleeve, the size of a playing card, grey, four steps printed, a boy's handwriting in the fifth line, a name and two more names on the front and no date.** He will not read out four sentences that are not his and says them in about nine words instead: a fitter said wait and went for a tea, an old lady was on a stair for eleven minutes, nobody was in danger, he is not saying it was wrong, eleven minutes is eleven minutes every Thursday and he is the only person who knows it, and he has not got a card and is not going to get one, and he has written it on the back of a docket in his kitchen and his son has read it and did not ask him about it. And: **I found out about a woman of sixty-eight telling a man to leave a date off a piece of paper by being in a shop, and I have decided that is a thing going round and I have not got a way of joining it up and I am not going to look.** The boy is Rafi Pell, in the fourth row of a hall, thirty metres away on the Tuesday, with a card in his wallet and a grey sequence card in a rucksack, and he was told to write it down because she asked him to and he writes neatly, and **he is on a Saturday at twelve pounds and he does not know that any of this has happened.**
  - **And then, from the window, in about nine sentences: none of that came from me. I did not find any of it, I was not asked for any of it, and I was not going to get any of it.** And the woman with the laptop offered to type it exactly, **and he told her not to, and could not tell her the reason, and the reason is that he would rather it was said and not kept, and he does not know if that is a principle or an excuse.**
  - **The finding, and the reason the volume ends without him:** Oren Vey says **nothing today, because a lot onward is not an address and a blank field is not a name and a number in a crew's book is not a company, and I have got four things that are consistent and I have got no thread that says all four are about the same bundle, and I have been in this trade for nineteen years and consistent is the word that men use before they are wrong.** And the recall decision is **the last Friday of the term**, and he says that out loud in front of five people and adds that he has not been asked and has not been told and is not going to ask. **And afterwards, the box number is in Marek Senn's head and nobody asks him for it and he does not say it: the whole edifice was never standing on his memory.**
- **THE REVIEW, SAT (Chapter 99).** Half past ten. **The chair is there, a typed strip in a plastic holder, against the wall and not at the table, because there was one card and the cards go out in alphabetical order.** Room four, second floor, Ninth Street block: two lights and a strip light, six chairs on one side and two on the other, a window that does not open, a flip chart nobody has turned over, and a fire notice four years old with a different stair on it. **A woman of about fifty-eight from a district office chairs it because there is nobody else, and says in her first four sentences that she does not know what the meeting is about, that she has read the form, that the form does not say, and that the middle box is ticked and nobody has ever rung her about it — and then writes *no terms of reference produced* on the top line and turns the sheet round and says that is the first line of the minute and it is the truest thing in the room and we are going to have it. She is the bravest thing in the volume and she does it before she has drunk anything.** Six at the table: the deputy registrar of the practical department, the instructor of twenty-two years, the district fitter, a woman of about forty-four from the district's own assessment side who never opens her folder, and **a woman of about twenty-four with a laptop and no template, who has never minuted anything and was given a form and told that if anybody used a word she did not have she was to write the word.** Two on the other side: **Talia Venn, a witness and not his**, and Rhoda Ilesanmi.
  - **The course exception is still open and no form was tabled.** The item on the poster was his and was not asked for and not agreed to. **THE MILESTONE, IN NINE WORDS:** he says three times and it is three times and she says **I am correcting a number, and I am not intervening**, and nobody thanks her and he does not thank her.
  - **The word *evidence*, used deliberately, about a box somebody else filled in on the Wednesday of week fourteen.** **He says the first half of the sentence she gave him on the Friday evening of week twenty-four and not the second half, because the second half was true on the evening she gave it and it was not true at half past ten on that morning**, and the reason is that the instructor is at the table. *I was not in the room, and I have not got anything.* Twice. **And: twice is not evasive, twice is a man who has been given a sentence by somebody and is keeping it.** And then the instructor is asked, and says **I do not remember. I do not think so. There are nine practical rooms and nine sessions a week and I do not remember that one.** **And Marek Senn had three words in his mouth and said them to himself, in his own head, in his own voice, in a room, and the woman with the laptop did not hear it and the deputy registrar did not hear it and the witness four feet away with a pad did not hear it. That is the entire record of it, and it is in a book in a shop, and that is two places, and neither of them is a room.**
  - **RHODA ILESANMI READS TWO HUNDRED AND FORTY WORDS OUT LOUD AT A QUARTER PAST ELEVEN, SITTING DOWN, AT THE SPEED OF SOMEBODY READING AN ADDRESS, AND THE WOMAN OF TWENTY-FOUR WRITES ALL OF IT AND GETS NINE WORDS WRONG, AND ONE OF THE NINE IS A VERB THAT MAKES THE SENTENCE SAY HE PUT HIS HAND ON HER ARM WHEN THE TWO HUNDRED AND FORTY WORDS SAY HE DID NOT.** Nobody corrects it. She watches the screen upside down because she is on the wrong side of the table, and says nothing, and so does he, **and that is not politeness and it is not the instruction.** She says: **that is the record, and I have known since a Friday in week twenty-four, in a library, for about an hour, that somebody was going to get about nine words of it wrong and that one of the nine was going to be wrong in a way that matters, and that is the trade, and I am not going to be sad about it this morning because I decided that in a library, and I am not going to be pleased about it either, because I have noticed that being pleased about it would mean I wanted the other thing.**
  - **NO SPONSOR, AND THE FILING, AND THE SIX WEEKS.** **Nobody invents a sponsor and nobody says who called it.** At about ten to twelve the woman of fifty-eight asks, and says she rang the practical department at nine that morning because she has never had a form come in with a middle box ticked and no name, **and they do not know what the form is, and the woman she spoke to had never seen it before this week, and it is not a form they send out. So it is not them.** She files it with the middle box still ticked and writes on the line under it: *no determination possible, no terms of reference, no convener, the form does not state a purpose, and the practical department does not recognise the form*, and says it will look exactly like a meeting that happened, and then says **it did happen, and that will be the one true thing in it.** **Six weeks separate a booking clerk who decided not to think about it any more because she had nine bookings before lunch, and a woman with a folder who has done the same thing for eleven months and it has held, and there is nobody in between and the only thing in the chain is a woman typing a room number because her job has a form and a room in it.** And about nine words from a fitter about a rota that ends today.
  - **And then a bench on a landing for about nineteen minutes, and both of them are exhausted, and neither of them says one word about any of it.** What they talk about is a boiler, a woman of sixty-eight's five lines, whether the woman of the twenty-fourth minute typed *instructor* wrong on purpose, a girl of nineteen in a library, a man of thirty-one with a stutter, and a man of fifty-eight with a pencil. **Milestone 4, and it is the last of it: the chapter says it was not nothing and it was not the same as what a person in a book is given, and the volume does not get any further than that and is not going to.**
- **THE RECALL, AND THE COMPANY THAT SURVIVES, AND THE BOY'S CARD, AND THE JOB (Chapter 100).** **Two sides of A4 with a reference on it at about one o'clock on the Friday afternoon. Training bundle 4-19 modules one to three and the demonstration rig stock, effective on receipt, no credit.** Not the bundle, not the manual (**which is still 3-08**), not the eleven, not the licensor. Four things: a lot onward on a venue line on a stock docket, a blank field in the same typeface as three other blanks, four words in a visitor column at a gate, a page number in a crew's book. **And the basis paragraph, which is the whole volume in a company's voice: the company identified the matter in the course of its own quality review, traced it to delivery documentation associated with delivery to a customer site, was not able at that time to identify the originating practice, did not consider it appropriate to assert an origin it could not evidence — and then, *this recall is made on a narrow set of facts. The company notes that other documentation may exist to which these facts do not reach.*** Four names in a table on page two with the date each thing was found, **and his name is not one of them, and he is not thanked and not mentioned, and he reads the whole thing twice to be sure and then sits on a bench in a depot canteen for about nine minutes with it in his hand.** **Nothing is broken up: the company continues to operate, supply, certify and train, the intake field is completed or refused at intake on the operations director's authority, and no document refers to a body that has done anything wrong, and a company that assembles a bundle from a supplier's assembly record is doing the correct thing about a document it has never been shown the first page of, and there is no such thing as a person in this city whose job it is to go and look.** **Oren Vey is not defeated and the door is still open.**
  - **THE FOUR HUNDRED AND SIX, AND THE PRICE OF THE ROOM.** A letter goes out on Monday and the third paragraph says **we would like to recognise that the open session held earlier this week was helpful in bringing this matter to our attention**, and it was not, and the number of people in that room who were there because of a room in week fourteen is zero. **Nia Bramhall has not sent it and is not going to, and a woman in Crown Terrace with a template will send it without reading the four paragraphs.** And a woman of about thirty-four telephones the booking office to ask whether the four lines she wrote down on the Tuesday are still right, because she has to go back on Thursday, and is told that they are still right, and that nobody is going to come and tell her about them, and that she is not on a list, and that this is not a comfort and is all there is. **And the load book: about four hundred people are going to be told, by somebody kind, that a Tuesday afternoon in a hall was the thing that found it; it is a lie; it is a kind lie; it costs nothing; and in about four years it is going to be the only thing in this whole case that anybody is actually pleased about, and I have not got a way of stopping it and I have not invented a person to stop it, and I have written it down instead, which is the least useful thing available.**
  - **ENID SARKIS, SIXTY-EIGHT, TELEPHONING A REPAIR SHOP AT ABOUT A QUARTER TO THREE FOR THE FIRST TIME IN TWO YEARS.** She wrote on the Sunday, posted on the Monday, telephoned this morning, and a woman of about thirty-eight read her back the exact sentence she has typed: *the note we hold about you has been destroyed and a copy of the destruction is recorded against this reference and there is nothing further held.* Is that all. That is all. **And she forbade the word thank you before he could say it, and gave the reason, and gave the price.** The reason: **I have been sixty-eight for a while and I know the difference between a person who is being kind at me and a person who has told me something, and I would have known it inside nine seconds.** The price: **the line manager has asked why, and she told him a woman of sixty-eight wrote to us and a company is not obliged to, and he said he would have to write that down somewhere, and she said write it wherever you like — and in about four years the next woman of thirty-eight will be reading it out of a procedure, and somebody will have written *on request in writing* on it, and somebody will have to decide what that means, and the woman who wrote the note will be dead.** And: **the eleven. Nine asked in writing and got it on Thursday. One did not ask, and she is not going to, and her note is still there and she knows it is still there. And one of them rang this shop this morning to ask a man who answers a telephone whether she should ask, and he said yes.** And the last sentence: **you gave us the thing that was right, and it made both of us go and do a thing on our own, and you were not there for either of it, and that is the only kind of help I have ever been able to use.** **He was not needed, and it is the first time in a term that he was not needed for something, and it cost somebody else.**
  - **THE FIFTH ROOM, AND THE ANSWER, WHICH IS NOBODY.** Four rooms in this city that a person can be put in for four minutes on a weekday, and the fifth is first-floor off Fell Road with a company name on a plate and no lift, open on the Wednesday of week sixteen and shut since about the Thursday, and a man of twenty-two went into it and lost a fortnight that nobody has ever paid him. **His name is on a page. The page is on a form. And the question of who takes the name out is answered with nothing at all, and it is not an oversight: there is no post in this city whose job it is to take a name out of a file about a person, and if there were one then the person in the post would be the last person in the room, and the last person in the room is the person who cannot be found. And a list of four hundred and six names went out of a building this afternoon, none of them was on that page, none of them will ever know it exists, and the four hundred and six are going to be told by somebody kind that a session on a Tuesday was the price of the room they sat in, and the room stays shut.**
  - **THE CARD ON THE SHELF, AND ONE NAME OFF THE ROW.** Rafi Pell and Gus Haldane in the shop at about ten past four. **The card is grey, twice the thickness of a playing card, four numbered steps in a typeface this city stopped using a long time ago, the fifth line in the boy's hand, a name and two more names and a rule and a date in the spring, and a fourth space with a line under it and the word *taker* above the line. Three names. Now two.** **Gus Haldane took himself off on Wednesday morning at seven in the fish market with a knife, and said take me off, and was told you cannot take yourself off a piece of paper, and said I can take myself off the row, which is a different thing and he had thought of it and Marek Senn had not. The reason: a clearance in about four years the way there is every four years, and a daughter of thirty-one, and a granddaughter of four, and I do not have the words for that and I am not going to get them.** And he wrote his own removal in the fourth column, because that is where the person who changes a card puts their name, and he said a man in a market invented the fourth column, and was told a woman of sixty-one did, and said she did not, and was right. **And: nobody took a vote about me**, said four times that week, and he will say it until somebody asks him to stop, and if somebody asks him to stop he will say it once more and then do as he is told, **because that is also what not taking a vote looks like and he has thought about that for a day and a half.** Marek Senn's whole sentence: **put it on the shelf.** *That is not an answer.* **It is the whole of the answer I have got, and I have had four days to think of a better one and I have not, and I am not going to tell you what to do with it, because you did not write it for me and you have named two people who can end it and neither of them is me, and if I put a condition on where it lives then it is a thing with a condition on it and a thing with a condition on it is a card, and a card is a receipt, and in about four years somebody is going to ask where the fifth line came from and it is going to be the fourth time somebody has asked me that.** And the card goes on the shelf above the bench, at the left-hand end, in front of a box of washers, where you would not see it unless you were looking for it. **And a man in a van, at the door, says the thing nobody has said: you counted, that is not a skill, that is just being the sort that counts.**
  - **THE JOB, AND ONE SYLLABLE, IN A CHAIR.** A room off a depot at about ten past five and a form that is not a company form. **Monday to Thursday, district service, grade two, the same rota — and a second thing, which he is read aloud because if he does not read it he will find out in the spring. Friday release callout rota. Two days, grade three, the release rate. Condition: the holder is to be in date with a supervised release card, or a course exception is to be recorded against the holder and renewed at the end of the term.** The fifth clause, and there is no other route onto that rota. **About eleven people a week are going to be taught to wait for him. About nine of them are on the rota and four are not, and the four are not because there is no course, and a company does not hold a course on a Friday.** He says the argument out loud first, because the manager asked him to: **a certificate is a portable dependency, the fourth beat is a supervisor's confirmation, and if I take the Friday rota I become the word for about eleven people a week, and in about four years a man of twenty-one with a count of his own is going to be told that the correct procedure is to wait for the technician, and he will be right, and I will be the reason.** It is a good argument. **And I am not able to hold it against a wage, and I said yes in one syllable.** The manager will write on the form that he has not got a reason, because that is what the box says, and will not invent one and will not write a bad one, **and wants the box to be true for a district assessor in about four years who is going to be a man of about fifty and is going to be fair.** **It is the last decision of Volume 02 and it is not undone.**
  - **THE IMAGE, AND THE FIFTH REFUSAL.** A grey transit sequence card on a shelf with a boy's handwriting in the fifth line, three names against a review date and one of the three taken off the whole row and the taker's name in the fourth column. **The original in the fourth pocket of a tool roll on the bench: four beats and a fifth line blank, blank for about four years, and nobody is going to fill it in.** On the wall, a job list in a plastic sleeve with a fifth line and his initials at the bottom of it, and the initials mean *not done*, **and the conditions that put the list there lapsed on the Wednesday of week sixteen and nobody has come to take it down and he has not taken it down either, and there is no longer any document in this city that requires it to be there.** Four days a week at a depot on the far side of the city from his own bench, and two on a Friday, and the lamp is on over the bench, and the shutter is up, and it is raining. **And on Monday morning at nine he opens the book, which no department and no trust is entitled to read, at forty-two lines with his own as the forty-second and a man's shoulder in the tin for twelve more days, and there is nobody in the book and there is nobody to hand it to, and the woman who counts to three and comes down on four is four miles away and does not know that he has opened it, and he is not going to ring her, and that is the fifth refusal in a term that he has written down and the only one he is not going to explain. And a number on page nine of a printout in a tray that goes at the end of the year, read four times, never written down.**

## The volume's power state, and the one thing in it that is not on the page

**Stage 2 Counterpoint, with a permanent sensory sensitivity — and the relay, which is the other half of the outline's Stage 2, is not demonstrated anywhere in this volume.** Both facts are recorded and neither is a canon change.

- **On the page:** he can compare several independent lines and identify a hinge. The third element of Stage 2 — the second clause of Chapter 92's finding — **is on the page and is this: the last person in a room of four hundred and six is a job, and a job cannot be asked whether it agreed and cannot be found afterwards.** **He cannot repair a public system, and the demonstration in Chapter 97 fails and he is the reason, in the first person, in the load book, and it is not a rail across an arm.** **Disagreement creates paralysis once in this volume, in a back room in the Saltmarket, where eleven people go two seconds quieter and nobody says a word.** **Relay privacy is imperfect, and the volume's version of it is a woman of thirty-nine with a page of her own that she has read six times and has given to nobody, and a man of thirty-one with a card in a tin under a tin of tea, and four sentences in a boy's handwriting on a card in a crew's book that the boy does not know he has done anything with.** **The permanent sensory sensitivity is on the page: in a room where a card holder is confirming somebody he cannot keep a sentence of his own, the word is *confirmed*, there is no way to turn it off, he said so out loud in front of four hundred and six people and was not believed, and he has said he is not going to say it twice. Its stated origin — about week twenty, a room, equipment carrying something for somebody else — is ASSERTED IN ABOUT SIXTY WORDS AND IS NOT DRAMATISED, because no share is performed in Movement V and the batch's hard rules forbid one.** **FLAGGED: a Volume 03 writer should either stage the relay or accept the outline's word for it, and should not let a later batch quietly imply that it happened in an earlier one.**
- **He still cannot invent a practice.** **The detection has no name of his own, releases nobody, is not a technique and is not a practice. The one sequence anybody in this volume could put a name to was written by a boy of twenty-one in a booth in Nine Bridges, on a card behind glass, and it is his, and it has two named people who can end it, and Marek Senn is not one of them.**

## Three things flagged, not fixed, for the reviewer

1. **Chapter 100 is 9,967 words and the batch is 58,108.** A close that carries nine resolutions, a power state, a final image and a load-book entry is entitled to be long, and Volume 01's Chapter 50 at 11,488 is the precedent. The three least load-bearing blocks are listed in `workspace/volume-02/batch-0005/SUMMARY.md` and none was cut, because the guardrail statement and the resolution are the point of a close.
2. **The permanent sensory sensitivity is asserted and its origin is not dramatised, and the relay is not demonstrated at all.** See the block above. Both are on the page as flags.
3. **The count of the seven is seven and four further things are not the seven.** The batch added a fifth line written by a boy of twenty-one, a question asked by a man of fifty-one, four sentences belonging to a man of thirty-nine and four lines belonging to a woman of thirty-four, and the count was not incremented and the word *pattern* was not used for any of them. **A reviewer should check that no Volume 03 material treats the eleven as eleven, and that the fourth column on Rafi Pell's card is not mistaken for the Exchange's fourth column — it is the same convention copied by a twenty-one-year-old from a card in a tin, and the taker of his row is a man with a knife in a fish market and not Bea Nunn.**

## Swept and found clean, batch 0005

- Load-book run **94 → 103**, continuous, no gap and no duplicate, one entry per chapter. **Asterisk parity clean in all ten files. Quotation-mark parity clean in all ten files, and no speech is split across paragraphs.** **Zero `* * *` panel markers.** **Zero occurrences** of *Crown Clause*, *Crown Law*, *Iona*, *Sorn*, *Leo Marr*, *Nell*, *Quiet House*, *Solo Seal*, *the week of week*, *about about*, *steady*, *plain* as a standalone word, *breathed*, *March*, and **zero of any month name and zero years.** *August*, *September* and *November* were in the first draft in Chapters 96, 98 and 100 as documentary dates and all three were cut. **No duplicated paragraph across the batch at a ninety-character threshold.** **The four who go and get the proof are not named as such before Chapter 98: Ruslan Petric and Rae Tolley are named nowhere in Chapters 91–97, and Nia Bramhall is named in five of them because she has been in the manuscript since week twenty-five as the training clerk, and is not named as one of the four and does not go and get anything before Chapter 98.**
